# Pushbutton Ideas

Running log of pushbutton concepts for the AnonGee toolkit, one entry per idea, newest on top.

---

## 2026-09-27 00:42 UTC

**Idea name:** Multi Filter Parameter — ElementId Write Support, porting the exact `[id] Name` bracket-encode/decode trick `One Filter Parameter` already ships and already uses to batch-set Level, Material, Phase, Design Option and Family-Type-referencing parameters, into the one tool built specifically to replace it for real batch work and which hard-refuses every one of those same parameters today

**Potential:** `Advance.panel/FilterParameter.splitbutton` ships two pushbuttons with near-identical bundle tooltips, both promising the same five edit operations — `One Filter Parameter/bundle.yaml` and `Multi Filter Parameter/bundle.yaml` both read, verbatim, "change a parameter... (Set / Prefix / Suffix / Replace / Delete)" — and both built the same way: filter elements by category and parameter condition, then batch-edit a chosen writable parameter on every match inside one `Transaction`. Multi is the newer, more capable sibling — multiple AND/OR filter conditions and multiple edit rows applied in one pass, versus One's single condition and single edit — the tool a modeler reaches for on any real filtered set. But the two tools disagree on one entire `StorageType`, and only one of them tells the truth about it up front. `OneFilterParameter.pushbutton/script.py:68-79` defines `_bracket_int(s)`, a small parser for a leading `"[<int>]"` prefix ("Replaces `re.match` — the `re` module is unavailable in this CPython3 engine"), used because `_get_param_val_str` (`script.py:259-271`) deliberately formats every ElementId-storage parameter's display value as `"[{eid_val}] {name}"` — e.g. a Base Level parameter shows as `"[482913] Level 2"` — specifically so the numeric id survives a round trip through a plain-text WPF `ComboBox`. `_get_param_values` (`script.py:376-388`) feeds that bracketed string into every filter- and edit-value dropdown, and `_execute_edit`'s `Set` branch (`script.py:749-767`) closes the loop: for `StorageType.ElementId` it calls `_bracket_int(str(val1).strip())` to recover the id, wraps it back into a real `ElementId`, and calls `param.Set(new_id)` (`script.py:760-764`) — a genuinely working, shipped, one-click way to batch-reassign any element-reference parameter (Base/Top Level, Material, Phase Created/Demolished, Design Option, a shared parameter typed `Family Type`) across a filtered set, by picking a name off a dropdown instead of hand-editing one element at a time in Properties. `MultiFilterParameter.pushbutton/script.py` never adopted any of it: `_get_param_values` (`script.py:298-324`) only ever calls `param.AsValueString()` with String/Integer/Double fallbacks — no ElementId branch, no bracket, no id captured anywhere — and `_get_common_params` (`script.py:280-296`) filters candidate edit parameters purely on `p.IsReadOnly`, so a writable ElementId parameter is offered in the edit-row dropdown exactly like any other, with real, friendly values in its value list (Revit's own `AsValueString()` already resolves most ElementId BuiltInParameters to a name, just without the id Multi would need to write it back). A modeler picks "Base Level" as an edit parameter, sees "Level 3" as a selectable value, ticks it, hits **Execute Changes**, and `_apply_single_modification` (`script.py:382-420`) runs the request into a dead end it has always had: `if st == StorageType.ElementId: return False, "Set ElementId not supported"` (`script.py:415-416`) — surfacing only as one more line buried in the run's partial-`errors` list (`script.py:1022-1034`), after the transaction already committed whatever else the row touched. The same function's `Delete` branch, one line above, has no such gap — `elif st == StorageType.ElementId: param.Set(ElementId.InvalidElementId)` (`script.py:392`) already writes an ElementId — so Multi can already clear a Level/Material/Phase reference on a hundred elements in one pass, and cannot set one to anything, on the same parameter, in the same tool. This is exactly the everyday batch chore both tools were built for and only one delivers: re-basing every footing a level's insertion point shifted onto its correct new Base Level, swapping the Material on every column of a given type after a spec revision, or bulk-correcting a batch of elements someone modelled on the wrong Phase — today possible one row at a time in One (or one element at a time in native Properties), and silently refused the moment the same job needs more than one filter condition or more than one parameter changed together, which is the entire reason Multi exists. It is a narrower, cheaper version of the gap the already-logged Cross-Element Parameter Copier (2026-09-23) and Parameter Migrator name for cross-document/many-to-many moves — both explicitly scoped to the *value already sitting on another real element*, neither touches this same-document, filter-driven, type-by-name write path Multi and One already share — and distinct from the already-logged Level Rehost (2026-09-24), which recalculates an element's offsets to preserve absolute elevation on a level change and is deliberately a heavier, elevation-aware operation; this idea is the plain, no-offset-math ElementId setter Level Rehost's own author could build on top of, not a replacement for it. On the market, Ideate BIMLink and CTC BIM Project Suite can batch-write a reference parameter, but only by round-tripping every edit through an exported spreadsheet under a paid, seat-licensed add-in; no free tool does a live, in-canvas, multi-condition-filtered ElementId batch-write, and this repo's own two-button pair proves the one missing piece is porting eleven already-working lines from one file to its sibling, not inventing anything new.

**Description:**
1. Multi Filter Parameter's Categories → Multi Filter → Multi Editing flow is unchanged end to end; the fix lives entirely inside the value-formatting and write functions.
2. **Read side.** `_get_param_values` (`script.py:298-324`) gains the same ElementId branch `OneFilterParameter._get_param_val_str` already has: for `StorageType.ElementId`, resolve `param.AsElementId()` to its element (`doc.GetElement(eid)`), format `"[{id}] {name}"` (falling back to `param.AsValueString() or "Unknown"` for a null referenced element, exactly as One's own fallback does), and keep the eid alongside the string in a small `val_to_eid[param_name]` map — one companion to the numeric `val_to_num` map the same function already keeps for Double/Integer filtering (`script.py:319-321`).
3. **Filter side.** `_evaluate_single_condition` (`script.py:326-380`) already runs `equals`/`does not equal`/`contains`-style text comparisons against `AsValueString()` for anything that isn't numeric; once ElementId values arrive bracketed, those same text operators keep working unchanged (matching on the bracketed string, "contains" on the name portion included) — no new operator, no new branch, since the comparison is still string-vs-string.
4. **Edit side.** `_apply_single_modification` (`script.py:382-420`) gets one new branch in `Set`, ported from `OneFilterParameter._execute_edit` (`script.py:760-764`): parse `val1` with the same `_bracket_int` helper (copied in, or promoted to a shared module per the Implementation note below), build `ElementId(System.Int64(id_int))`, and call `param.Set(new_id)` — the exact working call already proven in the sibling tool, now reachable from a multi-condition filter and alongside every other edit row in the same batch.
5. **Preview/report.** The existing "Modified N elements" / partial-errors summary (`_on_execute_done`, `script.py:1022-1034`) needs no change — an ElementId `Set` that previously always landed in `errors` now simply succeeds and counts toward `modified`, the same accounting path every other storage type already uses.

**Implementation:**
- Promote `_bracket_int` (`OneFilterParameter.pushbutton/script.py:68-79`) out of the single pushbutton file into a small shared helper both buttons import (e.g. `FilterParameter.splitbutton/_shared.py`, or, if the two bundles must stay fully independent per the repo's existing per-pushbutton `script.py` convention, a verbatim copy — either way the parser itself does not change), rather than leaving two implementations of the same 12-line function to drift the way the already-logged Shared Parameter GUID Doctor (2026-09-18) and Rebar Joint Clash & Congestion Checker (2026-09-17) both flag for this repo's geometry/parameter helpers elsewhere.
- `MultiFilterParameter.pushbutton/script.py`: extend `_get_param_values` (`:298-324`) with the ElementId branch and its `val_to_eid` side map; extend `_apply_single_modification` (`:382-420`)'s `Set` branch with the ported `_bracket_int` → `ElementId` → `param.Set` sequence; no XAML change, since `ui.xaml`'s edit-row combo is already a plain editable `ComboBox` populated at runtime by `_set_param_items` (`script.py:1057-1067`) regardless of what strings it's fed.
- Update `Multi Filter Parameter/bundle.yaml`'s tooltip to stop implying full storage-type parity with One's identical wording once this ships (or, once it ships, the wording becomes true and needs no caveat at all).
- Regression-test against exactly the parameters `RC Automation`'s own placement pipeline already writes as ElementId (`element_params.py`'s `HOST_PARAMETERS`/level-linked fields) so a modeler filtering RC-Automation-placed elements and reassigning them gets the same result Multi already gives for every String/Integer/Double field today.

**Future update:**
- Once this ships, the already-logged Phase Assignment Auditor (2026-09-25) and Level Rehost (2026-09-24) both gain a simpler, already-tested primitive to write their own resolved Phase/Level values through, instead of each hand-rolling its own `ElementId` write path.
- Extend the same bracket convention to the Design Option axis, so a batch "move these elements into Option B" — currently only a manual right-click-per-element operation in native Revit — becomes one more `Set` row in the same Multi Editing tab.
- Add a `Copy from element` value source next to the typed/picked value on an ElementId edit row (bridging toward the already-logged Cross-Element Parameter Copier's single-source-many-targets model) so a modeler can point at one already-correct element instead of hunting its name in a long Level/Material list.

## 2026-09-26 15:41 UTC

**Idea name:** Grid-Move Re-Anchor — reads the `ID_LIC` grid-intersection identity every RC Automation placement already stamps onto its own elements, recomputes today's actual crossing with the toolkit's own read-only grid-crossing math, and offers a batch move back onto it — closing the silent drift `grids.sync`'s own "position is synced" behaviour opens on every element that was ever placed "at" a grid and then left behind when that grid moved again

**Potential:** `AnonGee.tab`'s grid-driven placement pipeline is fully built and, on the identity side, already wired: `anongee_toolkit.rc_automation.identity.location_code(mark, type_mark, grid_x, grid_y)` (`identity.py:66-78`) turns a footing or column's `GridX`/`GridY` placement reference into the office's own `"A-1"`-style location code, `host_values()`/`from_placement()` (`identity.py:126-144`) fold that into the five-field `ID`/`ID_LIC`/`ID_V`/`ITEM`/`LEVEL_V` identity block, and `structural/structure_run.py:179,249-257` calls `element_params.write(element, item.identity)` to burn it onto the placed element as a real, bound shared parameter (`element_params.py:41`, `HOST_PARAMETERS = ("ID", "ID_LIC", "ID_V", "ITEM", "LEVEL_V")`) — `rebar_run.py:140` does the same for the bars hosted on it. So today, right now, a footing sitting at grid crossing C-3 carries an `ID_LIC` value of literally `"C-3"` on itself, permanently, unless someone edits it by hand. What nothing in the repo does is read that value back. `commands/grids.py`'s own docstring states the exact behaviour that creates the gap: *"Position is synced... two grids on the same infinite line are the same grid whatever their lengths"* — and its `_apply()` (per the already-logged Grid Sync idea, 2026-09-19 05:41) calls `ElementTransformUtils.MoveElement` to slide a matched grid onto the sheet's new line whenever a bay spacing gets revised, which on a live structural job is not rare — it is one of the most repeated edits there is, per that same idea's own opening line. When grid C moves 300 mm because the architect shifted a core wall, every footing and column already placed against `"C-3"`, `"C-4"`, `"C-5"`... stays exactly where it physically was built, because `MoveElement` only ever touches the `Grid` element itself — nothing re-reads the elements that claim to sit on it. A repo-wide grep for `ID_LIC` and for any caller of `element.LookupParameter` against that name (outside the write path in `structure_run.py`/`rebar_run.py`/`element_params.py`) returns nothing — the parameter is written once, at placement, and read by nobody, ever again, until it turns up years later in a quantities schedule labelling a column "C-3" that no longer physically shares a coordinate with grids C and 3. The already-logged Schedule Drift Reconciler (2026-09-23 00:42) comes closest but checks the opposite direction and a different mismatch: it matches a *second run's* spreadsheet rows against elements already in the model "on position and storey" to catch a wrong cover/size/rotation on a fresh placement, and never re-derives a position from a grid that has since moved. The already-logged Column Stack Auditor (2026-09-15 05:39) catches a column that has drifted from the column below it storey to storey via 2D proximity clustering, a different mechanism aimed at vertical continuity, not at an element's *stated* grid identity versus where that grid actually is today. Grid Sync's own future-update list (2026-09-19 05:41) only proposes extending the same picker to other `*.sync` commands and a diff mode for the grid rows themselves — nothing in it re-checks a single hosted element. The read-only math to do this safely already exists, proven and tested: `anongee_toolkit.structural.grids.grids(doc)` (`grids.py:25-33`), `segment_mm()` (`grids.py:40-53`) and `intersection_mm(doc_grids, name_x, name_y)` (`grids.py:56-69`) already resolve any two named grids to their crossing point in millimetres by hand-rolled line-crossing math — chosen, per that module's own docstring, specifically to avoid `Curve.Intersect`'s `clr.Reference` out-parameter headache under the CPython 3 engine — and `_placement.py:194-224`'s `_intersection(doc)` already wraps it into a closure read once per run for exactly this kind of repeated lookup. For a structural BIM team this is the invisible failure mode that erodes trust in the whole grid-driven placement convention the office has standardised on: a modeler trusts the `ID_LIC` on a schedule or a tag to mean "this is physically at that crossing," and after enough spacing revisions on a long-running job that stops being true for an unpredictable subset of elements, with no warning and no easy way to find which ones. On the market, Revit's native Copy/Monitor tracks *grids themselves* against a linked source for drift, never a hosted element's stated grid reference against a grid that moved inside the *same* model; nothing in Ideate BIMLink, CTC BIM Project Suite or Naviate Structure re-derives an element's intended position from a text identity field and re-offers a move — because none of them have an identity field like `ID_LIC` to read in the first place; it is this office's own convention, unused past the moment it is written.

**Description:**
1. Modeler runs **Grid-Move Re-Anchor** (no selection required — it scans the categories RC Automation places: Structural Columns, Structural Foundations, and any others the `element_params.HOST_PARAMETERS`/`REBAR_PARAMETERS` binding covers).
2. It collects every element with a non-empty `ID_LIC` parameter, splits it back into a grid-name pair by matching both halves of the value against `structural.grids.names(doc)` (trying the last `"-"` first, since a grid name may itself contain one), and skips — with a reported reason — any `ID_LIC` that does not split into two known grid names (already-flagged as manually edited, or from a coordinate-based placement whose `location_code()` fell back to a mark suffix per `identity.py:73-78`).
3. For everything that does resolve, it calls the existing `_intersection(doc)` closure (`_placement.py:194-224`, built on `structural.grids.intersection_mm`) to get today's actual crossing point for that named pair, and compares it — in millimetres, plan-only — against the element's own `Location.Point` (columns) or an equivalent representative point (a footing's placement point, read the same way `footings.create()` sets one, or its outline centroid for an outline pad).
4. Anything within the same tolerance `RoundElementDist` already exposes on its own tab is left alone and counted as matching; anything further off is listed — Mark, `ID_LIC`, level, current point, target point, Δmm and Δdirection — in the same tick/untick-before-anything-happens checklist shell every other pre-flight tool in this repo already uses, pre-ticked, with the exact "touching pair / already correct" rows pre-unticked the way `RoundElementDist` itself does for a zero-gap case.
5. **Apply** moves every ticked element from its current point to its target point in plan (columns via `ElementTransformUtils.MoveElement` on the location-point delta, footings via the existing `footings.rotate`-style helper generalised to a translate, both inside one `TransactionGroup` so the whole batch is a single undo step), preserving level, rotation and every other parameter untouched — this only ever corrects XY, never height or type.
6. Report: moved / already-matching / skipped-unparseable / blocked (an element whose target crossing itself could not be resolved, e.g. because one of the two named grids no longer exists) counts, exportable through the same `openpyxl` path `Export Schedule.pushbutton` already proves.

**Implementation:**
- New module `anongee_toolkit/structural/grid_anchor.py`: `split_lic(value, known_names)` (pure, testable — the "-" split-and-match-both-halves logic), `resolve_target(doc, grid_x, grid_y)` (thin wrapper over `_placement._intersection`/`structural.grids.intersection_mm`), and `drift_mm(current_point, target_point)`, kept Revit-free where possible the same way `structural.grids` itself is.
- New pushbutton `AnonGee.tab/Essential.panel/StrucBIM.stack/Grid Move ReAnchor.pushbutton` (sibling to `Grid Sync`, once that already-logged idea ships, or standalone otherwise), reusing the same modeless `ui.hostwnd`/checklist shell every other batch tool in the repo runs on.
- Element read/write reuses `structural.columns.placed(doc)`'s category-collector pattern (`columns.py:279-303`) for columns, generalised for Structural Foundations, and the plain `LookupParameter("ID_LIC")`/`get_Parameter` pattern `structural/columns.py:343-350`'s own `_parameter()`/`_set()` helpers already establish for reading and writing a named parameter defensively.
- Move primitive: `Autodesk.Revit.DB.ElementTransformUtils.MoveElement(doc, element.Id, XYZ(dx, dy, 0))`, the exact call `commands/grids.py`'s own `_apply()` already uses to slide a matched *grid* — used here on the hosted element instead, same API, same transaction-group convention.
- Same tolerance field and rigid-join-cluster awareness `RoundElementDist.pushbutton` already implements (a column joined to a wall should not be moved alone if the wall isn't also being re-anchored) — reused, not reimplemented, by importing its clustering helper rather than forking it.

**Future update:**
- Wire straight into a future `Grid Sync.pushbutton`'s Apply step as an optional "and re-anchor everything on a moved grid" follow-on pass in the same window, so a bay-spacing revision becomes one run instead of two.
- Extend `split_lic`/`resolve_target` to rebar hosted directly via `Host Category`/`Host Mark` (`REBAR_PARAMETERS`, `element_params.py:44-45`), so a re-anchored footing's own cage follows it rather than being left mid-air relative to its new pad edge.
- Feed unresolved/blocked rows straight into the already-logged Warning Auditor's batch-view pattern, so an `ID_LIC` that no longer names a real grid pair (because that grid was deleted or renamed) becomes a triaged item instead of a console line.
- A "preview only, never apply" mode that runs automatically right after a `Grid Sync` run finishes, purely as a heads-up count, so a modeler learns immediately how many elements a spacing revision just orphaned even before deciding to fix any of them.

## 2026-09-26 10:43 UTC

**Idea name:** CAD2BIM Round-Trip Fidelity Auditor — post-build model-vs-DXF delta report (SCOPE-2/P5, `geom/compare.py` reused on as-built geometry)

**Potential:** The cad2bim pushbutton's own roadmap names the gap in its own words: `AnonGee.tab/Core.panel/cad2bim.pushbutton/todo-list.md:1227` lists `SCOPE-2 | P5 Round-trip QA — the built model against the source DXF, delta reported | pending | Enough of the model to be worth diffing`, and line 1228 makes P6 Annotation wait on it. That dependency is now satisfied — WORK-1 through WORK-20 (`todo-list.md:1196-1215`) show columns, beams, slabs, stairs, grids, levels, walls and foundations all `done`/`testing` — yet nobody has picked P5 up. The tooling to close it is sitting half-built and mis-aimed. `lib/py3/anongee_toolkit/cad2bim/geom/compare.py:1-13` implements a generic, Revit-free, unit-tested `diff(revit_records, dxf_records, tol_ft)` (`compare.py:53`) and `format_console(comparison)` (`compare.py:120`) that midpoint-matches `CurveRecord`-shaped geometry (`model.py:12-19`, `.points`/`.layer_key`/`.length_ft`) between two sources and reports per-layer counts, "dxf_only" (dropped/merged), "revit_only" (import artifacts) and "clipped" (length-mismatched) members. But its only caller, `AnonGee.tab/Core.panel/cad2bim.pushbutton/script.py:695-698`, is explicitly labelled `# Diagnostic only: how much Revit's import dropped/clipped vs the raw DXF` and feeds it `revit_result.records` from `geometry_reader.read_link(doc, instance)` (`script.py:390`) — i.e. it diffs the *raw linked CAD import curves*, captured once at the start of the run, against the DXF text/geometry read, purely to decide whether Revit's own DWG import clipped a junction badly enough that the build should trust ezdxf instead. It never once looks at what the builders actually placed. Every builder hands back exactly the material needed to do that: `columns.py:86` initialises `result = {"created": [], "skipped": [], "errors": [], "notes": []}` and `columns.py:131` appends `instance.Id` for every placed `FamilyInstance`; beams, slabs, footings and walls follow the identical `"created": [ElementId, ...]` shape. Nothing in the repository ever re-reads `doc.GetElement(id).Location` / `get_BoundingBox(None)` for those IDs and re-runs them through `compare.diff()` against the DXF — the as-built check the roadmap asks for. `report.py:4-6` even documents the missing piece by name: the JSON export schema is "deliberately the intermediate format the future external CPython 3 + ezdxf validator will consume... the contract between the in-Revit reader and the out-of-Revit checker" — a contract written and never fulfilled. For a structural BIM team this is the exact failure mode that erodes trust in an automated CAD-to-BIM conversion: a column silently placed 40 mm off a drawn centreline by a snap-tolerance rounding, a beam whose builder-side split-at-columns logic (`beam_cleanup.split_beams_at_columns`, re-exported at `report.py:41-43`) leaves a segment short of the drawn length, or a slab edge that took a fallback profile (`slab_outlines.check_profile`, `slabs.py:297-330`) instead of the drawn ring — none of these are caught today because the only geometric check that exists runs before a single element is built. Ideate BIMLink and CTC BIM Project Suite QA tools check parameters and schedules, not "does the placed geometry match the source drawing"; Naviate has no CAD-import fidelity checker; a Dynamo graph doing this would have to reimplement the midpoint-matching and per-layer bucketing that `geom/compare.py` already has, tested, for free.

**Description:**
1. The modeler runs cad2bim as normal (or opens a model that was already converted from a DXF in an earlier session) and picks a new button, **Round-Trip Check**, from the same panel.
2. The tool asks for the linked/original DXF path (reusing the same picker `dxf_linker` already shows, `ui_window.py:35`) and the set of structural categories to audit (columns, beams, slabs, walls, foundations — whatever the naming/layer convention already classifies via `classify/layers.py`).
3. It collects the live elements in those categories (either the whole model or the current selection), and for each one extracts an as-built `CurveRecord`-compatible geometry proxy: a beam's `LocationCurve` becomes a two-point line record, a column's `BoundingBox` footprint becomes a closed rectangle record, a slab's boundary `CurveLoop` becomes a polyline record — all tagged with the same layer/category key the CAD-to-BIM naming convention already assigns.
4. It re-reads the source DXF with the existing `dxf_reader.read_dxf(path)` (`script.py:398`) and calls the existing `compare.diff(as_built_records, dxf_records, tol_ft)` unchanged, with `tol_ft` taken from the same `compare_tol_mm` tolerance already exposed on the Tolerances tab (`config.py:37`, `ui_window.py:779/839`).
5. The console prints the same style of summary `compare.format_console()` already produces (`compare.py:120-131`), but now labelled "as-built vs drawing" rather than "import vs drawing" — per-layer count/length deltas, members present in the DXF with nothing built for them (a beam the builder skipped and never reported loudly), and members built with a length or position that drifted past tolerance from what was drawn.
6. Flagged elements are collected into a plain list with their `ElementId`s and written to the same JSON export shape `export.build_export_payload` already produces (`report.py:44-47`), so the modeler can select-by-ID in Revit and open the drawing at the same spot to see what disagreed — closing the exact loop `report.py:4-6`'s docstring says was always the plan.

**Implementation:**
- New module `lib/py3/anongee_toolkit/cad2bim/as_built_reader.py`: walks a list of Revit elements (by category) and returns `CurveRecord` objects (`model.py:12`) built from `LocationCurve`/`LocationPoint`/`BoundingBoxXYZ`, mirroring the shape `geometry_reader.read_link` already returns (`readers/geometry_reader.py:28`) so `compare.diff` needs zero changes.
- Reuses, unmodified: `geom/compare.py:53` (`diff`) and `:120` (`format_console`), `readers/dxf_reader.py` (`read_dxf`), `classify/layers.py` (layer→category keying), `config.py:37` (`compare_tol_mm`), and `export.build_export_payload`/`export_json` (`report.py:44-47`) for the JSON side-channel.
- New pushbutton `AnonGee.tab/Core.panel/cad2bim.pushbutton` gets a second entry point (or a `RoundTripCheck.pushbutton` sibling in the same `Core.panel`) rather than folding into the build flow, since this runs independently of — and after — a build, on a model that may have been converted hours or days earlier.
- Ship it against the existing regression harness pattern in `cad2bim/tests/` (e.g. alongside `test_golden.py`) with a synthetic as-built record set and a DXF fixture, since `compare.diff` is already proven Revit-free and unit-testable per its own docstring (`compare.py:11-13`).

**Future update:**
- Feed flagged drift elements straight into a **Warning Auditor**-style batch view (already logged) so a "built off-tolerance" flag becomes a triaged, resolvable item rather than a one-off console dump.
- Cross-reference the **Type Splitter / Type Consolidator** family (already logged) — a round-trip delta caused by a wrong-type substitution (snapped to the nearest standard size instead of the drawn one) should suggest a type fix, not just report a number.
- Once P6 Annotation (`todo-list.md:1228`, SCOPE-3) is scoped, the same as-built vs drawn record pairs are exactly what a **Dimension Sweep** or **Tag Sweep** (both already logged) pass would need to annotate only the members that actually drifted.
- Extend the same `as_built_reader.py` extractor to feed a **Duplicate Instance Finder & Merger** (already logged) pass restricted to cad2bim-built elements, since a re-run of the converter on an already-built floor is a realistic way duplicates get created.

## 2026-09-26 05:42 UTC

**Idea name:** Curved Pad & Free-Form Peripheral Tie Placer — gives the structural pipeline the one geometry primitive it has never once used, an `Arc`, closing SCOPE-6 (`RC Automation.pushbutton/todo-list.md:334-337`, "`RebarUpdateCurvesData` for pad shapes that shape-driven bars cannot follow... a different constraint API from the one this tool uses, which is why it is scope and not a feature") so a circular or segmental pile cap, silo ring beam or rounded raft corner can be drawn accurately and reinforced with bars that actually curve, instead of the faceted polygon every other tool here — and the market's paid rebar add-ins — settle for

**Potential:**
Every outline this toolkit draws or reinforces is built from exactly one primitive: a straight `Line`. `structural/footings.py:236-262` builds a pad's `CurveLoop` with `loop.Append(Line.CreateBound(start, end))` for each vertex pair, and nothing else in that function ever constructs an `Arc`. On the rebar side it is the same story one layer down: `rc_automation/rebar_spec.py`'s whole footing-layer engine — `outline_for()` (`:307-311`), `bounds()`, `region_breaks()` (`:413-428`, "between two consecutive vertices of a polygon the edges are straight"), and `scan_segments()` (`:89-118`, explicitly built to handle "an L-shaped or waisted pad" by returning multiple straight crossings) — takes a flat list of `(x, y)` vertices and reasons entirely in straight polygon edges; and the geometry it hands to Revit is built by `structural/rebar_geometry.py:line_curves()` (`:66-88`), which does `curves.Add(Line.CreateBound(start, end))` for every single segment of every bar, U-bar leg included. This is genuinely sophisticated for anything a straight edge can describe — five-sided pads, pits cut out of a combined footing, gable-shaped pads split at each vertex — but there is no code path anywhere from pad outline to placed bar that can represent a curve, and `RC Automation.pushbutton/todo-list.md` names that gap itself, by its exact API, as SCOPE-6: `RebarUpdateCurvesData`, "a different constraint API from the one this tool uses" (`Rebar.CreateFromCurves` + `GetShapeDrivenAccessor`, per `structural/rebar_factory.py:344-380` and `:398`). A shape-driven `Rebar` sweeps one fixed straight-segment shape along a normal (`apply_layout()`, `:390-416`); it cannot make a single bar whose own centreline bends continuously, which is exactly what a ring of ties around a circular pile cap, or a peripheral bar following a rounded raft corner, needs. Circular and segmental pile caps are an everyday structural detail — piled foundations for columns near a boundary, silo and tank rings, rounded corners on a basement raft — and every BIM modeler's current option in Revit is either drawing an n-gon dense enough to look round and then placing dozens of straight tie segments by hand, or stepping outside this toolkit (and outside Revit's native rebar tools) entirely; the handful of paid marketplace add-ins that place curved/ring ties charge a per-seat subscription for exactly this one capability, and none of them are wired into this toolkit's own schedule-driven pipeline the way every other reinforcement layer here already is.

**Description:**
A new pushbutton, **Curved Pad Placer**, that reads a small extension to the existing FOOTING_PLACEMENT/FOOTING_TYPES sheets `footings.py` already parses (`commands/footings.py:10-14`) — the same `Outline` column, but each point may now optionally carry a bulge/radius so a pair of points describes an arc instead of a straight edge, the same convention DXF and most CAD outline formats already use, so a modeler can trace a curved pad straight off a linked CAD file rather than typing coordinates:

1. **The pad itself.** Extend `structural/footings.py`'s `CurveLoop` builder (`:236-262`) to emit `Arc.Create(...)` wherever a segment carries a bulge, alongside the existing `Line.CreateBound` for straight ones — the pad is still one `Floor.Create` call (`:327-329`), just with a mixed-curve profile instead of an all-straight one.
2. **The bars that can stay straight.** Any radial tie or straight main bar whose scan line only ever crosses straight edges keeps running through the existing `rebar_spec.py`/`rebar_geometry.py` pipeline unchanged — a circular pad's main bars across the middle are still straight lines of varying length, exactly as `plan_footing_layer()` already computes for any other non-rectangular pad.
3. **The bars that cannot.** For a peripheral tie or a bar whose own centreline must follow the curved edge, place it first as a normal, roughly-shaped `Rebar` (any valid `CreateFromCurves` shape, since Revit requires one to create the element at all), then call `RebarUpdateCurvesData` on it to overwrite its actual centreline with the true arc-derived point set — one bar, one real curve, not a fan of short straight facets.
4. **One dialog, two shapes of geometry.** The pushbutton's own UI is the same picked-outline / schedule-row flow every other placement command here already uses (`_placement.run`, per `commands/footings.py:32-40`); the arc handling is invisible to the modeler except that the pad comes out round instead of many-sided.

**Implementation:**
- `structural/footings.py`: teach the `CurveLoop` builder (`:236-262`) to read an optional per-vertex bulge/radius and emit `Arc.Create` for that segment; `outline_for()`/`bounds()` in `rc_automation/rebar_spec.py` need the same point format so the rest of the pipeline keeps working on the straight stretches untouched.
- `rc_automation/rebar_spec.py`: `region_breaks()` and `scan_segments()` already cut a layer at every vertex where the edge direction changes (`:413-428`); a curved stretch becomes one more kind of break — the region between two curve-flagged vertices routes to the new free-form path instead of `plan_footing_layer`'s straight-line `bar_points()`.
- New `structural/rebar_freeform.py`: `place_curve_following(doc, host, curve_points, bar_type_id, ...)` — create the placeholder `Rebar`, call `.GetShapeDrivenAccessor()` only to satisfy the initial create, then `RebarUpdateCurvesData` (Revit 2022+ API; confirm the exact overload against the repo's target Revit version before wiring it in, the one open question SCOPE-6 itself flags with "verify" language throughout `todo-list.md`).
- New `Curved Pad Placer.pushbutton` under `Essential.panel/StrucBIM.stack`, next to `ConvertFloor`/`DrawFloor`, reusing `commands/footings.py`'s `_placement.run` front end with the extended outline parser.

**Future update:**
- Once this ships, `Slab Fabric/Mesh Reinforcement Placer` (already logged, 2026-09-25) gets a natural extension: a curved slab edge or a rounded balcony fabric sheet, using the same arc-aware outline.
- `Footing Combiner` (already logged, 2026-09-20) could offer a filleted/rounded merge outline instead of only the straight combined-pad shape it currently proposes, once curved `CurveLoop`s exist to draw it with.
- Promote the arc-bulge outline convention into `anongee_toolkit` proper so `cad2bim` (which already reads real DXF arcs, per its own fixtures) can hand a genuinely curved pad straight through to this pipeline instead of the CAD importer's own rectilinear-recovery step flattening it first.

## 2026-09-26 09:15 UTC

**Idea name:** Rebar Re-Host Executor — builds `reconcile.py`'s own `RecreateAndRehost` strategy (SCOPE-1, `pending` since it was written) using the exact `Rebar.SetHostId` call its design comment says does not exist, and which `ConvertFloor.pushbutton` has already run successfully in production — closing SCOPE-2 ("verify `Rebar.SetHostId`") with evidence that has been sitting unused in this repo the whole time

**Potential:**
`reconcile.py` (`AnonGee.extension/lib/py3/anongee_toolkit/rc_automation/reconcile.py`) documents three rules for resolving a geometric disagreement between a schedule and an already-placed element (`:27-61`): (1) no dependents → edit the sketch in place; (2) dependents exist → create the corrected element, move each dependent onto it, retire the original; (3) neither is safe → report only. Rule 3 is the *only* one built — `GEOMETRY_CHANGES_ARE_DEFERRED = True` (`:85`) forces `strategy_for()` (`:94-104`) to always return `STRATEGY_REPORT_ONLY`, and `RC Automation.pushbutton/todo-list.md:304-311` logs the whole thing as **SCOPE-1**, `pending`. The reason rule 2 was never built is written right next to it, and it is wrong: "Revit has no re-host. There is no `Rebar.SetHostId`... 'Move each dependent onto it' is therefore implemented as capture -> recreate -> verify" (`reconcile.py:52-56`) — a far more expensive plan than a direct API call. But `Rebar.SetHostId(doc, hostId)` is exactly what `ConvertFloor.pushbutton/script.py:1373-1385` already calls, on real placed rebar, every time a slab converts to a foundation and back: `if hasattr(r, "SetHostId"): r.SetHostId(doc, new_el.Id); rehosted += 1`, then reports `"{rehosted}/{total} reinforcement re-hosted"` — proven working code, not a stub, already relied on to keep a footing's cage attached across a category change. `todo-list.md:313-317` even names this exact uncertainty as **SCOPE-2**, `pending`: *"`Rebar.SetHostId(doc, hostId)` appears to exist. If it does what it looks like, SCOPE-1's rule 2 is replaced by moving the bar... One read-only probe answers this."* The probe already ran — it just ran inside a different pushbutton, and nobody wired the answer back into `reconcile.py`'s design. A repo-wide `grep -rn "SetHostId" AnonGee.extension --include=*.py` turns up exactly the two hits above and nothing in `rc_automation/`: RC Automation still treats the question as open two commits after the toolkit answered it. The one place the comment is still right is `Dimension`s, which genuinely cannot be re-pointed at another element (`:53-54`, still true, unrelated to `SetHostId`) — but `reconcile.py`'s `Reconciliation.strategy(has_dependents=False)` (`:244-254`) takes a single boolean, so as written it cannot tell "only rebar depends on this" (safe to rehost) from "a dimension depends on this" (not safe) and would have to pick one blanket answer for both. `ConvertFloor.pushbutton`'s own `dependents_of()` (`script.py:580-609`) already solves exactly this: it walks `el.GetDependentElements(None)` and buckets every dependent into `rebar` / `dims` / `tags` / `hosted` / `openings` / `parts` / `slab_edges` / `analytical` by category and type — the per-category breakdown `has_dependents` needs and does not have.

**Description:**
No new pushbutton is the entry point — like the already-logged Schedule Drift Reconciler, this rides inside `RC Automation.pushbutton`'s second-run flow, since geometric drift only has meaning once an element has already been placed and the schedule has since changed under it:

1. **Give `Reconciliation.strategy()` the per-category breakdown it's missing.** Instead of one `has_dependents` bool, pass a dict of dependent buckets (reusing `ConvertFloor`'s own classification) so the strategy can tell "rebar-only" from "has a dimension/tag on it."
2. **Rule 2, for real:** when every dependent is in a re-hostable bucket (`rebar`, and — once the already-logged Slab Fabric/Mesh Reinforcement Placer ships — fabric), create the corrected element at the schedule's values, call `SetHostId(doc, new_el.Id)` on each dependent exactly as `ConvertFloor.pushbutton/script.py:1378-1379` already does, verify the re-host count matches the dependent count, and only then delete the original. Anything with a non-rehostable dependent (a `Dimension`, a tag) still correctly falls to rule 3 — the comment was right about dimensions, just wrong about rebar.
3. **Rule 1, for real, and cheaper than the doc assumes:** for a Column, `GEOMETRIC_FIELDS` (`width_mm`, `depth_mm`, `rotation_deg`) are an instance/type parameter and a rotation, not a sketch — "edit the sketch in place" is just a parameter `Set()` call with no sketch API involved. For a Footing (`length_mm`/`width_mm`/`thickness_mm`/`outline`, a `Floor` sketch), reuse `ConvertFloor.pushbutton`'s already-proven sketch-rebuild step rather than writing a second one.
4. **Flip the switch last, not first.** `GEOMETRY_CHANGES_ARE_DEFERRED` stays `True` until both rules are built and tested; the module already reads that flag in exactly one place (`:102`), so turning it on is a one-line, fully-reversible change once rules 1 and 2 are real.

**Implementation:**
- `rc_automation/reconcile.py`: change `strategy_for(has_dependents)` / `Reconciliation.strategy()` (`:94-104`, `:244-254`) to take a dependent-bucket dict instead of a bool, and split the current single "rehostable" assumption into an explicit allow-list (`rebar`, later `fabric`) versus everything else, which still forces `STRATEGY_REPORT_ONLY`.
- New `anongee_toolkit.rc_automation.geometry_apply` module: `edit_in_place(element, resolved_values)` for the parameter-only Column case; `recreate_and_rehost(doc, old_element, new_values, dependents)` for the Footing/Floor case, built by lifting the recreate → rebuild-sketch → `SetHostId` → verify → delete-original sequence straight out of `ConvertFloor.pushbutton/script.py` (its `dependents_of()`, shape-rebuild step, and lines 1373-1385) into a shared, callable function instead of leaving it fused into one pushbutton's script.
- `commands/columns.py` / `commands/footings.py`: once `Reconciliation.geometric_conflicts` is non-empty and `strategy()` returns something other than `ReportOnly`, call the new module inside the existing `_apply()` transaction, using the same per-row Excel/Model checklist the already-logged Schedule Drift Reconciler idea adds for the parameter-only conflicts.
- Ship Column support (rule 1) first — it needs no sketch work and proves the dependent-bucket split — then Footing support (rule 2), which needs the lifted `ConvertFloor` pipeline.

**Future update:**
- Once fabric reinforcement placement exists (the already-logged Slab Fabric/Mesh Reinforcement Placer), add `fabric` to the rehostable-dependent allow-list so a re-scheduled slab thickness change re-hosts its mesh the same way a re-scheduled footing re-hosts its cage.
- Extend the same lifted `recreate_and_rehost()` to `commands/beams.py` once Beam Reinforcement Placer ships, so a re-scheduled beam section gets the identical treatment instead of a third bespoke implementation.
- Promote `ConvertFloor.pushbutton`'s `dependents_of()` into `anongee_toolkit` proper (it is presently fused inside one pushbutton's `script.py`) so this module, the already-logged Type Splitter/Type Consolidator pair, and any future cross-category converter all read dependents the same documented way instead of each re-deriving the same `GetDependentElements` classification.

## 2026-09-25 10:42 UTC

**Idea name:** Slab Fabric/Mesh Reinforcement Placer — schedule-driven `FabricSheet`/`AreaReinforcement` placement for slab panels, closing SCOPE-5, the one reinforcement scope `RC Automation.pushbutton`'s own roadmap names by its exact Revit API classes and has left untouched since the scope was written, while every other structural category the toolkit places (columns, footings, and now framing) already gets its bars automated

**Potential:**
`RC Automation.pushbutton/todo-list.md:330-332` lists the gap in the tool's own words, under "Pending scope":
```
### SCOPE-5 — fabric reinforcement · `pending`

`FabricSheet` / `FabricArea` for slabs.
```
That sits next to SCOPE-3 (create columns, since closed by the already-logged Column Reinforcement Placer idea) and SCOPE-4 (laps and starter bars, deliberately excluded and "recorded here so the exclusion is a decision rather than an omission"). SCOPE-5 has had neither treatment — it is simply unstarted, and a repo-wide grep confirms nothing has quietly begun it: `grep -rn "AreaReinforcement\b" AnonGee.extension --include=*.py` and `grep -rn "FabricSheetType\|NewAreaReinforcement\|FabricArea(" AnonGee.extension --include=*.py` both return zero hits across roughly 110 Python files. The only trace of these Revit categories anywhere in the repo is a re-host safety net, not a placement path: `ConvertFloor.pushbutton/script.py:244-250` builds `CAT_REBAR` from `("OST_Rebar", "OST_AreaRein", "OST_PathRein", "OST_FabricAreas", "OST_FabricReinforcement")` purely so that converting a slab that already carries mesh doesn't orphan it — `script.py:1378-1379` calls `r.SetHostId(doc, new_el.Id)` on each dependent, fabric included. Nothing in the repository ever *creates* one.

The gap is structural, not incidental. `commands/slabs.py`'s own docstring states its scope in the same "named limitation" style the rest of the toolkit's docs use — *"one slab per panel, stopping at the inner face of its beams"* — with the edge-inset logic read from `BEAM_PLACEMENT`/`BEAM_TYPES` and zero mention of reinforcement anywhere in the file. `rc_automation/placement.py:91` defines `SHEET_SLABS = "SLAB_PLACEMENT"` and `parse_slabs()` (`placement.py:382`) reads panel geometry only; grepping `models.py` and `placement.py` for `MESH`/`FABRIC` returns nothing, next to `SHEET_FOOTING_REBAR`/`SHEET_COLUMN_REBAR` (`models.py:60,63`) and `footing_rebar_for()`/`column_rebar_for()` (`models.py:571,574`), which do exist. So `columns.sync` and `footings.sync` already carry a placed element straight through to its bars in one Excel-driven run, `beams.sync` is on the same track per the already-logged Beam Reinforcement Placer idea, and `slabs.sync` places bare concrete geometry and stops — the modeler is handed a fully panelled floor and then has to open every one of those panels in native Revit and sketch `AreaReinforcement` boundary sheets by hand, one panel at a time, re-typing top/bottom mesh type, lap length and cover from the same drawing that was already read once to build the Excel sheet RC Automation ran from.

That manual step is not a minor one. Welded-wire fabric or two-way mesh is the default reinforcement method for slabs on grade, suspended flat slabs and topping slabs on most RC jobs — meaning slabs, the single highest plan-area category this toolkit places, are also the one category whose reinforcement this toolkit cannot touch. Native Revit's `AreaReinforcement.Create` is a per-face, pick-the-boundary operation with no batch entry point, and nothing free automates it from a schedule; the closest market equivalents (AGACAD's Rebar for Revit / RC Structural Detailer product line, or ProStructures) are paid whole-suite add-ins whose slab-mesh tooling is still a manual, host-by-host sketch-and-assign workflow inside the add-in's own UI, not a spec sheet that drives placement the way `COLUMN_REBAR`/`FOOTING_REBAR` already drive this toolkit — so an Excel-driven `slabs.sync → mesh.sync` pass would be doing something no product on the market, paid or free, currently does for this specific step.

**Description:**
1. **New `SLAB_REBAR` sheet**, parsed the same way `COLUMN_REBAR`/`FOOTING_REBAR` already are (`models.py:571-575`'s `footing_rebar_for()`/`column_rebar_for()` pattern): one row per slab type or per panel override, carrying top/bottom fabric sheet designation (e.g. `A193`/`A252`-style mesh names), lap length, and top/bottom cover — the same fields a modeler currently reads off a rebar detail drawing and types into the native `AreaReinforcement` dialog by hand.
2. **`slabs.sync` extension.** Immediately after `commands/slabs.py`'s existing `_apply()` places a panel, a new step reads that panel's boundary (the same face `slabs.py` already computed to set the inset edges), resolves the mesh sheet type against `FabricSheetType`s already loaded in the project, and stages an `AreaReinforcement` plan for it — top and bottom layer, each with its resolved fabric sheet, lap and cover.
3. **Preview.** One row per panel on the shared `CheckList` shell (`anongee_toolkit/ui/checklist.py`), exactly like every other RC Automation sub-command: panel mark, resolved fabric sheet(s), cover, and an "unresolved" reason for any panel whose sheet spec names a fabric type not present in the project (mirroring `unresolved_bar_types()`/`unresolved_cover_types()` in `structural/rebar_types.py:114,220`, which already do exactly this for bar and cover types).
4. **Apply.** Ticked panels get their `AreaReinforcement` created inside the same chunked `RevitTransactionGroup` (`revit/transactions.py:117`) `slabs.sync` already runs its panel placement under, so mesh placement and slab placement land in one undo step, not two.
5. **Report.** Placed, unresolved (named, not silently skipped — the same discipline `unresolved_bar_types()` already enforces for columns and footings) and any panel whose face wasn't a simple flat boundary (sloped/free-form, deferred rather than guessed at, following SCOPE-6's own "different constraint API, so it is scope and not a feature" reasoning in `todo-list.md:337-339`).

**Implementation:**
- New `anongee_toolkit.rc_automation.rebar_spec.plan_slab_mesh(row, panel_boundary, ...)`, a sibling to `plan_column_mains()`/`plan_column_ties()` (`rebar_spec.py:682,715`), returning a pure-data plan (fabric sheet ids, lap, cover, boundary) with no transaction.
- New `structural.rebar_types.fabric_sheet_types(doc)`/`match_fabric_sheet(doc, name_hint)`/`unresolved_fabric_sheets(doc, rows)`, following `bar_types()`/`match_bar_type()`/`unresolved_bar_types()` (`rebar_types.py:58,90,114`) exactly, since `FabricSheetType` resolution is a new API surface the toolkit has never called and deserves the same "resolve up front, stop rather than guess" wrapper every other type-resolution path in this module already uses.
- New `structural.rebar_run.plan_slab_mesh(doc, workbook, ...)`/`place_slab_mesh(doc, plan, view=None, ...)`, structurally identical to `plan_footings()`/`place_footing()` (`rebar_run.py:118,221`), the pattern every other RC Automation reinforcement category already follows.
- `commands/slabs.py`'s `run()` gains an optional mesh step called right after `_apply()`, gated behind the new `SLAB_REBAR` sheet's presence so a workbook without it behaves exactly as today.
- Own `bundle.yaml`/`README.md` is unnecessary — this is a sub-command of the existing `RC Automation.pushbutton` (`Dev.panel/col2.stack/RC Automation.pushbutton`), the same way column and footing reinforcement are, not a new pushbutton.

**Future update:**
- Feed unresolved fabric sheets into the already-logged Type Matrix Generator, so a missing `FabricSheetType` can be bulk-created from the sheet spec instead of blocking the run.
- Extend `ConvertFloor.pushbutton`'s existing `SetHostId` re-host path (`script.py:1378-1379`) to also re-plan the `AreaReinforcement` boundary when a converted slab's outline changed shape, not just re-host it unchanged.
- Once slab mesh exists as placed data, wire it into the already-logged Reinforcement Density Heatmap and Rebar Cover Auditor, both of which currently have nothing to read for slabs.
- Investigate `RebarUpdateCurvesData` (SCOPE-6, `todo-list.md:337-339`) as a follow-on for slabs whose panel outline is not a simple flat polygon, once the flat-panel case here is proven in production.

## 2026-09-25 00:40 UTC

**Idea name:** Phase Assignment Auditor & Batch Re-Phaser — computes what each element's `Phase Created`/`Phase Demolished` *should* be from its level (and, where the mapping says so, from a demolished-without-a-modelled-replacement heuristic), flags every disagreement, and batch-corrects it, closing the one Revit-native staging mechanism this entire toolkit has only ever copied blindly, never read, decided, or checked

**Potential:**
A repo-wide grep for `PHASE_CREATED` and `PHASE_DEMOLISHED` across every `script.py` and every `anongee_toolkit` module returns exactly two hits, both on the same two lines: `ConvertFloor.pushbutton/script.py:895-897`, inside `copy_instance_params()`, where the two `BuiltInParameter`s are copied — alongside `ELEM_PARTITION_PARAM`, the workset — straight from a source element onto its recreated counterpart, with the comment `"# phase first: it can cascade onto other values"` (`script.py:894`). That is a preserve, not a decision: it never asks whether the source's phase was right to begin with, and every other command in the repo is silent on the subject entirely — `VIEW_PHASE`, `PhaseFilter`, `doc.Phases` and `ElementOnPhaseStatus` return **zero hits anywhere in `AnonGee.extension`**. RC Automation's own four placement commands confirm the gap from the creation side: `columns.py`, `beams.py`, `footings.py` and `slabs.py` all route through the shared `commands/_placement.py::run()` (`_placement.py:20-`), whose `apply` callback writes geometry, type, level and every schedule-mapped parameter onto a freshly created element — but never once touches `PHASE_CREATED`, so every element RC Automation places lands wherever `doc.Phases`'s currently active phase happens to be pointed at run time, regardless of which storey or which stage the sheet row actually describes. The sheet format itself already half-invites the word without ever meaning it: `rc_automation/placement.py:55` defines `_STOREY = ("storey", "story", "lift", "stage")` — a modeler who names a placement-sheet column `Stage`, meaning *construction phase*, gets it silently consumed as one more spelling of *floor level* instead, with no error and no way to tell the two apart from the sheet alone. The project's own roadmap wants the real thing and still doesn't have it: `todo-list.md:1019` (`FEAT-45`) records the owner's explicit ask for the rebar schedule to carry "**Phase or Procurement**, which must exist on **both** the concrete and the rebar parameters so a client can be told what a stage costs" — but that is a schedule *report* column; even once it ships, an element created by `columns.sync`/`footings.sync` for "Stage 2" and one created moments later for "Stage 1" in the same run would still land in whichever single Revit `Phase` was active for the whole session, so a phase-filtered plan or a Phase-split quantity schedule would show them identically and silently defeat the exact stage-cost split FEAT-45 exists to report. Outside this toolkit, mis-phasing is one of the most common and highest-consequence everyday mistakes on any renovation, addition or multi-stage RC project: an element pasted in from another model or another discipline's link defaults to whatever phase happens to be active in the *target* document, not the one it was drawn for; a wall that a later stage's demolition drawing shows removed is easy to forget to mark `Phase Demolished` on, so the demo plan still shows it standing; a modeler who forgets to switch the active phase before modelling silently mis-phases everything drawn that session with zero warning from Revit. Revit's own UI has no audit for any of this — Properties shows Phase Created/Demolished one element at a time, a Phase-filtered schedule can list the wrong ones but cannot compute what they *should* be, and there is no "does this element's phase agree with its level or its stage" check anywhere in the product. On the market, whole-suite paid add-ins (Ideate BIMLink, CTC BIM Project Suite) can bulk set Phase Created/Demolished via a schedule export/edit/reimport round trip, but that is a blind overwrite typed in by a human, exactly the workaround the already-logged Parameter Migrator and Workset Assigner ideas name for their own parameters — nothing free or paid derives the *expected* phase from an element's level or location and flags the disagreement.

**Description:**
1. **Mapping table** — a small, saved Level → Phase table (reusing whichever level-name vocabulary `AutoLevel.pushbutton` has already built for the project, rather than a second freeform level list), plus one toggle rule: an element whose `Phase Demolished` is earlier than the model's latest phase but has no later-phase element occupying the same location is flagged as a **likely missed replacement**, since a demolition with nothing modelled to replace it is usually an unfinished edit, not a real design intent.
2. **Scan** — every element in scope (selection / active view / whole model, category-filterable) read via `PHASE_CREATED`/`PHASE_DEMOLISHED` and its hosting `Level`; each is compared against the mapping table's expected phase for that level, and against the demolished-without-replacement heuristic.
3. **Checklist preview** — one row per disagreement: element id, category, level, current phase, proposed phase, reason — on the same `CheckList` shell (`anongee_toolkit/ui/checklist.py`) every other batch tool in the repo already runs on; level-derived mismatches pre-ticked, the demolished-without-replacement heuristic left unticked since it needs a human look rather than an automatic fix.
4. **Apply** — every ticked row's `Phase Created`/`Phase Demolished` is rewritten inside one `RevitTransaction` (`revit/transactions.py:71-`), one undo step for the whole batch.
5. **RC Automation hook** (later phase of this same idea) — a genuine `Phase` sheet column, resolved through the same mapping table and distinct from the existing `stage`-as-storey alias at `placement.py:55`, threaded into `_placement.py`'s shared `apply` callback so a newly created column, beam, footing or slab is phase-correct the moment it is placed instead of needing this auditor to catch it afterwards.

**Implementation:**
- New `anongee_toolkit.revit.phasing` module: `phase_by_name(doc)` (name → `Phase`, off `doc.Phases`), `expected_phase(level_name, mapping)`, `read_element_phase(elem)`/`set_element_phase(elem, phase, demolished=False)` — thin, `StorageType`-agnostic wrappers in the same style `revit/parameters.py::get_parameter_value`/`set_parameter_value` already establish for every other parameter write in the repo.
- New pushbutton bundle (`bundle.yaml`/`script.py`/`README.md`) in `Essential.panel/Modify.stack/`, alongside `JoinPriority.pushbutton`/`ToggleLinked.pushbutton`/`UnjoinByCategory.pushbutton` — the existing home for whole-model hygiene passes with no Excel bridge required.
- Level mapping loads from `AutoLevel.pushbutton`'s own already-built level table rather than duplicating a second level list the two tools could drift apart on.
- The RC Automation hook extends `commands/_placement.py::run()`'s `apply` signature with an optional phase resolver, and disambiguates `placement.py:55`'s `_STOREY` tuple so `"stage"` stops being consumed as a storey synonym once a sheet actually means construction phase by it.

**Future update:**
- Cross-check `Phase Created`/`Phase Demolished` against each sheet's own `View Phase` + `Phase Filter` pairing, so a demolition plan actually hides what it is supposed to hide instead of just carrying the right parameter values unseen.
- Extend the mapping to Rooms/Areas for an architectural handover, alongside the already-logged Room Finish Auto-Fill & Mismatch Auditor.
- Wire into the already-logged Design Option Scheme Comparator & Promoter, since a promoted option's elements often need re-phasing in the same transaction its Option Set is dissolved in.

## 2026-09-24 15:40 UTC

**Idea name:** Join Priority — Same-Category Tie-Break, closing the one join outcome `JoinPriority.pushbutton` names in its own docstring and then walks straight past: two elements of the *same* category meeting at a joint, where a category-rank list has nothing left to compare

**Potential:**
`JoinPriority.pushbutton` (`Essential.panel/Modify.stack/JoinPriority.pushbutton/script.py`) already solves cross-category join order well — a saved, reorderable category list, whole-model auto-join for touching-but-unjoined elements, and an edge-refresh pass for stale dashed linework — but it states its own ceiling in its top-level docstring in plain words: *"Same-category joins are left untouched in this version"* (`script.py:18`), and the code backs the claim exactly: every joined pair is classified once by `_cat_name(a) == _cat_name(b)`, and a same-category match is never added to `order_pairs` — it is only ever counted, both at scan time (`elif na == nb: same_cat += 1`, `script.py:359-360`) and again for newly auto-joined pairs (`else: same_cat += 1`, `script.py:428-431`) — then reported back to the modeler as a bare number, *"Same-category joins skipped: N"* (`script.py:472`), with no way to act on it. That is not a corner case on an RC structural model; it is the single most common join decision a modeler makes by hand every day: a primary (girder) beam framing into a column has to cut every secondary (joist) beam that lands on it so the secondary reads as *supported by* the primary rather than continuous through it, and today nobody but Revit's own arbitrary "last modified wins" join order decides which one actually gets cut — the modeler finds out only when a section view shows the wrong beam running through, or a concrete-volume schedule silently double-counts the overlap because the wrong element was left uncut. `IsCuttingElementInJoin`/`SwitchJoinOrder`, the exact two API calls that would fix it, are already imported and already working in this same file (`script.py:447,451`) — they are simply never invoked for a same-category pair because the tool has no second axis to rank by once category ties. Repo-wide, `BuiltInParameter.INSTANCE_STRUCT_USAGE_PARAM` (Revit's native "Structural Usage" field on Structural Framing — Girder / Joist / Purlin / Other / Automatic, exactly the primary/secondary distinction this needs) returns **zero hits anywhere in `AnonGee.extension`** or `anongee_toolkit` (confirmed by grep), so nothing in this toolkit has ever read the one built-in field Revit already ships for this. On the market, this exact "same-category join tie-break" is not sold as a feature at all — CTC's and IMAGINiT's join/geometry-cleanup add-ins expose category-priority join order (the half this tool already has) but stop at the same wall Revit's own priority system does; the primary-vs-secondary-beam convention is universally handled by a modeler eyeballing the join and switching it by hand, one beam at a time, via the tiny on-screen join-order arrows.

**Description:**
Extends `JoinPriority.pushbutton` in place — same dialog, same saved config, same one-pass apply — rather than shipping as a second tool, since the two problems (rank across categories, rank within one) are one workflow with two axes:

1. **Read the tie-break signal, per category** — for `Structural Framing`, read `INSTANCE_STRUCT_USAGE_PARAM` and rank `Girder` above `Joist`/`Purlin` above `Other`/`Automatic`; for every other category (columns, footings, walls, floors), fall back to a size comparison — the larger of the two by net solid volume cuts the smaller, on the ordinary structural convention that the bigger member is the one already fully formed when the smaller one lands on it (a stepped/combined footing cutting the isolated pad it absorbs, a thicker transfer wall cutting a thinner partition wall of the same category).
2. **Surface it in the same dialog** — the existing category `ListBox` (`PRIORITY_XAML`, `script.py:41-73`) gains a small per-category "tie-break" column next to each row: `Structural Usage` (only offered where that parameter exists on the category), `Larger volume cuts smaller`, or `Leave as-is` (today's behaviour, kept as the default so nothing changes for a modeler who ignores the new column).
3. **Extend the classification, not replace it** — `na == nb` no longer means "skip"; it means "read the chosen tie-break for that category, and only skip the pair if the tie-break itself can't distinguish them (same Structural Usage value, or a volume difference under a configurable percentage tolerance so two visually-identical beams aren't flipped on floating-point noise)." That residual, genuinely-unresolvable count is what still gets reported as "skipped," now meaning something real instead of covering every same-category pair unconditionally.
4. **Same apply path** — a resolved same-category pair is pushed into the existing `order_pairs` list (`script.py:437-456`) and goes through the exact same `IsCuttingElementInJoin`/`SwitchJoinOrder` block every cross-category pair already uses, inside the same `TransactionGroup` — one undo entry, whether the run touched only cross-category joins, only same-category ones, or both.
5. **Result dialog** — the existing summary line (`script.py:467-474`) splits into "cross-category: switched/correct" and "same-category: switched/correct/skipped (tie could not be broken)", so a modeler can see at a glance whether the beam framing on this floor actually got sorted or needs a manual look.

**Implementation:**
- New helper in the same `script.py`: `_tie_break_key(elem, rule)` returning a sortable value — the `INSTANCE_STRUCT_USAGE_PARAM` enum's own rank for the `"Structural Usage"` rule, `anongee_toolkit.revit.geometry.get_solid_volume_m3(elem)` (`geometry.py:10-37`, already public API, already zero-call-sites in `AnonGee.tab`) for the `"Larger volume cuts smaller"` rule — or `None` for `"Leave as-is"`, which short-circuits straight back to today's skip behaviour.
- `config.tie_break` persisted alongside the existing `config.cat_order`/`config.autojoin`/`config.refresh_edges` (`script.py:329-332`), one saved choice per category name, remembered the same way the category order already is.
- `PriorityDialog`'s XAML gains a `ComboBox` per list row (offered rules filtered to what that category actually supports — `Structural Usage` only where `INSTANCE_STRUCT_USAGE_PARAM` resolves on a sampled instance of that category in the current selection), wired the same way `autojoin_cb`/`refresh_cb` already feed `_apply()` (`script.py:155-164`).
- The `elif na == nb:` branch (`script.py:359-360`) becomes: look up `config.tie_break.get(na)`, compute both elements' tie-break keys, and either append to `order_pairs` (keys differ) or increment `same_cat` (rule is "Leave as-is", or the keys tie within tolerance) — the exact same shape the cross-category branch already has, just fed a same-category key instead of the category-rank dict.

**Future update:**
- A third tie-break rule, `"Higher top elevation cuts lower"`, for the vertical-join case (a taller wall segment cutting a shorter same-type infill) once the volume rule is proven in production.
- Per-pair manual override list (pick two specific same-category elements and force a direction) for the rare case a modeler disagrees with every automatic rule — the same "checklist beats blanket automation" posture every other batch tool in this repo already holds to.
- Extend `Structural Usage`-based ranking into the already-logged Analytical-Physical Alignment Auditor's read layer, since a beam whose `Structural Usage` disagrees with its analytical role (a `Joist` carrying what the analytical model treats as a primary load path) is a modelling error this tie-break would otherwise just quietly act on.

The bridge roadmap names this gap directly, twice, and both entries are still `pending`: `todo-list.md:1049` (`FEAT-14`) reads *"`schedules.sync` — build Revit schedules with fields, filters, sorting, formatting | The easiest win on the list. That document (`:9-55`, "Part 1 — `schedules.sync`") is not a sketch — it specifies the exact sheet shape (`Name`, `Category`, `Fields`, `SortBy`, `GroupBy`, `Totals`, `Filter`, `Itemise`, one row per schedule, `:24-33`), the matching rule (*"Matched by name, never renamed… Never deleted… A field the model does not have is reported by name… Idempotent: run twice, the second says `matched`"*, `:42-48`), and even the reason it matters: *"the first time `S_ScheduleMark` earns its keep. A repo-wide grep for the API surface this needs — `ViewSchedule.CreateSchedule`, `AddSortGroupField`, `ScheduleFilter(` — returns **zero hits anywhere in `AnonGee.extension`**, confirming it was never started; the toolkit's own view-enumeration helper makes the same gap visible from the read side, since `anongee_toolkit.revit.views.ALLOWED_VIEW_TYPES` (`revit/views.py:9-17`) deliberately lists `FloorPlan`, `CeilingPlan`, `EngineeringPlan`, `Section`, `Elevation`, `ThreeD` and has no `ViewType.Schedule` — nothing in the repo's shared view utilities has ever been asked to touch a schedule as an object to create, only `Export Schedule.pushbutton` reads one that already exists (`Export Schedule.pushbutton/script.py:455-666`, one-way, per the already-logged Schedule Round-Trip idea). Meanwhile the manual cost this closes is real and recurring, not hypothetical: every new project on this toolkit needs the same five-or-so standard schedules — column, footing, beam, slab, a combined concrete-volume roll-up — built by hand through Revit's Schedule Properties dialog, field by field, sort rule by sort rule, project after project, because Revit's own cross-project answer (`Transfer Project Standards`) copies *every* schedule wholesale with no field-level diff or selective update, the same all-or-nothing ceiling the already-logged Standards Diff & Selective Transfer idea names for line styles and materials — that idea's own scope (`:200`) lists "line styles, line patterns, fill patterns, materials, view filters and view templates" and stops there; it never mentions a schedule. On the market, spreadsheet-driven schedule authoring is the headline, per-seat-licensed feature of Ideate BIMLink and CTC's suite (create schedules from an external definition) or it is manual, once per project, native-only; nothing free reads a plain-text spec and both creates missing schedules and safely re-applies it to ones that already exist.

**Description:**
`Schedule Builder.pushbutton`, in `Essential.panel/Action.stack/` alongside `Export Schedule.pushbutton` — same audience, opposite direction, and built to run standalone (no Excel bridge, no macro, no `.xlsm` required), with the bridge workbook's own `SCHEDULES` sheet format as an optional import so a spec written for the bridge (per the spec doc) works here unchanged:

1. **Load the spec** — either a picked `.xlsx`/`.csv` in the sheet shape `docs/specs/2026-08-26-schedules-and-footing-rebar.md:24-33` already lays out (`Name, Category, Fields, SortBy, GroupBy, Totals, Filter, Itemise`), or a fresh row built in the dialog itself via a category picker plus a field checklist populated from `ScheduleDefinition.GetSchedulableFields()`, for a modeler with no spreadsheet to hand.
2. **Match existing** — `FilteredElementCollector(doc).OfClass(ViewSchedule)`, keyed by `Name`, exactly the *"matched by name, never renamed"* rule the spec states; a schedule the model already has that the spec doesn't mention is listed as **left alone**, never deleted, the same posture the spec itself commits to.
3. **Resolve fields** — each `Fields`/`SortBy`/`Totals` cell is a comma-list, parsed with the same `split()` the schedule-mark resolver already uses for an identical shape (`commands/_marks.py:48-55`, *`"S_ScheduleMark, ID, Comments"` -> the three, in order*); a named field with no matching `SchedulableField` on that category is reported **by name**, not silently dropped, mirroring the *"no column type called `C-300x600`"* posture `ConvertFloor.pushbutton`/RC Automation already hold for a missing type.
4. **Preview checklist** — one row per spec row: create / update-in-place / already-matches / unresolved-fields-with-names, each pre-ticked except the unresolved ones, the same modeless `CheckList` shell every other batch tool in the repo runs on.
5. **Apply** — for **create**, `ViewSchedule.CreateSchedule(doc, categoryId)` then `definition.AddField()` per resolved field in order, `AddSortGroupField()` per `SortBy`/`GroupBy` entry, `AddFilter()` for the one `Filter` condition, a `HasTotal`/`TotalsCalculation` flag per `Totals` name, and `definition.IsItemized = (Itemise != "No")`; for **update**, the same calls against the matched schedule's existing `Definition`, additive only — an existing field/sort/filter the spec no longer lists is left in place and reported, not removed, since silently narrowing a schedule someone else has since hand-edited is a worse failure than leaving it slightly stale.
6. Result report — created / updated / already-matched / unresolved counts, same shape every batch tool in the repo already closes on.

**Implementation:**
- New `anongee_toolkit.revit.schedules` module: `existing_by_name(doc)` (the `ViewSchedule` collector + name-keyed match), `schedulable_field(definition, field_name)` (name → `SchedulableField`, or `None` with the attempted name preserved for the report), `apply_spec(doc, schedule, spec_row)` (the `AddField`/`AddSortGroupField`/`AddFilter`/`IsItemized` sequence, shared by both the create and update paths so they can never drift apart).
- Reuses `commands/_marks.split()` (`commands/_marks.py:48-55`) for every comma-list cell rather than writing a second parser for the same `"a, b, c"` shape.
- Spec loading reuses `anongee_toolkit.rc_automation.excel_engine`'s `openpyxl` read path (`excel_engine.py:444,494,504`) when a workbook is picked, and a small dependency-free CSV path otherwise — the same "workbook or file, either way" flexibility `Export Schedule.pushbutton` already gives on the export side.
- New pushbutton bundle (`bundle.yaml`/`script.py`/`README.md`) in `Essential.panel/Action.stack/`, on the same modeless `CheckList`/`RevitTransaction` shell every batch tool in the repo already runs on.

**Future update:**
- Wire this module in as the actual `schedules.sync` bridge command (`commands/schedules.py`, registered in `commands/__init__.py` next to `beams`/`columns`/`footings`) once proven standalone, so `CONTROL`-sheet automation and the manual pushbutton share one engine instead of drifting into two.
- `Totals`/`Itemise`/`Filter` today cover the spec's stated first-version scope; the spec's own *"what it will not do in the first version"* list (`:50-55`) — calculated values, conditional formatting, multi-condition filters, key schedules, embedded schedules — is the natural v2 queue, in the order the spec itself gives it.
- Saved spec profile per project type (a standard five-schedule RC building kit vs. a steel-frame kit), reusable across new projects with one pick, the same saved-profile posture already proposed for Parameter Migrator and Universal Renamer.
- Pair with the already-logged Schedule Round-Trip: once a schedule exists from a spec, its exported/edited copy has a stable field list to diff against, closing the create → export → edit → write-back loop end to end.
