## 2026-09-15 15:40 UTC

**Idea name:** Sheet Issue Kit — one-click PDF + DWG + NWC export of a rule-picked sheet set, token-named, with a since-last-run diff so a re-issue only touches what actually changed

**Potential:**
Every drawing issue — a weekly coordination set, a milestone submission, a tender package — repeats the same non-modelling admin for every sheet in scope: open Revit's PDF export dialog, hand-pick or reuse a saved sheet set, set naming, export; reopen the completely separate DWG export dialog, re-pick the same sheets (its own naming options don't match the PDF dialog's), export; if Navisworks coordination is also owed, a third dialog, a third naming pass — three independent settings screens, repeated in full for every issue, even when only three of forty sheets actually changed since last week. Nothing in this repo touches this at all: grepping every script under `AnonGee.tab` and every module under `anongee_toolkit` for `PDFExportOptions`, `DWGExportOptions`, `ExportOptions`, `ViewSheetSet`, `PrintRange`, `IPrintSetup`/`GetPrintSetup` and `ExportImage` returns zero Revit API hits anywhere in the codebase (the `pythonwin`/`ezdxf` vendored-library hits for `printRange`/`.print()` are unrelated MFC/CLI code, not Revit). The one place "PDF" appears at all, `Export Schedule.pushbutton`'s `ChkSavePdf` checkbox (`script.py:309,421`) feeding `_excel_polish(xlsx_path, want_pdf)` (`script.py:669-732`), is a different job entirely: it drives **Excel COM** (`xlTypePDF`, `script.py:728`) to print the *just-exported .xlsx workbook* to PDF, degrading gracefully when Excel isn't installed (`script.py:673`, "degrades gracefully without Excel") — it never calls `Document.Export`, never touches a `View`/`ViewSheet`, and has nothing to do with issuing drawing sheets. The view-enumeration helper that exists, `anongee_toolkit.revit.views.get_eligible_views()`/`ALLOWED_VIEW_TYPES` (`revit/views.py:9-17,35-56`), explicitly excludes `ViewSheet` from its allowed set — built for element-visibility tools, not sheet issuance — so it cannot be reused here as-is. The only code that reads a sheet's own `SheetNumber`/`Name` at all is `anongee_toolkit.structural.inventory._views()` (`structural/inventory.py:323-341`), and only as one read-only field in `Extract Template.pushbutton`'s audit report — never as a live, selectable list, and never combined with a titleblock read for per-sheet paper size. Revit's own `Revision`/issue-date concept (`ViewSheet.GetCurrentRevision`, `SHEET_ISSUE_DATE`) is likewise never read anywhere in the repo; the only "revision" in the codebase is `BBS Generator.pushbutton`'s own unrelated Excel-sidecar diff (`chk_revision`/`tb_revision`, `ui/main_window.py:193-200,423-434`), which tracks bar-list changes between two spreadsheet snapshots, not a sheet's Revit revision. Revit's native PDF/DWG exporters can already batch off a manually pre-built, saved `ViewSheetSet` element, but that native path still forces three separate dialogs (PDF, DWG, and — if owed — a fourth-party Navisworks exporter) with three independent, non-token, non-parameter-aware naming schemes reconfigured by hand on every single issue, and it has no concept of "only the sheets that changed since the set was last issued" — every run re-exports the whole set regardless of what actually moved. On the market, wrapping the native exporter in a single multi-format pass with templated file names is exactly the headline feature CTC's BIM Tools, Ideate Sheets/BIMLink, and IMAGINiT Clarity sell as one line item of a much larger paid, per-seat suite; none of them — free or paid — skip re-exporting an unchanged sheet on a re-issue.

**Description:**
`Sheet Issue Kit.pushbutton` (new tool in `Essential.panel/Action.stack`, sibling to `Export Schedule.pushbutton` and forking that tool's `ExportScheduleDialog` shell — search box, checklist, badge triplet — for sheets instead of schedules) runs in one window:

1. **Sheet list** — every `ViewSheet` in the model (`FilteredElementCollector(doc).OfClass(ViewSheet)`, the same collector `structural.inventory._views()` already proves at `inventory.py:337`, promoted from a read-only audit field to a live, checkable list), shown with the same search-and-checkbox `ListBoxItem` pattern `ExportScheduleDialog._populate`/`_on_search_changed` already build (`Export Schedule.pushbutton/script.py:329-359`), all ticked by default.
2. **Filter to scope, not just tick by hand** — an optional rule row (parameter + value: discipline, a sheet-list "Revision" text parameter, a Yes/No "Issue" checkbox parameter, or a saved Revit `ViewSheetSet` name if one already exists) that pre-ticks the matching subset via `anongee_toolkit.revit.parameters.get_parameter_value` (`revit/parameters.py:36-82`) — the same read function the already-logged Schedule Round-Trip and Parameter Migrator ideas both call, this becomes a further caller — so a "just the structural set" or "just what's marked Issue = Yes" re-issue doesn't require re-hand-picking sheets from a 200-sheet list every week.
3. **Since-last-run diff** — the genuinely new piece nothing in the repo or, as far as this codebase's own market research shows, any competing add-in does: a small per-project sidecar JSON (`sheet_issue_kit_log.json`, alongside the model, one entry per sheet keyed by `ElementId` storing the sheet's content-hash — every placed view's `ElementId` + last-changed `Element.UniqueId`/`Document.GetElement(id).VersionNumber` pairing already exposed by the Revit API — and the export timestamp) is diffed against the current model on open; each ticked sheet shows **unchanged since last issue** or **changed — will export**, with a one-click "export everything anyway" override, so a Friday re-issue after three sheets changed doesn't silently regenerate all forty.
4. **Formats** — independently toggleable PDF / DWG / NWC checkboxes; PDF via `Document.Export(folder, name, ViewSet, PDFExportOptions)`, DWG via `Document.Export(folder, name, viewIds, DWGExportOptions)`, NWC via the Navisworks exporter's `NavisworksExportOptions` when that add-in is installed — detected once at startup and the NWC checkbox disabled with an inline reason rather than failing mid-run if it isn't, the same "degrade, don't crash" posture `Export Schedule.pushbutton`'s own Excel-COM absence handling already proves (`script.py:673`).
5. **File naming** — a token template (`{SheetNumber}_{SheetName}_{Revision}_{Date}`) applied per sheet per format, reusing the token formatter the already-logged Universal Renamer idea proposes if that tool has landed by the time this one is built, otherwise a small standalone `re`-based formatter promoted into the shared one the moment Universal Renamer exists — the same fallback posture the already-logged Sheet Set Builder idea commits to for its own naming step.
6. **Preview checklist** (`anongee_toolkit.ui.checklist.CheckList`, the shell every other batch tool in the repo already runs on) — one row per (sheet, format): resolved file name, size/scale read off the sheet's own titleblock via `structural.inventory._views()`'s existing title-block enumeration (`inventory.py:343-...`), and status (**will export** / **unchanged — skip** / **blocked — NWC exporter not installed**).
7. **Export** — one pass through the ticked rows per format, each format's own `ExportOptions` object built once and reused across every sheet in that format rather than reconstructed per sheet; a single bad sheet (a corrupt titleblock, a locked output path) is caught and reported without aborting the rest of the run.
8. **Report** — exported / skipped-unchanged / blocked counts per format, the sidecar log updated for every sheet actually exported, and an `openpyxl` issue-record (reusing the styling constants `Export Schedule.pushbutton` already proves, `script.py:148-161`) naming exactly what went out in this issue — the artifact a modeler currently has to build by hand for a transmittal record.

**Implementation:**
- New `anongee_toolkit.revit.sheet_export` module — the genuinely new piece, since no `PDFExportOptions`/`DWGExportOptions`/`ViewSheetSet` call exists anywhere in the toolkit today (confirmed by the repo-wide grep above): one function per format wrapping `Document.Export`, and one `NavisworksExportOptions` probe that returns `None` rather than raising when the Navisworks exporter isn't installed.
- Sheet enumeration goes straight through the same `FilteredElementCollector(doc).OfClass(ViewSheet)` collector `structural.inventory._views()` (`inventory.py:337`) already proves, rather than a second hand-rolled collector; titleblock size/scale reuses that same function's title-block read (`inventory.py:343-...`).
- Parameter-rule filtering and diff-detection both go through `anongee_toolkit.revit.parameters.get_parameter_value` (`revit/parameters.py:36-82`), the same read function the already-logged Schedule Round-Trip and Parameter Migrator ideas call — no second parameter-read convention introduced.
- UI shell is a direct fork of `ExportScheduleDialog` (`Export Schedule.pushbutton/script.py:281-360`) — same `XamlReader.Load`, search box, badge triplet — with the schedule checklist swapped for a sheet checklist and one added filter-rule row.
- `sheet_issue_kit_config.json` alongside `script.py` (default naming template, default rule parameter name, which formats default-on, sidecar log path), mirroring `slab_convert_config.json`'s override-by-config shape every other configurable tool in the repo already uses.
- Excel issue-record export reuses the `openpyxl` path and brand constants already proven in `Export Schedule.pushbutton` (`script.py:148-161,455-...`).
- Sits in `Essential.panel/Action.stack/`, next to `Export Schedule.pushbutton`, its direct sibling and the tool it forks its dialog shell from.

**Future update:**
- Read a project's actual `ViewSheetSet` elements (if any already exist) as a fourth filter option alongside the parameter rule, so a firm that already maintains named sheet sets in-model doesn't have to re-express that grouping as a parameter rule.
- Feed the sidecar diff log into a "what changed since last issue" summary view, similar in spirit to the already-logged Warning Auditor and Dimension Sweep ideas' own re-run/diff modes, so a coordination meeting can open with "these six sheets moved since Tuesday" instead of a modeler reconstructing that by memory.
- Batched combined-PDF output (one file for the whole set, Revit's own "combine into one file" PDF option) as a togglable alternative to one-file-per-sheet, for submissions that want a single transmittal PDF rather than a folder of them.
- Tie the token naming template to the same shared formatter the already-logged Universal Renamer and Sheet Set Builder ideas both plan to converge on, so all three tools stop carrying independent standalone fallback formatters the moment one of them lands first.

## 2026-09-15 05:39 UTC

**Idea name:** View Graphics Sync — one-view-to-many propagation of category overrides, filters, view range and worksets, generalized off Copy Rebar Visibility's own narrow element-hide engine

**Potential:**
Getting a set of similar views to look consistent — every structural framing plan carrying the same category overrides, the same filter list with the same colours and the same enabled state, the same view range, the same workset visibility — is one of the most repeated setup chores on a BIM team, and today this repo gives a modeler exactly one narrow tool for it: `Copy Rebar Visibility.pushbutton` copies hide/unhide state plus the `Unobscured`/`Solid` flags, but only for `OST_Rebar` (`Copy Rebar Visibility.pushbutton/script.py:274-279` collects `FilteredElementCollector(doc).OfCategory(BuiltInCategory.OST_Rebar)` — nothing else). Everything past that is manual, view by view: category graphic overrides (line colour/weight, surface pattern, halftone, transparency), the filter list and each filter's own override + enabled flag, view range, and workset visibility have no copy-between-views tool anywhere in this codebase — confirmed by grepping every script under `AnonGee.tab` and every module under `anongee_toolkit` for `SetCategoryOverrides`, `GetCategoryOverrides`, `SetFilterOverrides`, `IsFilterEnabled`, `GetViewRange`, `SetViewRange` and `SetWorksetVisibility`: `SetFilterOverrides`/`AddFilter` turn up exactly once, in `anongee_toolkit/cad2bim/builders/view_filters.py:170-171`, and only as a **write** the first time a filter is created on every *open* view (`view_filters.py`'s own docstring: "applied to every OPEN view in the document") — never read from one view and replicated onto a chosen target set; `GetCategoryOverrides`/`SetCategoryOverrides`, `GetViewRange`/`SetViewRange` and `SetWorksetVisibility` return **zero hits anywhere in the repo**. The one related precedent, `ToggleLinked.pushbutton`, only flips a single category's (`OST_RvtLinks`) hidden flag on the active view and already had to solve the "a view template is silently blocking this" problem once (`template_controls_links`/`release_links_from_template`, `ToggleLinked.pushbutton/script.py:14-33`, keyed to `BuiltInParameter.VIS_GRAPHICS_RVT_LINKS`) — that per-parameter template-override check has never been generalized past that one link-visibility parameter. Revit's own native answer is View Templates, but a template is all-or-nothing per parameter and project-wide by design; it cannot selectively push "just these three filters, as they are right now on this one cleaned-up view" onto eleven others without either forcing every one of them onto a template (dragging in every *other* template-controlled setting too) or hand-replicating the change eleven times — 10–20 minutes per view for a non-trivial override set, redone every time a new plan is duplicated mid-project. On the market this exact "view A's graphics → views B, C, D" copy is sold only bundled inside large paid production suites (CTC BIM Tools, IMAGINiT Utilities) as one line item of a much bigger licensed toolset; nothing free does a direct, selective, view-to-view graphics copy outside of template assignment.

**Description:**
`View Graphics Sync.pushbutton` (new sibling to `Copy Rebar Visibility.pushbutton` in `Bulk Tool.stack`, reusing that tool's own source-combo / target-checklist window shell) runs in one pass:

1. **Pick the source view** — a ComboBox of every eligible view (same `ALLOWED_VIEW_TYPES` set `Copy Rebar Visibility.pushbutton/script.py:39-46` already defines: floor/ceiling/structural plan, section, elevation, 3D), defaulting to the active view exactly like the rebar tool already does (`script.py:117-123`).
2. **Pick the target views** — the same `CheckBox`-per-`ListBoxItem` multi-select list (`script.py:125-134`), built from `anongee_toolkit.revit.views.get_eligible_views()` (`revit/views.py:30-56`) instead of a hand-rolled collector, so this becomes that helper's second real caller.
3. **Choose which property groups to sync**, each independently toggleable so a modeler who only wants filters fixed isn't forced to also touch view range: Category visibility + graphic overrides (line colour/weight/pattern, surface pattern, halftone, transparency — every category the source view actually overrides, read via `View.GetCategoryOverrides`/hidden state via `GetCategoryHidden`, generalizing `ToggleLinked.pushbutton`'s single-category `SetCategoryHidden` call to every category the source touches); Filters (the source view's own filter list plus each filter's override settings and enabled/visible flag, added to a target view via `AddFilter`/`SetFilterOverrides` the same way `cad2bim/builders/view_filters.py:170-171` already creates them, but read from an existing view instead of freshly authored); View Range (plan-type views only, via `GetViewRange`/`SetViewRange`); Workset visibility (`SetWorksetVisibility` per workset).
4. **Template-lock detection per (view, property)** — before anything is queued, checks whether a target view's `ViewTemplateId` controls the parameter in question, reusing `ToggleLinked.pushbutton`'s own `template_controls_links` pattern (`script.py:14-24`) generalized from the one `VIS_GRAPHICS_RVT_LINKS` parameter to every `BuiltInParameter` the chosen property groups touch; a locked pair is flagged **template-controlled — skip** in the preview rather than silently no-op'ing, with the same "edit template / remove template / cancel" three-way choice `ToggleLinked.pushbutton` already offers (`script.py:45-60`), scoped per template rather than per run.
5. **Preview table** — one row per (property group, target view), showing will-copy / already-matches / template-locked-skip, unticked rows pre-excluded, same resolve-preview-tick-write shape every other batch tool in the repo already uses.
6. **Apply** — one `Transaction`, generalizing `Copy Rebar Visibility.pushbutton`'s own commit/rollback block (`script.py:205-264`); a per-view failure is caught and reported without aborting the rest of the run, matching that tool's existing per-call `try/except`.
7. **Report** — copied / already-matched / template-locked / failed counts per view and property group, shown in the same info/success/error badge triplet the source tool already renders (`script.py:153-169`).

**Implementation:**
- New `anongee_toolkit.revit.view_graphics` module — the genuinely new piece, since none of `GetCategoryOverrides`/`SetCategoryOverrides`/`GetViewRange`/`SetViewRange`/`SetWorksetVisibility` exist anywhere in the toolkit today (confirmed by the repo-wide grep above): one read function per property group returning a plain snapshot from the source view, one apply function writing that snapshot onto a target view, and one `template_controls(view, doc, builtin_param)` helper lifted straight out of `ToggleLinked.pushbutton/script.py:14-24` and parameterized instead of hardcoded to `VIS_GRAPHICS_RVT_LINKS`.
- Filter copy reuses the `AddFilter`/`SetFilterOverrides` call shape already proven in `cad2bim/builders/view_filters.py:170-171`, but sourced from `View.GetFilters()` + `View.GetFilterOverrides(id)` on the chosen source view instead of a filter this same run just created.
- View/target enumeration goes through `anongee_toolkit.revit.views.get_eligible_views()` (`revit/views.py:30-56`) rather than the hand-rolled `FilteredElementCollector(doc).OfClass(View)...` block `Copy Rebar Visibility.pushbutton/script.py:289-296` still carries — both tools converge on the one shared helper.
- UI shell (`ui.xaml`, the source-combo/target-checklist/badge layout, `_flush_ui`'s `Dispatcher.Invoke` pattern) is a direct copy-and-extend of `Copy Rebar Visibility.pushbutton`'s own `ui.xaml` and `script.py`, adding one more panel of property-group checkboxes above the existing target list.
- New `view_graphics_sync_config.json` alongside `script.py` (which property groups default on, whether workset visibility is offered when the document isn't workshared — hidden rather than shown-disabled), mirroring `slab_convert_config.json`'s override-by-config shape.
- Sits in `Essential.panel/Bulk Tool.stack/`, next to `Copy Rebar Visibility.pushbutton`, with its own `bundle.yaml`/`CHANGELOG.md` since, like `AutoLevel.pushbutton`, this is a tool likely to grow more property groups (annotation categories, linked-model category visibility, phase filters) over time.

**Future update:**
- Fold `Copy Rebar Visibility.pushbutton`'s own rebar-specific hide/`Unobscured`/`Solid` logic in as one more property group once this tool is proven in production, so the two converge into one and a modeler doesn't need to run two separate "copy from this view" tools back to back.
- Saved, named view-graphics profiles (a captured snapshot, not tied to a live source view) reusable across sessions and projects — the same "save the rule set, not just the one-off copy" step the already-logged Universal Renamer and Grid & Level Extent Sync ideas both end on.
- Drift re-check mode: re-run against the same source/target pairing later in the project and report which target views have since diverged, rather than only ever doing a one-shot push.
- Extend past model views to Legends and Drafting Views' filter lists, and to linked-model category visibility (generalizing `ToggleLinked.pushbutton` fully rather than leaving it as the one-category special case it is today).

## 2026-09-15 00:39 UTC

**Idea name:** Grid & Level Extent Sync — batch 2D-extent and bubble-visibility propagation for datum planes across every structural view

**Potential:**
Every plan, section and elevation a modeler opens shows the model's grids and levels at whatever 2D extent and bubble on/off state Revit last happened to leave them at in *that specific view* — a `Grid`/`Level` is one model element, but its visible endpoint and whether its bubble shows are **per-view overrides**, so a project with 12 structural plans and 8 grids means up to 96 independent bubble/extent states a modeler drags and toggles by hand, one grid, one end, one view at a time, and redoes every time a new plan is duplicated or a grid is added mid-project. Revit's own "Reset to 3D Extents" is the only native shortcut, and it only ever restores the family's default 3D extent in that one view — it cannot copy a chosen, tidied extent-and-bubble pattern from a view a modeler has already cleaned up onto the other eleven, and it works one grid end at a time, never as a batch across a view set. The pieces this needs already exist in this repo, built for an entirely different job and never exposed to a button: `anongee_toolkit.structural.grids.grids(doc)` (`anongee_toolkit/structural/grids.py:25-33`) already reads every `Grid` in the model into `{name: Grid}`, and `segment_mm(grid)` (`grids.py:40-53`) already reduces each one to a clean 2D line segment in millimetres — refusing a curved grid by name rather than approximating it, the same explicit-refusal posture `ConvertFloor.pushbutton` uses for its own unsupported cases — but the only callers of that module today are `structural/inventory.py`, `structural/structure_run.py` and RC Automation's own `commands/grids.py`/`commands/_placement.py` (confirmed: grepping the whole toolkit for `structural.grids` turns up exactly those four, and none of them is a pushbutton), so it exists purely to cross two named grids into an `(x, y)` footing-placement point — never to read a grid's own line for a view-level operation. `anongee_toolkit.structural.levels.levels(doc)` (`structural/levels.py:19-28`) already returns every level sorted bottom-to-top, proven only inside `AutoLevel.pushbutton`'s own planner and the already-logged Column Stack Auditor and Sheet Set Builder ideas' stack/level enumeration — never for a Level's own per-view datum overrides. Nothing under `anongee_toolkit` or any `AnonGee.tab/**/script.py` calls `DatumPlane.SetDatumExtentType`, `SetCurveInView`, `GetCurvesInView`, `ShowBubbleInView`, `HideBubbleInView` or `IsBubbleVisibleInView` at all — confirmed by grepping every script for `DatumEnds`, `ShowBubble`, `HideBubble` and `SetDatumExtentType`, which return no hits anywhere in `AnonGee.extension` — so the entire per-view datum-override surface of the Revit API is untouched by this codebase today, on both the Grid and the Level side. On the market, propagating a cleaned-up grid/level extent-and-bubble pattern across a whole view set is sold only as one line item bundled inside paid whole-project drafting-production suites (CTC's and BIM One's grid/level tools, sold as part of a much larger licensed toolset); nothing free reads a "reference" view's own per-grid state and replicates it, view by view, across the rest of a set.

**Description:**
`Grid & Level Extent Sync.pushbutton` (new tool in `Essential.panel/StrucBIM.stack`, sibling to `RoundElementDist.pushbutton` and the already-logged Grid Mark Assistant — all three read the same `Grid` geometry, this one is the one that touches per-view datum overrides rather than the `Mark` parameter) runs in one window:

1. **Pick the reference view** — any plan, section or elevation already showing the datums the way the modeler wants them (extents trimmed to the sheet, bubbles only where they belong); reads its current per-grid and per-level state via `Grid.GetCurvesInView(DatumExtentType.ViewSpecific, ref_view)` / `Level.GetCurvesInView(...)` and `IsBubbleVisibleInView(DatumEnds.End0/End1, ref_view)`, matched to the model-wide `{name: Grid}` map `grids(doc)` (`grids.py:25-33`) already provides, and `levels(doc)` (`levels.py:19-28`) for the level side.
2. **Pick the target views** — a checklist of every other plan/section/elevation in the model (or a chosen view set), reusing the multi-select checklist shell (`anongee_toolkit.ui.hostwnd`/`checklist`) every other batch tool in the repo already runs on; a view where a given grid or level doesn't appear at all (wrong crop, wrong phase) is marked **not visible in this view — skip** rather than blocked outright, since that is a legitimate, common state rather than an error.
3. **Match & resolve per (view, datum) pair** — grids matched by name (the same identity `grids(doc)` already keys on) and levels the same way via `levels(doc)`'s name field; each pair resolves to **will copy extent**, **will copy bubble state**, **already matches — skip**, or **not visible — skip**, shown as a live preview table before anything is touched, the same "resolve, preview, tick, then write" shape `ConvertFloor.pushbutton` and `AutoLevel.pushbutton` already use.
4. **Two independently-toggleable operations** — extent copy and bubble-state copy can be run together or separately (a modeler who only wants bubbles fixed shouldn't be forced to also re-trim every extent, and vice versa), each with its own column in the preview grid.
5. **Apply** — one `Transaction`; for each ticked pair, `SetDatumExtentType(end, target_view, DatumExtentType.ViewSpecific)` then `SetCurveInView(DatumExtentType.ViewSpecific, target_view, ref_curve)` copies the extent, and `ShowBubbleInView`/`HideBubbleInView` per end copies the bubble state; one failing pair (a locked view, a view template that overrides datum visibility) rolls back only that pair, the rest of the run continues, the same per-element rollback posture every conversion/QA tool in the repo already uses.
6. **Report** — copied / already-matched / skipped-not-visible / failed counts per view, exportable via the same `openpyxl` path `Export Schedule.pushbutton`/`BBS Generator.pushbutton` already use, so a "grids tidied across 11 views" line exists for a coordination hand-off the same way other batch tools already produce one.

**Implementation:**
- Grid enumeration and identity go straight through `anongee_toolkit.structural.grids.grids(doc)`/`segment_mm(grid)` (`grids.py:25-53`), promoting that module from an RC-Automation-only footing-placement helper to its first direct, interactive caller; level enumeration reuses `anongee_toolkit.structural.levels.levels(doc)` (`levels.py:19-28`) the same way `AutoLevel.pushbutton` and the already-logged Column Stack Auditor/Sheet Set Builder ideas already do.
- New `anongee_toolkit.revit.datum_views` module — the one genuinely new piece, since no per-view datum-override API (`SetDatumExtentType`/`SetCurveInView`/`GetCurvesInView`/`ShowBubbleInView`/`HideBubbleInView`/`IsBubbleVisibleInView`) exists anywhere in the toolkit today: a read function returning a `Grid`/`Level`'s per-view extent curve and per-end bubble state, and a write function applying a captured state to a target view, both operating on the `DatumPlane` base class so `Grid` and `Level` share one implementation rather than two near-duplicate ones.
- View enumeration for the target checklist reuses `anongee_toolkit.revit.views.get_eligible_views()` (`revit/views.py:30-56`) — filtered to plans/sections/elevations, the same helper the already-logged Sheet Set Builder idea proposes promoting from its current sole caller (its own `API.md` usage example) to real production use; this tool becomes the second caller.
- `grid_level_sync_config.json` alongside `script.py` (whether Levels are in scope by default alongside Grids, whether extent-copy and bubble-copy default on or off independently, name-matching case sensitivity), mirroring `slab_convert_config.json`'s override-by-config shape.
- Same modeless window shell (`anongee_toolkit.ui.hostwnd`/`progressbar`/`checklist`/`theme`) every other multi-step tool in the repo already runs on; Excel report reuses the `openpyxl` path already proven in `Export Schedule.pushbutton`/`BBS Generator.pushbutton`.
- Sits in `Essential.panel/StrucBIM.stack/`, next to `RoundElementDist.pushbutton`, following the same `bundle.yaml`/`README.md`/`CHANGELOG.md` layout the root README documents for versioned tools.

**Future update:**
- Rule-based mode alongside the reference-view copy: a configurable padding beyond the model's own bounding box per view, and a per-axis-family bubble-end assignment (numbered grids bubble one side, lettered grids another) reusing the same direction-family clustering the already-logged Grid Mark Assistant idea proposes promoting into `anongee_toolkit.geometry.lines` — so a project with no "reference view" to copy from yet can still be normalized in one pass.
- Designate one view as the enforced master and flag drift on a later re-run: a grid added since the last sync that was never propagated, or a target view whose extent was hand-edited after the last run and now disagrees with the reference again.
- Feed a grid or level missing from a target view's Visibility/Graphics (rather than merely cropped out) into the already-logged Warning Auditor's report-only category list, since that is a visibility-settings gap neither tool currently checks.
- Extend past Grids/Levels to Reference Planes used as informal grid lines, once the straight-line-only Grid case (matching `segment_mm`'s own curved-grid refusal) has proven the sync pipeline in production.

## 2026-09-14 15:39 UTC

**Idea name:** Lap & Starter-Bar Continuity Auditor — cross-storey rebar lap-splice and starter-bar continuity check for placed column reinforcement

**Potential:**
Every RCC structure's column reinforcement is only as continuous as its lap splices: a lower storey's main bars project as "starters" into the storey above, the upper column's own main bars lap alongside them for a code-minimum length, and if that overlap is short, missing, or simply never modelled — a starter left stranded after a slab pour, an upper bar begun mid-air with nothing lapped beneath it — the load path the drawing implies does not exist in the model, and today nothing in this repo would ever say so. The team has already named this exact gap in its own words and left it unaddressed on purpose: `RC Automation.pushbutton/todo-list.md`'s own `SCOPE-4` (`RC Automation.pushbutton/todo-list.md:325-327`) records "laps and starter bars" as "explicitly out of scope and reported as such per column, so nobody mistakes the output for a complete cage" — a deliberate exclusion, not an oversight, and one that survives even for columns RC Automation itself places, let alone the far larger share of a project's cages a modeler builds by hand. The closest thing to a check today, `anongee_toolkit.rc_automation.validation`, only ever runs on the Excel workbook before a transaction opens — its cover/spacing/cage-fit checks explicitly disclaim being "sanity limits, not code compliance" and never touch a single placed `Rebar` element — so a modeler working outside the Excel/bridge pipeline gets none of it, and even an RC-Automation-built model only ever had its spreadsheet checked, never its final geometry. The read side already proves the enumeration works regardless of provenance: `BBS Generator.pushbutton/core/rebar_reader.py`'s `_collect_rebars()` (`rebar_reader.py:115-123`) already gathers every `OST_Rebar` element `WhereElementIsNotElementType()` across whole model/view/selection, hand-placed or not, and `_host_member_type()` (`rebar_reader.py:68-83`) already resolves each bar back to its host via `GetHostId()`. `anongee_toolkit.structural.rebar_hosts`'s elevation/origin helpers already prove the bounding-box-to-millimetre pattern this needs, just aimed at a footing/column host rather than a bar; `anongee_toolkit.structural.columns.placed(doc)` (`columns.py:279-303`) already returns every column's XY and level for stack grouping. What is missing everywhere is any comparison of one bar's geometry against its neighbour's, and `anongee_toolkit/revit/geometry.py` confirms there is no distance/overlap helper of any kind in the toolkit today, only volume and bounding-box-overlap tests. The already-logged Column Stack Auditor idea checks the concrete columns' own verticality and never opens a single rebar; this starts exactly where that stops. On the market, reading a model's own placed reinforcement and flagging short or absent lap splices exists only inside paid rebar-detailing/QA modules bundled into full BIM-coordination suites (AGACAD's RC tools, BIM One's rebar QA add-ins, Tekla-class detailing software, all priced per seat); Revit's Review Warnings never inspects lap continuity at all, and nothing free reads a `Rebar` element's own geometry against its neighbour's to say whether two bars actually overlap by the required length.

**Description:**
`Lap & Starter Auditor.pushbutton` (new tool in `Essential.panel/StrucBIM.stack`, sibling to `RoundElementDist.pushbutton` and the logged Column Stack Auditor — a QA tool in the same "check first, never write" family) runs in one window:

1. **Scope & lap rule** — whole model / active view / selection, the identical three-way scope `_collect_rebars()` already offers (`rebar_reader.py:115-123`); Structural Columns' main bars only for v1 (ties/links out of scope, matching what SCOPE-4 names); a lap-length rule expressed as a bar-diameter multiplier per condition (tension/compression), defaulting to common IS 456 detailing practice but explicitly labelled a configurable sanity threshold rather than a certified code check — the same posture `validation.py`'s own docstring already commits to for its bounds.
2. **Read every placed main bar and its host** — reuses `read_bars()`/`_collect_rebars()` (`rebar_reader.py:115-266`) filtered to bars whose `_host_member_type()` (`rebar_reader.py:68-83`) resolves to a Structural Column, keeping each bar's diameter (the same `REBAR_BAR_DIAMETER` read `rebar_types.bar_diameter_mm()` already performs) and its top/bottom elevation and plan position, extracted with the bounding-box-to-mm pattern `rebar_hosts`'s own elevation/origin helpers already prove, applied to the bar instead of the host.
3. **Build column stacks and pair bars across each joint** — host columns grouped into vertical stacks from `columns.placed(doc)`'s (`columns.py:279-303`) XY and level data, then at each storey joint every lower column's main bar is paired to the nearest upper column's main bar sharing plan position within a configurable tolerance.
4. **Classify each pairing** — **OK** (measured overlap ≥ the configured minimum for that diameter), **short lap** (overlap present but under minimum, actual vs. required shown in mm), **gap — no lap** (upper bar's bottom sits above the lower bar's top: the cage is physically discontinuous), **orphaned starter** (a lower bar with no upper bar found above it within tolerance), **orphaned continuation** (an upper bar with no lower starter beneath it).
5. **Preview checklist** (`anongee_toolkit.ui.hostwnd`/`checklist`/`progressbar`, the shell every QA tool in the repo already runs on) — one row per joint pairing: stack id, level, bar marks (reusing the same mark read `bar_mark_manager` already performs for BBS Generator), diameter, measured lap, required lap, status, with a **Locate** action isolating both bars and their host columns (`Selection.SetElementIds` + `ShowElements`).
6. **Report only** — no write path at all; exportable via the same `openpyxl` path `Export Schedule.pushbutton`/`BBS Generator.pushbutton` already use, giving a sign-off artifact naming exactly which joints need a site check or a rebar rework before pour.

**Implementation:**
- Reuses `_collect_rebars()`/`read_bars()` (`rebar_reader.py:115-266`) for enumeration and host resolution, and `_host_member_type()` (`rebar_reader.py:68-83`) to filter to column-hosted main bars — no new Rebar-collection code.
- New `anongee_toolkit.structural.rebar_continuity` module: elevation/plan-position extraction adapting the bounding-box pattern already proven in `rebar_hosts`, pointed at `Rebar` elements; the joint-pairing and classification logic is the one genuinely new piece, kept Revit-free (plain elevation/XY tuples in, a status out) so it can sit in `tests/` the way the logged Column Stack Auditor's proposed `stacking` module already plans to.
- Column-stack grouping reuses `columns.placed(doc)` (`columns.py:279-303`) directly rather than re-deriving host XY/level.
- Diameter read reuses the same `REBAR_BAR_DIAMETER` lookup `rebar_types.bar_diameter_mm()` already performs.
- `lap_audit_config.json` alongside `script.py` (lap-length multiplier per condition, XY pairing tolerance, whether ties/links are swept — off by default), mirroring `slab_convert_config.json`'s override-by-config shape.
- Same modeless window shell (`anongee_toolkit.ui.hostwnd`/`checklist`/`progressbar`/`theme`) every other QA tool in the repo runs on; Excel export reuses the `openpyxl` path already proven in `Export Schedule.pushbutton`/`BBS Generator.pushbutton`.

- Extend past columns to shear/RC wall vertical bars, paired the same way, once the column case is proven — the identical widening path the logged Column Stack Auditor proposes for its own concrete-only v1.
- Extend to horizontal lap splices in beam/slab main bars (a bar lapped mid-span rather than across a storey joint), reusing the same pairing/classification core with a horizontal rather than vertical axis.

## 2026-09-14 10:39 UTC

**Idea name:** Sheet Set Builder — batch plan-view creation, titleblock sheet placement and viewport setup, driven straight off the model's own levels

**Potential:**
Every project kickoff, and every later phase that adds a handful of levels, forces the same drawing-production grind on a BIM modeler that has nothing to do with modelling geometry at all: for each level, duplicate or create a Structural Plan (and, for a shell-and-core or MEP-coordinated project, a Foundation Plan, a Framing Plan, a Reflected Ceiling Plan), rename it to the office standard, apply the right view template, then create a sheet, pick the titleblock, drop the view on it at the standard location and scale, and number the sheet without colliding with what's already in the set — 4-8 clicks and two dialogs per view, repeated per level, per discipline, for every level in the building, and then again every time a later revision adds one more level. The repo already has one narrow, unadvertised slice of this exact job, and its own scope proves how much is still missing: `AutoLevel.pushbutton`'s `_apply_views()` (`AutoLevel.pushbutton/anongee_autolevel/revit_ops.py:473-498`) already calls `ViewPlan.Create(doc, view_type_id, level_id)` per chosen `ViewFamilyType` and applies a view template id (`revit_ops.py:483-496`) — but only ever for `report["new_ids"]` (`revit_ops.py:480`), the levels **that exact run** just created; run it again later, point it at levels that already existed before AutoLevel touched the model, or ask for a **sheet** rather than a bare view, and there is nothing there — `_apply_views()` never calls `ViewSheet.Create` or `Viewport.Create`, and grepping every `script.py` and every `anongee_toolkit` module for `ViewSheet` turns up exactly one hit in the whole repo: `anongee_toolkit.structural.inventory._views()` (`structural/inventory.py:323-359`), which only **reads** `FilteredElementCollector(doc).OfClass(ViewSheet)` — sheet number and name — to list what already exists for `Extract Template.pushbutton`'s audit report, never to create one. The other half already sits unused in plain sight: `anongee_toolkit.revit.views.get_eligible_views()`/`get_view_label()` (`revit/views.py:30-56`) already filter the model's views down to real, non-template plans/sections/elevations/3D and label them — but the only place in the repo that actually calls `get_eligible_views` today is `API.md`'s own usage example (`API.md:708-710`); no `AnonGee.tab/**/script.py` imports it, so this would be its first production caller. On the market, "create a plan view and a titled, numbered sheet for every level, for every discipline, in one batch" is exactly the headline feature of paid sheet/view-creation add-ins (CTC's Sheet & View Creator, IMAGINiT Clarity's view/sheet tools, BIM One's equivalents) sold as part of a much larger licensed suite; Revit's own UI has no batch path at all — every view and every sheet is created and numbered one at a time — and nothing free in this space reads the model's own levels and title-block library and produces the set directly.

**Description:**
`Sheet Set Builder.pushbutton` (new tool in `Essential.panel`, sibling to `AutoLevel.pushbutton` — the natural next step once a level exists, and the tool whose own view-creation code this one generalizes) runs in one window:

1. **Levels** — a checklist of every level in the model, built from `anongee_toolkit.structural.levels.levels(doc)` (`structural/levels.py:19-28`), not just the ones a prior AutoLevel run happened to create; ticked by default, with an "only levels with no matching view yet" quick filter for the common re-run case.
2. **Disciplines** — one row per configured view type (Structural Plan, Foundation Plan, Framing Plan, RCP, …): which loaded `ViewFamilyType` it maps to, its naming template, its view template (if any), and whether it also needs a sheet; ships with a sensible structural-only default and is fully editable per firm.
3. **Duplicate guard** — for every (level × discipline) pair, checks whether a matching view already exists using the same enumeration `get_eligible_views()` already proves (`revit/views.py:36-56`, promoted from documentation-only use to this tool's real, first caller) filtered to the discipline's `GenLevel` and `ViewFamilyType`; a match is marked **already exists — skip**, everything else **will create**, so re-running after a revision that adds three levels only ever touches those three.
4. **Preview checklist** (`anongee_toolkit.ui.hostwnd`/`checklist`/`progressbar`, the same shell `ConvertFloor.pushbutton` and `AutoLevel.pushbutton` already run on) — one row per (level, discipline): resolved view name, resolved sheet number (if a sheet is wanted), and status (**will create** / **already exists** / **blocked — no such ViewFamilyType loaded**); blocked rows pre-unticked and un-tickable, same posture every checklist tool in the repo already uses.
5. **Create** — one `Transaction`: `ViewPlan.Create()` per ticked row exactly as `AutoLevel.pushbutton`'s `_apply_views()` already does (`revit_ops.py:483-489`), the view template applied the same way (`revit_ops.py:490-496`); where a sheet is wanted, `ViewSheet.Create(doc, titleblock_type_id)` with the titleblock chosen from a picker seeded by `structural.inventory._views()`'s existing title-block enumeration (`structural/inventory.py:344-355`, promoted from an audit-report field to a live selectable list), numbered by the configured scheme with a collision check against that same function's existing sheet-number read (`structural/inventory.py:337-342`), then `Viewport.Create()` places the new view at the sheet's configured anchor point and the discipline's configured scale; one bad row (a titleblock family that fails to place, a duplicate sheet number the config didn't catch) rolls back only that row.
6. **Report** — created / skipped-already-exists / blocked counts per discipline, exportable via the same `openpyxl` path `Export Schedule.pushbutton` and `BBS Generator.pushbutton` already use, giving a "sheet set stood up" artifact for a kickoff hand-off the same way other tools already produce one for their own runs.

**Implementation:**
- Generalize `AutoLevel.pushbutton`'s `_apply_views()` (`revit_ops.py:473-498`) into a shared `anongee_toolkit.revit.sheet_sets` module: same `ViewPlan.Create` + view-template-apply call, but taking an explicit list of level ids (any levels, not only `report["new_ids"]`) so `AutoLevel.pushbutton` and this new tool call one function instead of the logic living inside one button's private script forever.
- New view/sheet existence check built on `get_eligible_views()` (`revit/views.py:36-56`), filtered by `view.GenLevel.Id` and `ViewType` — the first real caller of a helper that has, until now, only ever appeared in `API.md`'s own example.
- Titleblock and existing-sheet-number enumeration reuses `structural.inventory._views()`'s already-proven reads (`structural/inventory.py:323-359`) rather than a second `FilteredElementCollector(doc).OfClass(ViewSheet)` / `OfCategory(OST_TitleBlocks)` scan.
- Naming reuses the token-template formatter the already-logged Universal Renamer idea proposes (`{Discipline}`, `{Level}`, `{Index:03}`) if that tool has landed by the time this one is built; otherwise a small standalone `re`-based formatter, promoted into the shared formatter the moment Universal Renamer exists, so the two tools don't diverge on template syntax.
- `sheet_set_config.json` alongside `script.py` (per-discipline `ViewFamilyType` name, naming template, view template name, `needs_sheet` flag, default titleblock type, sheet-number scheme, viewport anchor point and scale), mirroring `slab_convert_config.json`'s override-by-config shape.
- Same modeless window shell (`anongee_toolkit.ui.hostwnd`/`checklist`/`progressbar`/`theme`) every other multi-step tool in the repo already runs on.
- Excel report reuses the `openpyxl` path already proven in `Export Schedule.pushbutton`/`BBS Generator.pushbutton`.

**Future update:**
- Fold `AutoLevel.pushbutton`'s own `_apply_views()` call over to the shared `sheet_sets` module once it is proven here, so "create views for the levels I just added" and "batch-build the whole set" stop being two independent implementations of the same call.
- Feed the "blocked — no such ViewFamilyType loaded" rows into a one-click "load this view family type now" action, closing the gap instead of only reporting it.
- Re-run/diff mode as a background health check: flag a level whose expected view or sheet was deleted since the last build, the same "re-run and diff" shape already logged for Type Matrix Generator, the Sleeve Coordinator and Grid Mark Assistant.
- Saved per-firm sheet-set presets (which disciplines, which templates, which numbering scheme), reusable project to project without re-configuring `sheet_set_config.json` by hand — the same saved-profile posture Universal Renamer's own future items propose for rename templates.

## 2026-09-14 05:39 UTC

**Idea name:** Schedule Round-Trip — push edited Excel values back into the exact Revit parameters `Export Schedule.pushbutton` only ever reads out

**Potential:**
`Export Schedule.pushbutton` already solves the hard half of getting a Revit schedule into a spreadsheet a non-Revit person can work in: `export_schedules_to_excel()` (`Export Schedule.pushbutton/script.py:455-666`) drives Revit's own `sched.Export(_TEMP_DIR, fn, opt)` (`script.py:521`) through a `ViewScheduleExportOptions` configured for tab-delimited output (`script.py:504-507`), parses the result with its own dependency-free `_parse_tsv_row`/`_read_tsv` (`script.py:172-201`), normalizes every cell's units via `_strip_unit()` (`script.py:232-275`) so `"230 mm"` becomes a real number, and even temporarily unhides every hidden field inside a `Transaction`, exports, then restores the original hidden state afterward (`script.py:509-519` unhide, `:523-528` restore) so a hidden QA column can be included without permanently exposing it in the model. But the whole pipeline runs in exactly one direction: nothing calls `openpyxl.load_workbook` to read a copy of that same export back in after a modeler hands it to a structural engineer or a quantity surveyor for review, gets 40 corrected column Marks, 200 revised rebar "ITEM_V" details, or a QS's bulk-corrected footing sizes back, and then has to manually retype every one of those changed cells into Revit one element at a time — the exact same one-way ceiling `Parameter Migrator`'s own writeup (already logged above) works around for parameter-to-parameter moves, but never for "values a person edited outside Revit." Revit's own native schedule export has no import counterpart at all through the UI, and the team has already named this exact class of gap once, on the export side: `todo-list.md:1018`'s `FEAT-44` records the owner's own words — *"Revit's native schedule will not take every parameter an element has... generate the table from the model — read every parameter, including the ones a native schedule refuses, and write it"* — which is about getting more data **out**, not about getting corrected data safely back **in**, so the write-back gap this idea targets survives even after FEAT-44 ships. The read-a-spreadsheet-into-Revit half exists in the codebase, but for a completely different job: `anongee_toolkit/rc_automation/excel_engine.py:444` (`read_grid`) already opens a workbook via `openpyxl.load_workbook` (`excel_engine.py:494,504`) — the only other `openpyxl` read anywhere in the repo (confirmed: no `AnonGee.tab/**/script.py` outside `Export Schedule.pushbutton` and `CPython3 engine.pushbutton` even imports `openpyxl`) — but it is built to parse RC Automation's own fixed placement-schedule format (`#SHEET,<name>` markers, per `rc_automation/excel_engine.py`'s own `done-list.md:27-29`), not to diff an arbitrary edited schedule export against live model parameters. The one real technical wall neither tool has to cross today: `sched.Export()`'s TSV output (`script.py:521`) carries no `ElementId` column unless the schedule itself happens to already have one configured, so an edited row cannot be matched back to the right element without one — but `Export Schedule.pushbutton` already proves, in its own `include_hidden` toggle, the exact "temporarily mutate the schedule's field list, export, put it back" mechanism this needs one field further. On the market, this exact capability — edit a Revit schedule in Excel, push the edits back into the model — is sold as the flagship, per-seat-licensed feature of Ideate BIMLink, Excelerator BIM's Sync tools, and similar "push/pull" add-ins; Revit itself has no native import path for a schedule at all, and nothing free replicates it.

**Description:**
`Schedule Round-Trip.pushbutton` (new tool in `Essential.panel/Action.stack`, sibling to `Export Schedule.pushbutton`, reusing its dialog shell and export engine rather than re-implementing them) runs in two linked modes from one window:

1. **Prepare for edit** — reuses `Export Schedule.pushbutton`'s own schedule-picker list and search box (`ExportScheduleDialog._populate`/`_on_search_changed`, `script.py:329-344, 354-359`) to pick one or more schedules, then runs the same export pipeline (`export_schedules_to_excel`, `script.py:455-666`) with one addition: before exporting, for any schedule that doesn't already carry one, it temporarily adds a scheduled "Element Id" field and marks it hidden, using the identical `Transaction`-wrapped toggle `include_hidden` already performs at `script.py:509-519` (add/unhide → export → `script.py:523-528`-style restore, extended to remove the added field afterward instead of just re-hiding it) — so the round-trip workbook looks exactly like a normal `Export Schedule.pushbutton` output plus one extra key column, styled with the same `_BR_*` brand constants (`script.py:147-168`) so it visibly belongs to this tool.
2. **Edit outside Revit** — the modeler (or the engineer/QS they hand it to) edits any writable cell in the `.xlsx`, leaving the Element Id column untouched; no Revit session needs to stay open.
3. **Import & diff** — pick the edited workbook back in the tool; it loads it via the same bundled-runtime guard `Export Schedule.pushbutton` already proves (`_ensure_bundled_openpyxl()`, `script.py:91-118`), resolves each row's Element Id to a live element via `doc.GetElement`, and for every other column resolves the schedule field back to its real `Parameter` on that element (the one genuinely new piece: matching a `ScheduleField` to a writable `Parameter`, falling back through the same `BuiltInParameter → shared GUID → name` order `ConvertFloor.pushbutton`'s `find_target_param()` already uses, `ConvertFloor.pushbutton/script.py:866-886`), then compares the cell's value against the parameter's current value via `anongee_toolkit.revit.parameters.get_parameter_value` (`revit/parameters.py:36-82`).
4. **Preview checklist** (`anongee_toolkit.ui.hostwnd`/`checklist`/`progressbar`, the same shell `ConvertFloor.pushbutton` and `Parameter Migrator`'s proposed tool already run on) — one row per **changed cell**, not per exported row, showing element, schedule column, old value, new value, and status: **will write** / **unchanged** / **blocked — read-only parameter** / **blocked — element deleted since export** / **blocked — value/type mismatch**; blocked rows pre-unticked and un-tickable, same posture every other checklist tool in the repo uses.
5. **Apply** — one `Transaction`, writing every ticked change via `anongee_toolkit.revit.parameters.set_parameter_value` (`revit/parameters.py:85-113`), wrapped so one bad write doesn't roll back changes already committed for other elements in the same run.
6. **Report** — changed / unchanged / blocked counts per schedule, exportable via the same `openpyxl` write path `export_schedules_to_excel` already proves, so the round-trip leaves the same kind of audit artifact every other Excel-driven tool in the repo produces.

**Implementation:**
- Promote the field-hide/unhide toggle pattern already proven at `Export Schedule.pushbutton/script.py:509-519, 523-528` into a small shared helper in a new `anongee_toolkit.revit.schedule_io` module, extended to temporarily **add** a scheduled Element Id field rather than only toggle `IsHidden` on existing ones — the module's only genuinely new Revit API surface, since nothing in the repo currently adds or reads a schedule's field-to-parameter mapping.
- Field → parameter resolution in the same module reuses `ConvertFloor.pushbutton`'s exact matching order from `find_target_param()` (`ConvertFloor.pushbutton/script.py:866-886`: `BuiltInParameter` first, shared `GUID` second, name lookup last) rather than inventing a second matching convention.
- Read/write of individual parameter values goes through `anongee_toolkit.revit.parameters.get_parameter_value`/`set_parameter_value` (`revit/parameters.py:36-82, 85-113`) exactly as `Parameter Migrator`'s proposed tool above plans to be the first direct caller of that module — this tool becomes the second, sharing one read/write engine instead of a third bespoke parameter-setter.
- Excel read reuses `_ensure_bundled_openpyxl()` (`Export Schedule.pushbutton/script.py:91-118`) for the runtime guard, and the export half reuses `export_schedules_to_excel()`'s brand styling constants (`script.py:147-168`) and `_strip_unit()` (`script.py:232-275`) unchanged, so a round-trip workbook is visually and numerically identical to a normal export.
- `schedule_roundtrip_config.json` alongside `script.py` (which categories/fields are writable by default, conflict policy when the model value changed since export as well as the spreadsheet cell, Element Id column name/position), mirroring `slab_convert_config.json`'s override-by-config shape.
- Same modeless window shell (`anongee_toolkit.ui.hostwnd`/`checklist`/`progressbar`/`theme`) every other multi-step tool in the repo runs on.
- Sits in `Essential.panel/Action.stack/`, next to `Export Schedule.pushbutton`, its natural sibling and the tool it forks its export half from.

**Future update:**
- Lock the Element Id column with openpyxl cell/sheet protection so it survives a careless edit or column reorder in Excel, rather than only documenting "don't touch this column" in the UI.
- Feed "blocked — value/type mismatch" rows into `anongee_toolkit.revit.units` (`strip_unit`/`mm_to_ft`/`ft_to_mm`), the same module `Parameter Migrator`'s proposed tool already plans to reuse, so a QS's `"230mm"` typed into a numeric cell parses cleanly instead of blocking.
- Two-way live sync mode, matching the sync posture already proposed as a future item for `Type Matrix Generator`: re-import the same workbook after a later revision and diff again, rather than requiring a fresh export/edit/import cycle each time.
- Multi-schedule batch import (drop a folder of edited workbooks, one per discipline or trade, and reconcile all of them in one run) for firms that split schedule review across multiple reviewers in parallel.

## 2026-09-14 00:39 UTC

**Idea name:** Dimension Sweep — whole-view, chained dimension-string batching built directly on AutoDIM's own per-element engine

**Potential:**
`AutoDIM.pushbutton` already solves the hard geometry problem of reading an element's own extents along whichever of its axes lie in the current view plane (`element_frame`/`planar_faces`/`extreme_faces`/`extent_along`, `script.py:69-143`) and placing a clean, correctly-offset dimension off them (`dimension_element`, `script.py:183-237`), but `main()` only ever acts on `revit.get_selection()` (`script.py:312`) — a modeler has to manually multi-select every column, wall and beam that needs a dimension, run the tool, then reselect the next group and run it again, bay by bay and row by row, for the same 40-150-element plan the already-logged Bay Fill idea calls out for slab placement — the identical "click through every instance by hand" tax, just spent on annotation instead of modeling. Worse, every dimension `AutoDIM` places is scoped to exactly what got selected: `dimension_element()` draws one float dimension across a single element's own faces, and `dimension_datums()` (`script.py:254-302`) chains a dimension between whatever 2+ grids/levels happen to be in that one selection — neither ever builds the object a structural plan actually needs, a single continuous dimension **string** referencing every column centerline along one shared grid line in one `ReferenceArray`, ordered end to end; that requires re-selecting and re-running per row today, and nothing assembles the row automatically. `RoundElementDist.pushbutton` already proves the missing piece exists half-built next door — `build_direction_groups(elements, tol)` (`script.py:169-201`) already clusters walls/columns into rows sharing one axis/angle, and `place_dimension()` (`script.py:683-701`) already builds a `ReferenceArray` + `doc.Create.NewDimension` off it — but only ever between exactly **two** elements (a datum and one target), for its own "does this element sit where the datum says" QA job; it stops one reference short of chaining a whole row. And neither tool checks what is already on the sheet: both `dimension_element()`'s and `dimension_datums()`'s `doc.Create.NewDimension` calls (`script.py:232`, `script.py:299`) fire unconditionally, so re-running `AutoDIM` on a row after a later revision (two columns added, one resized) stacks a second dimension line under the first instead of updating it, leaving exactly the kind of duplicate annotation clutter the already-logged Warning Auditor idea's "exact-duplicate instances" fix category exists to catch for model elements — but that idea's `doc.GetWarnings()` scan never looks at `Dimension` elements at all. On the market, "select everything, one dimension string per row, auto" is sold only as a bundled feature inside paid drafting-automation add-ins (CTC's dimensioning tools, BIM One's auto-dimension utilities, assorted Autodesk App Store add-ins priced per seat); Revit's own multi-pick dimension tool still requires picking every reference by hand, and nothing free reads a plan's own column/wall/framing rows and grid lines and strings them in one batched pass.

**Description:**
`Dimension Sweep.pushbutton` (new tool in `Essential.panel/Action.stack`, sibling to `AutoDIM.pushbutton` — this calls AutoDIM's own engine rather than re-implementing it) runs in one window:

1. **Scope & categories** — active view only (dimensions are view-specific, same constraint `AutoDIM` already enforces via `_NO_DIM_VIEWS`, `script.py:34-37`), tick which categories to sweep: Structural Columns, Structural Framing, Walls, and/or Grids/Levels — no selection needed, the whole point being to replace `revit.get_selection()` with a full collector.
2. **Cluster into rows** — every ticked element collected via `FilteredElementCollector(doc, active_view.Id)`, then bucketed by shared axis/angle using `build_direction_groups()` lifted out of `RoundElementDist.pushbutton` (same clustering, second caller — the same "one function, two callers" promotion the already-logged Grid Mark Assistant idea proposes for that tool's `_distance_to_line`); grids/levels cluster the same way `dimension_datums()` already groups a manual selection, just run across every datum in the view instead of only a chosen few.
3. **Build one chained string per row** — the genuinely new piece: for a column/wall/framing row, every member's extreme-face reference (from `AutoDIM`'s own `planar_faces()`/`extreme_faces()`, unmodified) is collected into one `ReferenceArray` ordered by position along the row's axis and fed to a single `doc.Create.NewDimension` — one continuous string, not `dimension_element()`'s N isolated per-element floats; a grid/level row calls `dimension_datums()` exactly as it exists today, unchanged.
4. **Duplicate-run guard** — the other genuinely new piece neither source tool has: before building anything, `FilteredElementCollector(doc, active_view.Id).OfClass(DB.Dimension)` is read and each existing dimension's `.References` compared against a row's computed reference set; a row whose references are already fully covered by one existing dimension is marked **already dimensioned** and skipped by default, and a row that's a superset of an existing dimension's references (new columns added to an existing row since the last sweep) is marked **row changed — will replace**, so a re-run after a revision only ever adds or refreshes what actually changed instead of stacking a second string under the first.
5. **Preview checklist** (`anongee_toolkit.ui.hostwnd`/`checklist`/`progressbar`, the same shell `ConvertFloor.pushbutton` and `AutoDIM`'s proposed siblings already run on) — one row per detected chain: category, member count, axis, and status (**will place** / **already dimensioned** / **row changed — will replace** / **blocked**, with the exact block reason `dimension_element()` already returns — "no planar faces", "no element axis lies in the view plane", etc., `script.py:186-194` — surfaced per member instead of silently dropped), plus a **Locate** action per row that isolates the row's members in the view.
6. **Place** — one `Transaction`; a "row changed" row deletes its stale dimension (`doc.Delete`) before creating the replacement, everything else creates fresh; one bad row's reference failure rolls back only that row, the rest of the sweep continues.
7. **Report** — placed / replaced / skipped-already-dimensioned / blocked counts, with the block reasons grouped the same way `AutoDIM`'s own `main()` already prints per-note today (`script.py:338-339`), just rolled into one run summary instead of one console line per manual selection.

**Implementation:**
- Promote `AutoDIM.pushbutton`'s `element_frame`, `planar_faces`, `extreme_faces`, `extent_along`, `classify` and `dimension_element` (`script.py:69-237`) into a shared `anongee_toolkit.revit.dimensioning` module so both the existing single-selection tool and this new sweep call one copy instead of a second implementation of the same face-reading math; `AutoDIM.pushbutton/script.py` becomes a thin caller of the promoted module, behaviour unchanged.
- Promote `RoundElementDist.pushbutton`'s `build_direction_groups()` (`script.py:169-201`) the same way, into `anongee_toolkit.revit.clustering`, so this tool, `RoundElementDist`, and the already-logged Grid Mark Assistant idea's grid-family clustering all import one function.
- New chain-builder function (the one piece with no existing equivalent): takes a row's members, orders them by their `bbox_center()` projection onto the row's shared axis (reusing `AutoDIM`'s existing `bbox_center()`, `script.py:146-150`), pulls each member's extreme-face `Reference` on that axis, and appends them into one `DB.ReferenceArray` in order before the single `NewDimension` call.
- Duplicate-guard reads `Dimension.References` off `FilteredElementCollector(doc, active_view.Id).OfClass(DB.Dimension)` and compares `ElementId`/`LinkElementId` sets against each candidate row's resolved reference set — no new Revit API surface, just a read nothing in the repo currently does against annotation elements.
- `dimension_sweep_config.json` alongside `script.py` (which categories default-ticked, row-changed replace-vs-skip default, minimum members per row to bother stringing), mirroring `slab_convert_config.json`'s override-by-config shape.
- Same modeless window shell (`anongee_toolkit.ui.hostwnd` / `progressbar` / `checklist` / `theme`) every other multi-step tool in the repo already runs on.

**Future update:**
- Fold back into `AutoDIM.pushbutton` itself as a mode toggle ("Dimension selection" vs. "Sweep view") once the shared `dimensioning`/`clustering` modules have two independent, proven callers — the same fold-back posture the already-logged Bay Fill idea proposes for `DrawFloor.pushbutton`.
- Two-tier stacking: an overall dimension string beneath each per-bay row showing the cumulative end-to-end length, matching the two-line dimension stack convention a real structural plan is drawn with, rather than the single string v1 produces.
- Multi-view batch mode: run the sweep across every plan view in a chosen view set (one pass per level) instead of the active view only, for a modeler dimensioning an entire building's structural plans in one sitting.
- Feed "row changed — will replace" counts into the already-logged Warning Auditor's proposed trend CSV, so a weekly model-health snapshot can show how much dimension rework a revision caused, the same axis that idea's own future items propose for warning counts.

## 2026-09-13 15:39 UTC

**Idea name:** Type Consolidator — find every duplicate/near-duplicate type sharing one identical structure, merge every instance onto one canonical type, purge the rest

**Potential:**
Every model that gets copy-pasted between projects, linked in from a consultant, or built by more than one modeler ends up with the same wall or floor type created twice under two different names — `200mm RCC Wall` and `WALL - 200 RCC` with byte-identical compound structures, or a slab type re-created after a paste-align instead of picked from the Type Selector — and both stick around because both are *in use*, so Revit's own `Purge Unused` (which only drops types with zero instances) never touches either one. The result compounds every day after: the Type Selector for every category grows a second, third, fourth near-duplicate entry a modeler has to visually diff before picking the right one, schedules split one real assembly into two rows, and the file carries the weight of both. Nobody in this repo currently reads the model for this: `ConvertFloor.pushbutton` already builds the exact signature this needs — `cs_signature(ftype)` (`ConvertFloor.pushbutton/script.py:704-716`) reduces a `CompoundStructure` to a tuple of `(layer width, material id, layer function)` per layer, and `resolve_type()`'s `"identical layers"` branch (`script.py:756-759`) already uses that signature to treat two same-structure types as interchangeable — but only to pick a *conversion target* for one element at a time inside one run, never to sweep the whole model and report every group of types that collapses to the same signature. `anongee_toolkit.structural.inventory.gather()` (`anongee_toolkit/structural/inventory.py:33-48`) already walks every category and family in the model for the template-audit report `Extract Template.pushbutton` produces, so the "what types exist" enumeration exists too, just never grouped by structural identity or fed a merge action. The instance-reassignment half is proven elsewhere in the repo under a different name: `structural/stairs.py:224,339` and `cad2bim/builders/stairs.py:211` already call `element.ChangeTypeId(new_type_id)` to swap a placed instance onto a different type/symbol id, and `operations/bulk_delete.py`'s `RevitTransaction`-wrapped `GenericBulkDeleteDialog` (`operations/bulk_delete.py:90,222`) already proves the "collect candidates, tick which to remove, one transaction" shape for exactly the case of deleting now-orphaned style/pattern elements. Nothing wires these three proven pieces — structure signature, model-wide grouping, reassign-then-delete — into one tool. On the market this is sold only as "purge / clean-up" line items inside paid whole-model housekeeping add-ins (Ideate Sidekick's "Purge" and BIM One's "eTools"-class utilities, both licensed per seat for a much larger bundle); the free option is Revit's own Purge Unused, which by definition cannot see a duplicate that is still in use, and no free tool groups in-use types by identical structure and reassigns instances before deleting.

**Description:**
`Type Consolidator.pushbutton` (new tool in `Essential.panel/Bulk Tool.stack`, alongside `Bulk Delete.pulldown` / `Bulk Rename.pulldown` — a housekeeping tool in the same family, one step past what either already does) runs in one window:

1. **Scope** — pick one or more system-family categories to scan (Walls, Floors, Roofs, Structural Foundations first, since all four expose `GetCompoundStructure()`; loadable-family categories — Structural Columns, Framing, doors/windows — follow in a second pass, matched on identical instance/type parameter values instead of a compound structure, via the same `get_parameter_value` sweep `Parameter Migrator`'s proposed tool above already plans to promote from `anongee_toolkit.revit.parameters`).
2. **Group by identity** — every type in scope reduced to `cs_signature()` (promoted out of `ConvertFloor.pushbutton` into a shared `anongee_toolkit.revit.types` module rather than re-implemented), types sharing one signature clustered together; each cluster ranked to propose a **canonical type** (most-used by instance count, ties broken by oldest `ElementId` so the type more likely referenced by a firm standard wins over a stray paste-in).
3. **Preview checklist** — one row per non-canonical type in a cluster: its name, instance count, the canonical type it would merge into, and a **parameter-loss** flag if any writable instance parameter values on its instances don't already match what the canonical type's own instances carry (reusing the "list what doesn't survive" posture `ConvertFloor.pushbutton`'s own checklist already uses) — so a modeler can untick a type that only *looks* identical in structure but is tracked separately on purpose (a firm sometimes keeps twin types for scheduling reasons), rather than the tool silently assuming structure equality means intent equality.
4. **Merge** — one `TransactionGroup`, per ticked type: every instance's `ChangeTypeId()` (the same call `structural/stairs.py` and `cad2bim/builders/stairs.py` already make) repointed at the cluster's canonical type id, then the now-orphaned type deleted with `doc.Delete(type_id)` inside the same `RevitTransaction` shape `operations/bulk_delete.py`'s `GenericBulkDeleteDialog` already runs, so one type's instances failing to reassign (locked, in a group, in a linked/nested family) rolls back only that type and the rest of the merge continues.
5. **Report** — clusters merged / instances repointed / types deleted / skipped-parameter-mismatch, exportable via the same `openpyxl` path `Export Schedule.pushbutton` and `BBS Generator.pushbutton` already use, so a "before/after Type Selector count" line exists for a handover or a file-size review.

**Implementation:**
- Promote `cs_signature()` and the `"identical layers"` matching it already backs (`ConvertFloor.pushbutton/script.py:704-759`) into a new `anongee_toolkit.revit.types` module, so both `ConvertFloor.pushbutton`'s single-element conversion lookup and this tool's whole-model grouping call one shared function instead of a second copy.
- Loadable-family matching (columns/framing/doors/windows, no `CompoundStructure` to read) reuses `anongee_toolkit.revit.parameters.get_parameter_value` (`revit/parameters.py:36`) across every writable type parameter to build an equivalent signature tuple, keyed by family name + parameter values rather than compound layers.
- Instance discovery per type is `FilteredElementCollector(doc).OfCategoryId(cat_id).WhereElementIsNotElementType()` filtered by `element.GetTypeId() == type_id`, no new API surface.
- Reassignment is the existing `ChangeTypeId()` call already proven at `structural/stairs.py:224,339` and `cad2bim/builders/stairs.py:211`; deletion afterward follows `operations/bulk_delete.py`'s own `RevitTransaction`-per-item shape (`operations/bulk_delete.py:222`) rather than a new transaction pattern.
- `type_consolidator_config.json` alongside `script.py` (which categories are in scope by default, whether a parameter-mismatch row blocks the merge or just warns, canonical-type tie-break rule), mirroring `slab_convert_config.json`'s override-by-config shape.
- Same modeless window shell (`anongee_toolkit.ui.hostwnd` / `progressbar` / `checklist` / `theme`) every other multi-step tool in the repo already runs on.
- Excel report reuses the `openpyxl` path already proven in `Export Schedule.pushbutton` / `BBS Generator.pushbutton`.

**Future update:**
- Fold in `anongee_toolkit.structural.inventory.gather()`'s existing per-category enumeration (`structural/inventory.py:33-48`) so a consolidation run doubles as an update to the same template-audit report `Extract Template.pushbutton` already produces — "N duplicate types found" becomes one more line in a model-health snapshot.
- A "near-miss" tier below exact signature match — same layer count and materials, thickness within the same `thickness_tolerance_mm` convention `ConvertFloor.pushbutton`'s config already uses — surfaced as **review, not auto-grouped**, for the case of two types one millimetre apart that were never meant to be the same.
- Cross-project mode: run the same signature grouping against a firm's shared type-catalogue project and flag which in-project types already match a standard type by structure, closing the loop from "duplicate found" to "replaced with the firm-standard type," not just "collapsed to whichever instance count won."
- Feed the merge report's "instances repointed" list into `Warning Auditor`'s duplicate-Mark category once both exist, since a merge that repoints two previously distinct types onto one can surface a Mark collision that was invisible while the types were still separate.
