## 2026-09-13 10:45 UTC

**Idea name:** Bay Fill — one-pass batch slab placement across every enclosed structural bay `DrawFloor.pushbutton` already finds but only lets you click one at a time

**Potential:**
`DrawFloor.pushbutton` already solves the hard geometry problem of "find every enclosed region bounded by beams and columns in this plan" — it reads exact footprints, splits them into a planar graph and traces closed cycles into candidate faces (`build_graph`/`trace_faces`/`FaceIndex`, `DrawFloor.pushbutton/script.py:674-806`), then filters them down to a real, already-computed list of usable regions (`usable = [f for f in faces if abs(signed_area(points, f)) >= MIN_FACE_AREA]`, `script.py:1255`) before the user ever touches the mouse. But `main()`'s only way to turn a face into a slab is `draw_session()`'s `while True: uidoc.Selection.PickPoint(...)` loop (`script.py:1306-1397`), which places exactly one `Floor.Create` per click (`script.py:1368`) and never walks the `usable` list it already built end-to-end — a modeler filling a podium or a typical floor plate with 40–150 structural bays clicks every single one by hand, waits for the pick prompt each time, and gets no indication of which bays remain. Worse, nothing in the file checks whether a bay already has a floor in it (no `OST_Floors` collector anywhere in `script.py` besides the type-picker at `script.py:1106`), so re-running the tool after a later revision adds a few new beams means either re-clicking only the new bays from memory or risking a stacked duplicate floor in a bay already filled. This is exactly the "type generator"/batch-modeling chore the team already funds elsewhere in the toolkit (`AutoDIM.pushbutton` has the identical gap — it dimensions only the current selection, never every column or grid-bay in a view in one run) applied to the single most repetitive slab-modeling task a structural BIM modeler does. On the market, "create floor by enclosed boundary" exists only as Revit's own native `Create Floor > Pick Walls`/mass-face tools (which need a wall loop or a modeled mass face, not beam/column outlines, and place one floor per manual sketch anyway) or as one feature bundled inside paid whole-project automation suites (AGACAD, CTC); nothing free reads real beam/column geometry and fills every enclosed bay in a view in a single confirmed batch.

**Description:**
`Bay Fill.pushbutton` (new tool in `Essential.panel/StrucBIM.stack`, sibling to `DrawFloor.pushbutton`, reusing its scan pipeline rather than re-implementing it) runs in one window:

1. **Scan** — calls `DrawFloor`'s own `build_regions(view)` (`script.py:1218-1299`) to get the full, already-cached `usable` face list for the active view — same session cache (`CACHE_KEY`/`signature`/`load_cached`/`store_cached`, `script.py:168,1177-1211`) and the same SHIFT-click-to-force-rescan convention, so switching between DrawFloor's click mode and this batch mode in one session never forces a redundant re-scan.
2. **Guard against duplicates** — the one genuinely new check: collect every placed `OST_Floors` (or `OST_StructuralFoundation`) element already in the model and test each existing floor's boundary against every candidate face's centroid using the same `point_in_cycle` DrawFloor already has (`script.py:757-767`); a face already covered is marked **already filled** and excluded by default, so a re-run after a revision only ever offers newly-enclosed bays.
3. **Preview checklist** (`anongee_toolkit.ui.checklist`/`progressbar`, the shell already proven on `ConvertFloor.pushbutton`, in place of DrawFloor's plain `output.print_md` console log) — one row per face: area, centroid, curve/arc count (from `build_loop`, `script.py:895-936`), and a status — **will place** / **already filled** / **too small** (below a configurable minimum, catching a sliver face nobody wants a slab in) / **boundary failed** (the same "loop build failed" case DrawFloor already reports at `script.py:1352-1360`) — with a **Locate** action per row that isolates the face's centroid in the view.
4. **One type per run** — matches DrawFloor's own one-floor-type-per-round convention exactly; a bay needing a different thickness is just run again with the leftover rows re-ticked, the same way DrawFloor's own ESC-to-change-type loop already works.
5. **Place** — one `Transaction`, one `Floor.Create` per ticked face (the identical call DrawFloor already makes at `script.py:1368`, including setting `FLOOR_PARAM_IS_STRUCTURAL`), wrapped so one bad boundary doesn't roll back bays already placed in the same run.
6. **Report** — placed / skipped-already-filled / skipped-too-small / failed counts, the same information DrawFloor already prints per click, rolled into one run summary instead of one console line per pick.

**Implementation:**
- No new geometry code: lift the scan/graph pipeline DrawFloor already owns (`SegmentGrid` through `build_regions`, `script.py:538-1299`, plus `point_in_cycle`/`build_loop`/`FaceIndex`) out of `DrawFloor.pushbutton/script.py` into a shared `anongee_toolkit.structural.bay_graph` module, so both DrawFloor's existing click-mode and this new batch mode import one copy instead of a second tool depending on logic still owned by the first tool's private script.
- New duplicate-guard is the only genuinely new function: `FilteredElementCollector(doc).OfCategory(BuiltInCategory.OST_Floors).WhereElementIsNotElementType()`, each existing floor's boundary curve loop tested against each candidate face's centroid via the lifted `point_in_cycle`.
- `bay_fill_config.json` alongside `script.py` (minimum face area, whether a "boundary failed" row blocks the whole run or is skipped-and-continued, which categories count as "already filled" for the duplicate guard — Floors only, or Floors + Foundations), mirroring `slab_convert_config.json`'s override-by-config shape.
- Same modeless window shell (`anongee_toolkit.ui.hostwnd`/`checklist`/`progressbar`/`theme`) the already-logged ideas in this file standardize on, rather than DrawFloor's current plain pyRevit `forms`/console output, since a 40–150-row run needs a scrollable tick-list, not a scrolling log.

**Future update:**
- Fold back into `DrawFloor.pushbutton` itself as a mode toggle in its existing type-picker dialog ("Click bays" vs. "Fill all bays") once the shared `bay_graph` module has two independent, proven callers, so a modeler doesn't need to remember which of two buttons to press for which case.
- Per-bay type inference from the framing depth around each face (reusing the extent-reading helpers `AutoDIM.pushbutton` already has at `script.py:70-237`) so a thicker slab bay under deeper beams is proposed automatically instead of requiring a second manual run.
- Extend the duplicate guard into a stale-bay report: a placed floor whose boundary no longer matches any current usable face (a beam moved after the fill) flagged for review, closing the loop DrawFloor's own geometry signature almost tracks already.
- Apply the same batch-fill pattern to Structural Foundations once proven for Floors, reusing `ConvertFloor.pushbutton`'s existing Floor⇄Foundation type-resolution logic to decide which target category a given fill run should produce.

## 2026-09-13 05:39 UTC

**Idea name:** Column Stack Auditor — cross-level verticality, continuity and rotation-drift audit for structural columns

**Potential:**
Every RCC structure over a few storeys lives or dies on one invariant nobody in this repo currently checks against the model itself: a column at a given grid position has to actually stand on the column below it, storey after storey, or the load path it is drawn to carry does not exist. In practice a stack breaks in ways a modeler cannot see just by looking at a plan view: a paste-aligned floor with one instance nudged before or after the paste, or an architectural coordination update that shifted a grid without the structural columns following it exactly, drops a column a few centimetres off from the one below it; a floor plan copied and then had one instance deleted, or added later without cross-checking the level below, leaves a level with no column where the stack implies one must be; and a level's top level/offset does not actually land where the level above's base level/offset starts, leaving a modeled gap or an invisible overlap that is not caught until a schedule/quantity discrepancy or a site RFI surfaces it. None of this is hypothetical for this project: `todo-list.md`'s own FEAT-28 (`todo-list.md:1036`) records the owner's explicit rule that a column's `Mark` is a stack label that must hold "grid position plus storey" together — "a column at A1 marked `C1` is `C1` all the way up" — which is exactly the invariant this checks, and FEAT-10 (`todo-list.md:1040`) records that `columns.sync` already matches Revit columns to a spreadsheet plan "on position and storey" for RC Automation's own bridge-driven placement, but only against a spreadsheet a modeler must first build by hand; there is no live-model, no-spreadsheet-needed check that a real column stack, however it got modelled, is actually continuous, unmoved and un-rotated top to bottom. The read layer to do this already exists but has never been exposed to a button: `anongee_toolkit.structural.columns.placed(doc)` (`anongee_toolkit/structural/columns.py:279-303`) already walks every `OST_StructuralColumns` instance and returns `(ElementId, (x, y), level_name, type_name)` — built for RC Automation's internal reconciliation against a workbook, never surfaced for a modeler to run stand-alone — and `anongee_toolkit.structural.levels.levels(doc)` (`anongee_toolkit/structural/levels.py:19-28`) already returns every level sorted bottom-to-top by elevation. `Rotate Column.pushbutton/script.py` already reads a column's `DB.LocationPoint` and its `.Rotation` to spin columns by a calculated step (`script.py:43`), so the rotation-read half of this exists too, just wired to a different job. Nothing clusters these into vertical stacks and walks them for continuity, offset or rotation drift. On the market, "column verticality/continuity checking" is sold only as one line item bundled inside paid whole-model QA/clash suites (Naviate/Symetri's Model Checker family, BIM One's structural QA tools, Revizto's rule checks) that charge per seat for a whole toolset a modeler does not need the rest of; nothing free reads Revit's own structural columns and reports "this stack breaks at Level 6" on its own.

**Description:**
`Column Stack Auditor.pushbutton` (new tool in `Essential.panel/StrucBIM.stack`, sibling to `RoundElementDist.pushbutton` — a QA tool in the same "check first, fix on approval" family) runs in one window:

1. **Scope** — whole model, or a category-filtered selection (reusing the scope/category picker pattern `Parameter Combine.pushbutton`'s `ScopePickerApp` already ships), Structural Columns only for v1 (walls are a future item, see below).
2. **Build stacks** — reads every column via `columns.placed(doc)`, groups instances into vertical stacks by 2D (X, Y) proximity within a configurable tolerance (default in the same small-tolerance convention `RoundElementDist.pushbutton`'s `DEFAULT_MAX_SHIFT` already uses), then orders each stack bottom-to-top using `levels.levels(doc)`'s elevation ordering. A tolerance a modeler can widen for towers with an intentional minor plan offset per lift.
3. **Walk each stack, flag per adjacent pair**:
   - **Missing** — a level has a column in the stack above and below it but none at that level itself.
   - **Offset** — XY drift beyond tolerance between one column and the next one up in its stack, reported with the exact distance and direction.
   - **Level-chain gap/overlap** — the lower column's top level + top offset does not land at the elevation of the upper column's base level + base offset (reading the same `FAMILY_TOP_LEVEL_PARAM` / `FAMILY_TOP_LEVEL_OFFSET_PARAM` / `FAMILY_BASE_LEVEL_PARAM` / `FAMILY_BASE_LEVEL_OFFSET_PARAM` parameters `columns.create()` already writes at `anongee_toolkit/structural/columns.py:270-275`), reported as a gap or overlap in mm.
   - **Rotation drift** — the column's `LocationPoint.Rotation` differs from the one below it beyond a configurable angular tolerance, the same property `Rotate Column.pushbutton` already reads and writes.
   - Size steps are listed too, but never flagged as a fault — a stack narrowing at level 8 is normal design — so they are reported for visibility (which level, from what size to what) without a status colour, distinct from the four fault types above.
4. **Preview checklist** — one row per finding: stack id (by nearest-grid label if grids are present, else a running index), levels involved, finding type, magnitude, and a **Locate** action that isolates the relevant column(s) in the active view (`Selection.SetElementIds` + `ShowElements`) — the same "jump to the problem" posture the already-logged Warning Auditor idea proposes for its own findings, closing the exact gap Revit's own Review Warnings dialog leaves open.
5. **Report only** — this tool never writes to the model; it is a checker, not a fixer, since which fix is right (delete the extra column, move it back, or accept the offset as a deliberate architectural decision) is a judgement call for the modeler or the structural engineer, not a batch action. Exportable to Excel via the same `openpyxl` path `Export Schedule.pushbutton` / `BBS Generator.pushbutton` already use, giving a QA sign-off artifact ("N stacks clean, M flagged") for a handover the same way the already-logged Warning Auditor's report does for warnings.

**Implementation:**
- Promote `anongee_toolkit.structural.columns.placed(doc)` to direct button use — today it is only imported inside `rc_automation`'s own reconciliation path — and extend its returned tuple with base/top level ids and offsets (reading the same four `BuiltInParameter`s `create()` already writes) rather than duplicating the read logic in the new button.
- New `anongee_toolkit.structural.stacking` module: 2D-proximity clustering (a grid-bucketed nearest-neighbor union-find, the same union-find shape `RoundElementDist.pushbutton`'s own module docstring describes using for join clustering, pointed at XY distance instead of join geometry), stack ordering off `levels.levels(doc)`'s elevation sort, and the four per-pair checks (missing / offset / level-chain gap / rotation drift) as small, independently testable functions — kept Revit-free where possible (the clustering and the gap arithmetic take plain `(x, y, elevation)` tuples) so this can sit in `tests/` the way `anongee_toolkit.rc_automation.datum`'s geometry helpers already do.
- Rotation read reuses the exact `DB.LocationPoint.Rotation` property `Rotate Column.pushbutton/script.py:43` already reads; no new API surface.
- `stack_audit_config.json` alongside `script.py` (XY tolerance, angle tolerance, whether to flag a stack that starts above the foundation or stops below the roof — a column beginning or ending a building's own vertical extent is not itself a fault), mirroring `slab_convert_config.json`'s override-by-config shape.
- Same modeless window shell (`anongee_toolkit.ui.hostwnd` / `progressbar` / `checklist` / `theme`) every other multi-step tool in the repo runs on.
- Excel export reuses the `openpyxl` path already proven in `Export Schedule.pushbutton` / `BBS Generator.pushbutton`.

**Future update:**
- Extend past Structural Columns to Structural Walls acting as vertical stacks (shear wall continuity between levels), reusing the same clustering approach against wall centerline endpoints instead of a single point.
- Feed flagged "missing" rows straight into a column-placement action (create the missing instance at the resolved position, matched type from the stack's own consistent type) once the check is proven in production — the same "check first, fix on confirm" upgrade path the already-logged Warning Auditor's future items describe for its own findings.
- Cross-reference with the already-logged Grid Mark Assistant idea: once both exist, a stack's `Mark` can be verified against FEAT-28's own rule directly, flagging any level in a stack where `Mark` does not match the rest of the stack.
- Trend/report history alongside the already-logged Warning Auditor's proposed CSV trend log, so "stacks flagged" becomes a second axis of the same weekly model-health snapshot.

## 2026-09-13 00:39 UTC

**Idea name:** Grid Mark Assistant — automatic Column/Beam/Wall Mark numbering derived from live grid-intersection geometry

**Potential:**
Every structural modeler on a project of any real size does the same manual chore for the whole life of the model: open Properties on each column, read off the nearest two grid bubbles by eye, type `C-A1`, `C-B2`, `C-C3`... into Mark, and redo it by hand every time a column moves, a grid is renumbered, or a revision adds a bay — 200 columns is an afternoon the first time and a recurring tax every revision after, and a single fat-fingered mark (`C-A1` typed twice) is invisible until a schedule or a rebar tag collides with it. Nothing in this repo derives a Mark from grid geometry today: `RC Automation.pushbutton` writes footing marks straight from an Excel placement schedule (`_marks`/`item.mark`, `RC Automation.pushbutton/script.py:363-373` and `:426-440` — the mark is a spreadsheet column, never computed from position), and the already-logged **Universal Renamer** idea above explicitly scopes its token templates to Views/Sheets/Levels/Grids/Schedules/Family Types, not to Mark values on model elements, so there is no overlap. The pieces this needs are already sitting in three unrelated corners of the codebase, unconnected: `RoundElementDist.pushbutton/script.py:48-54` already imports `Autodesk.Revit.DB.Grid` and clusters walls/columns into direction families off real grid geometry; `anongee_toolkit.rc_automation.datum` already has the exact "which infinite line is this, and how far is a point from it" math (`_angle_of`, `_distance_to_line`, `same_line`, `datum.py:746-852`) — built for a spreadsheet-vs-model grid *planner*, not a live reader, but the geometry is identical either way; and `BBS Generator.pushbutton/core/bar_mark_manager.py` already solves "assign a mark, never collide with one already claimed" for rebar. Nobody has wired grid geometry to the Mark parameter. On the market, this exact feature — derive a structural element's Mark from its grid position automatically — is sold as one line item inside paid numbering/renumbering add-ins (Naviate's numbering tool, CTC's Renumber, BIM One's tools); nothing free reads Revit's own `Grid` elements and proposes a Mark from them.

**Description:**
`Grid Mark Assistant.pushbutton` (new tool in `Essential.panel/StrucBIM.stack`, sibling to `Convert Slab.pushbutton` and `RoundElementDist.pushbutton` — both structural-geometry tools it borrows math from) runs in one window:

1. **Scope & category** — pick Structural Columns, Structural Framing (beams), Structural Walls and/or Foundations, scoped to active view / whole model / current selection, reusing the same scope-and-category picker `Parameter Combine.pushbutton`'s `ScopePickerApp` already ships (`Parameter Combine.pushbutton/script.py:655-801`).
2. **Read grids** — collects every `DB.Grid` in scope into `(name, line)` pairs, clusters them into direction families (parallel grids sharing an axis, skewed grids their own family) the same way `RoundElementDist.pushbutton` already clusters walls/columns by direction, then classifies each family as "primary" (e.g. numbered 1-2-3…) or "secondary" (lettered A-B-C…) by count/orientation, user-overridable.
3. **Resolve nearest intersection** — per element, takes its anchor (column = location point; beam = location-curve midpoint; wall = centerline midpoint), finds the nearest grid in each direction family via the perpendicular-distance-to-line math already proven in `anongee_toolkit.rc_automation.datum` (`_distance_to_line`, `datum.py:844-852`), and composes a label from the configured template (`C-{Primary}{Secondary}` → `C-1A`, `B-{Secondary}{Primary}` → `B-A1`, a beam spanning two intersections → `B-A1~A2`). An element farther than a configurable tolerance from any grid (a free-standing column, an oddly offset wall) is flagged **no confident match** rather than guessed at.
4. **Preview checklist** — one row per element: current Mark, proposed Mark, distance-to-grid, and status (**will set** / **unchanged** / **no confident match — skip** / **collision, suffixed** `-2`); collision suffixing reuses the "never reuse a mark already claimed" rule `BBS Generator.pushbutton/core/bar_mark_manager.py` already implements for rebar, generalized to any Mark-bearing category. Rows tick/untick, same posture as every other checklist tool in the repo (`ConvertFloor.pushbutton`, `AutoLevel.pushbutton`).
5. **Apply** — one `Transaction`, writes to `Mark` (or a configurable target — `Comments`, or a firm's own shared parameter, for teams that keep Mark for something else) for every ticked row; **Report only** dry-run mode available up front, same toggle `Convert Slab.pushbutton` offers.
6. **Report** — result table (set / skipped-no-match / skipped-unchanged / collision-resolved) exportable via the same `openpyxl` path `Export Schedule.pushbutton` / `BBS Generator.pushbutton` already use.

**Implementation:**
- Promote `anongee_toolkit.rc_automation.datum`'s currently underscore-prefixed geometry helpers (`_angle_of`, `_distance_to_line`, `same_line`) into a small public module (e.g. `anongee_toolkit.geometry.lines`) shared between the existing spreadsheet-side grid planner and this new live-model reader — same function, two callers, no duplicated math.
- Direction-family clustering for grids adapts the clustering `RoundElementDist.pushbutton` already runs for elements (union-find over shared axis/angle tolerance), pointed at `DB.Grid.Curve` endpoints instead of wall/column geometry.
- Per-category anchor extraction (`Location.Point` for columns, `Location.Curve` midpoint for beams/walls) — the same kind of location read `AutoLevel.pushbutton/anongee_autolevel/planner.py` and `RoundElementDist.pushbutton` already do for their own placement logic.
- Mark collision-avoidance generalizes the uniqueness check in `BBS Generator.pushbutton/core/bar_mark_manager.py` beyond rebar bar marks, scanning the target parameter across the run's element set plus whatever the model already has.
- `grid_mark_config.json` alongside `script.py` (mark template per category, primary/secondary axis assignment, distance tolerance for "no confident match", target parameter name) — same override-by-config shape `slab_convert_config.json` established.
- Excel report reuses the `openpyxl` path already proven in `Export Schedule.pushbutton` / `BBS Generator.pushbutton`.
- Same modeless window shell (`anongee_toolkit.ui.hostwnd` / `progressbar` / `checklist`) every other multi-step tool in the repo runs on.

**Future update:**
- Diff/re-run mode: re-scan after a revision and flag only elements whose nearest intersection changed since the last run (a moved column, a renumbered grid) — the same "re-run and diff" shape already logged as a future item for Type Matrix Generator and the Sleeve Coordinator.
- Once marks are assigned, auto-place independent tags at the resolved intersection point with a configurable leader, closing the loop from computed Mark to drawing annotation in one pass.
- Feed the already-logged Warning Auditor's "duplicate Mark" auto-fix category the same uniqueness check this tool enforces at write time, so the two tools can't drift on what counts as a Mark collision.
- Extend beam labeling to a running sub-index for multiple parallel beams between the same two grid intersections (`B-A1a`, `B-A1b`), once the single-beam case is proven in production.

## 2026-09-12 15:39 UTC

**Idea name:** Parameter Migrator — batch, many-to-many parameter value moves driven by a reusable, save/load mapping table

**Potential:**
Every firm-wide shared parameter file revision, every consultant handover with its own parameter naming, and every BIM standard change mid-project (a client's COBie/data-drop spec landing after modelling has already started, or the model simply outgrowing a legacy text parameter that should have been a real shared one) produces the same job: values sitting in parameter A need to end up in parameter B, across hundreds of elements, and then A needs clearing so nobody edits a now-dead field by mistake. Today that's either a manual per-element Type Properties edit, or — the best this toolkit currently offers — `Parameter Combine.pushbutton`, whose `TargetParamCombo` (`Parameter Combine.pushbutton/script.py:197`) is a single `ComboBox` with one `SelectedIndex`: it writes into exactly **one** target parameter per run (confirmed at `script.py:623-625`, which errors "Please select a target parameter" if none is chosen), so migrating 20 parameters after a shared-parameter-file change means reconfiguring scope, categories and formula 20 separate times. The gap shows up again one layer over: `ConvertFloor.pushbutton`'s own README documents that parameters are carried over "matched by BuiltInParameter → shared GUID → name; anything without a counterpart in the target category is listed by name in the report" — i.e. every conversion already produces a list of elements now missing a value because the old and new parameter names don't line up, and nothing in the repo closes that list back up in bulk; the same gap is inherited by the proposed `Convert Column-Wall.pushbutton` idea above, which documents the identical "unmatched ones listed by name" behaviour. On the market, this is sold as one feature of paid whole-project data-management add-ins (Ideate BIMLink's parameter-copy dialogs, BIM One/Naviate's parameter tools) that charge per seat for exactly this one operation; nothing free does a template-driven, many-pairs-at-once move with a reusable mapping profile.

**Description:**
`Parameter Migrator.pushbutton` (new tool in `Advance.panel`, sibling to `Parameter Combine.pushbutton`) runs in one window:

1. **Build the mapping** — add rows of `Source Parameter → Target Parameter`, each row scoped to its own category set (reusing the same Categories tab pattern `MultiFilterParameter.pushbutton` already ships: scope = whole model / active view / selection, tick categories, live element count) and an explicit **Instance** or **Type** parameter mode, a **Copy** or **Move** direction (Move clears the source after a successful write), and an on-conflict rule (**Skip if target already has a value** / **Overwrite**). Rows can be typed by hand, or the whole table can be imported from a two-column-plus-flags Excel workbook — the fast path for a client-supplied rename schedule or a firm's shared-parameter-file diff that already exists as a spreadsheet.
2. **Preview grid** — one row per (element, mapping): current source value, current target value, and the resolved action — **will write**, **skipped (target occupied)**, **skipped (source blank)**, or **blocked (unit/type mismatch)** — tick to confirm, blocked rows pre-unticked and un-tickable, same posture `ConvertFloor.pushbutton`'s checklist already uses.
3. **Execute** — one `RevitTransaction` per mapping row (`anongee_toolkit.revit.transactions.RevitTransaction`, the same wrapper already proven as the engine behind `operations/bulk_rename.py` and `operations/bulk_delete.py`, i.e. `Bulk Rename.pulldown` / `Bulk Delete.pulldown`), each element inside the row read/written through `anongee_toolkit.revit.parameters.get_parameter_value` / `set_parameter_value` — so a bad mapping definition rolls back only its own row, not rows already applied successfully.
4. **Save the profile** — the exact mapping table (source/target pairs, scope, mode, direction, conflict rule) saves as a small JSON "migration profile," reusable next project without re-building the table — the shared-parameter-file scenario is firm-wide, not project-specific, so the same profile runs again the next time the standard changes.
5. **Report** — a result table (migrated / skipped-occupied / skipped-blank / blocked, per mapping row and per element) exportable to Excel via the same `openpyxl` path `Export Schedule.pushbutton` and `BBS Generator.pushbutton` already use (confirmed present in both scripts), giving a handover-ready audit trail of exactly what moved where.

**Implementation:**
- Core read/write goes through `anongee_toolkit.revit.parameters.get_parameter_value(..., as_string=False)` / `set_parameter_value` directly — that module already handles all four `StorageType`s and the instance-then-type fallback, but today it's only reached internally via `anongee_toolkit/utils/helpers.py`; no pushbutton script currently imports it (checked: no `AnonGee.tab/**/script.py` references `revit.parameters`), so this would be the first tool to put it to direct, user-facing bulk use.
- Numeric/unit handling reuses `anongee_toolkit.revit.units` (`strip_unit`, `mm_to_ft`, `ft_to_mm`) — already the engine behind every `structural/*` module RC Automation depends on — so a legacy text parameter storing `"230 mm"` can migrate cleanly into a real Length parameter, and a mismatch between an unparseable text value and a numeric target is what trips the "blocked (unit/type mismatch)" preview row rather than a silent bad write.
- Category/scope picking reuses `anongee_toolkit.revit.filtering.get_elements_by_categories`, the same helper already behind the Categories tab in `MultiFilterParameter.pushbutton`.
- Transaction wrapper is `anongee_toolkit.revit.transactions.RevitTransactionGroup` + one `RevitTransaction` per mapping row — not a new pattern, but the first time it is reused by name outside `operations/bulk_rename.py` / `operations/bulk_delete.py`.
- Mapping-table Excel import/export and the migration-profile JSON both sit alongside `script.py`, mirroring `slab_convert_config.json`'s "override by config, not code" shape.
- Same modeless window shell (`anongee_toolkit.ui.hostwnd` / `progressbar` / `checklist` / `theme`) every other multi-step tool in the repo already runs on, so the UI behaves consistently with `ConvertFloor.pushbutton` and `MultiFilterParameter.pushbutton`.
- `bundle.yaml` / `README.md` / `CHANGELOG.md` layout following the root README's documented convention for versioned tools, since — like `AutoLevel.pushbutton` — its mapping vocabulary (conflict rules, unit handling) is expected to keep growing.

**Future update:**
- Feed the "unmatched, listed by name" report `ConvertFloor.pushbutton` (and the proposed `Convert Column-Wall.pushbutton`) already produces straight into this tool as a pre-filled mapping table, so a conversion's parameter gaps close in the very next click instead of a separately remembered chore.
- Many-to-one migration (several legacy parameters folding into one canonical target, last-non-blank-wins or first-non-blank-wins) for the case where a firm's standard consolidates duplicate fields, complementing rather than duplicating `Parameter Combine.pushbutton`'s formula-based combination.
- A "diff mode" that re-runs a saved profile against a model months later and reports only what's changed since — useful when a migration profile is applied repeatedly across a rolling multi-phase project rather than once.
- Cross-project batch mode: point the tool at a folder of project files (or a list open in one Revit session) and apply one saved profile to all of them in sequence, for firm-wide parameter standard rollouts.

## 2026-09-12 10:39 UTC

**Idea name:** Warning Auditor — categorize, explain and batch-resolve Revit's own warning log, with a jump-to-element action for everything it can't safely fix

**Potential:**
Every model that survives more than a few weeks of team modelling accumulates a warning list that nobody reads until handover forces someone to: "Room is not in a properly enclosed region," "Elements are joined but do not intersect," "Highly used" duplicate-Mark warnings on rebar and framing, identical instances stacked at the same point after a copy/paste-align mistake. Revit's own **Manage → Review Warnings** dialog dumps every one of these into one flat, unsearchable, ungrouped list with no batch action at all — you click each row, "Show," look at it, close, next — so most teams either ignore the list until a coordination review flags it or burn an afternoon at handover clicking through 200+ rows by hand. This repo already fought exactly one instance of the pain this causes blind: `todo-list.md`'s own `BUG-22`/`BUG-23` record chasing a "duplicate-Mark" warning down manually in `RC Automation.pushbutton`'s column sync, the same failure mode this tool would have caught in seconds as a named, countable category instead of a build-log entry. Every tool in this repo that touches Revit's failure-suppression API (`ConvertFloor.pushbutton`, `AutoLevel.pushbutton`, `RC Automation.pushbutton`, `FramewinToBIM.pushbutton`) uses `IFailuresPreprocessor` only to *swallow* warnings during its own transaction — none of them ever calls `Document.GetWarnings()` to look at the model's standing list, and no such module exists anywhere under `anongee_toolkit`. On the market, this exact capability — read the warning log, group it, explain it, fix what's safe to fix automatically — exists only bundled as one feature among dozens inside paid whole-model BIM-QA suites (the kind of thing sold alongside clash detection and model-health dashboards); nothing free does this one job on its own for a modeler who just wants their warning count down before a Friday upload.

**Description:**
`Warning Auditor.pushbutton` (new tool in `Essential.panel/Bulk Tool.stack`, alongside `Bulk Delete.pulldown` — this is a cleanup tool in the same family) runs in one window:

1. **Scan** — calls `doc.GetWarnings()` once, groups every `FailureMessage` by its `FailureDefinitionId` GUID, and resolves each GUID to a human category via a lookup table shipped with the tool (Revit doesn't expose a readable name for most of its own warning types through the API — only the pre-formatted description string, which the table is keyed against as a fallback for anything not in the GUID map). Results show as one row per category: warning text, instance count, and a severity tag (**auto-fixable** vs **report-only**).
2. **Preview checklist** (`anongee_toolkit.ui.hostwnd` + `progressbar` + `checklist`, the same shell `ConvertFloor.pushbutton` and `AutoLevel.pushbutton` already run on) — auto-fixable categories are ticked by default per `warnings_config.json`, report-only categories are shown unticked and un-tickable, each with a **Locate** action per row that isolates the failing elements in the active view (`Selection.SetElementIds` + `ShowElements`) so a modeler jumps straight to the problem instead of hunting through Revit's own dialog one click at a time.
3. **Fix** — for v1's three auto-fixable categories: (a) **exact-duplicate instances at the same location** (same category + type + placement point) are collapsed to one, keeping the lower ElementId unless the config says otherwise; (b) **duplicate `Mark` values within a category** are resolved by appending a configurable numeric suffix to every collision but the first — the identical failure `RC Automation · BUG-22/23` hit and fixed by hand; (c) **"joined but do not intersect"** pairs are unjoined and rejoined, reusing the exact refresh routine `JoinPriority.pushbutton` already implements for its own "refresh edge-to-edge joins" option. One `TransactionGroup`, one `Transaction` per category, so a bad fix in one category rolls back without touching the others.
4. **Report** — a result table (per category: found / fixed / skipped / still-flagged) exportable via the same `openpyxl` path `Export Schedule.pushbutton` and `BBS Generator.pushbutton` already use, giving a QA sign-off artifact that says the warning count *and* what specifically changed to get there — the same audit trail `ConvertFloor.pushbutton`'s geometry-delta report gives for conversions.

**Implementation:**
- New `anongee_toolkit.revit.warnings` module: `doc.GetWarnings()` → group-by-GUID, a GUID→category lookup table (with a text-match fallback for unmapped GUIDs across Revit versions), and one fix function per auto-fixable category — kept separate from any one button's `script.py`, the same separation `anongee_toolkit.revit.geometry`/`transactions` already give `ConvertFloor.pushbutton`.
- Duplicate-instance and duplicate-Mark detection use `FilteredElementCollector` + a dict keyed on (category, type, location) or (category, Mark) respectively — no geometry-intersection code needed, unlike the Sleeve Coordinator idea above.
- Join refresh calls into `JoinPriority.pushbutton`'s existing unjoin/rejoin helper rather than reimplementing it, so the two tools can't drift on what "refresh a join" means.
- `warnings_config.json` alongside `script.py`, mirroring `slab_convert_config.json`'s shape: which categories default-ticked for auto-fix, the Mark-suffix format, and which ElementId is kept on a duplicate-instance collapse — safe defaults ship with everything but duplicate-Mark and exact-duplicate-instance off, since join-refresh has a wider blast radius on a real model.
- Report export reuses the `openpyxl` write path already proven in `Export Schedule.pushbutton`/`BBS Generator.pushbutton` — no new dependency.
- Sits in `Essential.panel/Bulk Tool.stack/` next to `Bulk Delete.pulldown`, following the same `bundle.yaml`/`README.md`/`CHANGELOG.md` layout the root README documents for versioned tools, since — like `AutoLevel.pushbutton` — its category coverage is expected to keep growing.

**Future update:**
- Fold in the "type bloat" health-check the Type Matrix Generator idea above proposes as a future update, so one "Model Health" report covers duplicate types and standing warnings together.
- Trend tracking: append each scan's category counts (timestamp + username) to a small CSV/Excel log, so a team lead can see whether a modeler's warning count is trending down over the week rather than only ever seeing a single snapshot.
- Firm-specific fix rules loaded from `warnings_config.json` without touching code — e.g. a different Mark-collision naming convention per discipline — the same override-by-config posture `slab_convert_config.json` and the proposed `sleeve_config.json` already use.
- Extend the auto-fixable set once these three are proven in production: snapping near-miss room-separation boundaries closed, and collapsing coincident line-based elements (detail lines, grid extents) reported as overlapping.

## 2026-09-12 05:39 UTC

**Idea name:** Sleeve & Opening Coordinator — MEP-clash-driven structural opening placement, sizing rules and blocked-zone reporting

**Potential:**
Every RCC project with MEP in the model or linked in hits the same weekly grind: a clash report (Navisworks export, or the linked MEP model itself) names 40–300 pipe/duct/conduit crossings through beams, walls, slabs and footings, and a modeler works through them one by one — measure the pipe/duct diameter and angle, check it against the beam's depth and the code's max-opening-to-depth ratio, check it isn't inside a column strip or too close to a support, place a generic opening or sleeve family at the right host/location/rotation, and tag it back to the clash ID for the MEP team to trace. Fifteen to forty crossings an hour by hand is optimistic once beam-depth and edge-distance checks are done properly instead of eyeballed, and a missed edge-distance check is exactly the kind of thing that surfaces as a site RFI months later. Every existing tool in the repo that touches structural elements (`ConvertFloor.pushbutton`, `RC Automation.pushbutton`, `RoundElementDist.pushbutton`) works host-by-host on elements already selected or already scoped by category — none of them read a second, MEP-side model and turn its crossings into structural openings; that geometry-intersection step doesn't exist anywhere in `anongee_toolkit` yet (`revit/geometry.py` has none, and there's no `sleeve`/`opening` module under `structural/`). On the market, this exact workflow is sold as a paid module inside larger BIM-coordination suites (Naviate/Symetri's openings tool, BIMOne's sleeve manager) — nothing free does clash-in, sized-and-code-checked-opening-out for Revit natively.

**Description:**
`Sleeve Coordinator.pushbutton` (new tool in `Essential.panel/StrucBIM.stack`, sibling to `Convert Slab.pushbutton` and `RoundElementDist.pushbutton`) runs in one window:

1. **Load crossings** — either import a Navisworks clash report (CSV/XML export, matched by element GUID/UniqueId back to the host Revit model) or scan directly: pick a linked MEP model (or a category set in the same model), pick the structural host categories (Structural Framing, Walls, Floors, Foundations), and the tool geometrically intersects MEP curve-based elements (pipes, ducts, conduits, cable trays) against host solids to build the same crossing list itself, one row per penetration.
2. **Resolve & size** — per crossing, works out opening shape (circular for pipe/conduit/round duct, rectangular for rectangular duct/tray) and size from the MEP element's diameter/width-height plus a configurable clearance allowance; checks it against per-category rules (max opening size as a fraction of beam depth/width, minimum edge distance from any column/support face or from another opening, no penetration inside a defined column-strip/shear-wall-boundary zone) — same shape as `ConvertFloor.pushbutton`'s pre-flight plan: every row resolves to **place** / **blocked** with the specific rule it failed.
3. Preview checklist (`anongee_toolkit.ui.hostwnd` + `progressbar`, same shell `AutoLevel.pushbutton` and `Convert Slab.pushbutton` already use): tick rows to place, blocked rows pre-unticked with the reason shown, each row tagged with the source clash ID for traceability.
4. **Place** — creates (or updates, if a sleeve at that clash ID already exists) a host-based opening/sleeve family instance per confirmed row, sets its size/rotation/host parameters and a "Clash Ref" instance parameter back to the originating clash ID; one `TransactionGroup`, one `Transaction` per element, same rollback-per-element posture as `Convert Slab.pushbutton`.
5. **Report** — result table (placed / blocked / updated / removed-because-crossing-no-longer-exists-on-rerun) exportable to Excel via the same `openpyxl` path `Export Schedule.pushbutton` and `BBS Generator.pushbutton` already use, so the MEP team gets back exactly the list they need to close their clash report against.

**Implementation:**
- New `anongee_toolkit.structural.openings` module: crossing detection (`Solid`/`Curve` intersection via `Autodesk.Revit.DB.BooleanOperationsUtils` or bounding-box-then-exact-solid test against each MEP curve swept as a thin solid), sizing/rule evaluation, and opening-family placement, following the same host-parameter-matching convention (`BuiltInParameter` → shared GUID → name) `ConvertFloor.pushbutton` uses for its own parameter carry-over.
- Reuses `anongee_toolkit.revit.geometry` for the low-level intersection/bounding-box helpers rather than duplicating them inline, and `anongee_toolkit.revit.transactions` for the per-element rollback pattern already proven there.
- Clash-report import: a thin CSV/XML reader matching Navisworks' export columns (test-name, item1/item2 GUIDs, clash point) to Revit elements via `UniqueId`; falls back to the direct-scan mode when no report is supplied, so the tool works even for teams that don't run Navisworks.
- Sizing/edge-distance rule set lives in a `sleeve_config.json` alongside `script.py`, mirroring `slab_convert_config.json`'s shape — per-category max-opening-ratio, min-edge-distance, clearance allowance, all overridable per project/firm standard.
- Opening family itself: ships one parametric round + one rectangular generic-model-based-opening family (or a face-based void-cutting family, matching whatever the firm's existing opening family already is, if one exists in the extension's family library) with type parameters for diameter/width/height and an instance "Clash Ref" text parameter for traceability.
- Excel report reuses the `openpyxl` write path already proven in `Export Schedule.pushbutton` / `BBS Generator.pushbutton` — no new dependency.
- `bundle.yaml` / `README.md` / `CHANGELOG.md` layout following the root README's documented convention for versioned StrucBIM tools.

**Future update:**
- Re-run mode that diffs against the last placed set: crossings that moved get the sleeve relocated, crossings that vanished (pipe re-routed) get the orphaned sleeve flagged for removal instead of silently left behind.
- Feed blocked rows (opening too big for the beam, or inside a shear-wall boundary) straight into a structural-engineer review queue, cross-referenced by the same "Clash Ref" id, so a redesign request is one export away instead of a re-explained email.
- Extend past MEP crossings to structural-vs-structural coordination: a duct penetrating both a beam and the slab it frames into, reconciling the two openings into one that satisfies both hosts' rules.
- Multi-linked-model support (structural host model with two or more linked MEP disciplines — plumbing, HVAC, electrical — scanned in one pass), each discipline's crossings tagged separately in the report.

## 2026-09-12 00:38 UTC

**Idea name:** Type Matrix Generator — Excel-driven bulk type creation, parameter sync and duplicate-type detection, across system families and loadable families

**Potential:**
Every structural revision that adds a new column/beam/wall size, or a schedule handed over by the engineer as "here are the 18 column sizes for this project," turns into the single most repetitive click-chain a BIM modeler does: open Type Properties, Duplicate, rename by hand, retype 3–6 dimension parameters, close, repeat — 2–4 minutes per type, 18 types is an hour of pure clicking, and a mistyped dimension on type 14 of 18 is invisible until a clash or a quantity take-off surfaces it. The toolkit already has a `type_name_mode` "convention" matcher baked into `ConvertFloor.pushbutton` and an Excel round-trip (`openpyxl`) proven in `Export Schedule.pushbutton` / `BBS Generator.pushbutton`, but nothing turns those two proven pieces into a general type factory — the closest thing, `BIMGeneration.pushbutton`'s "auto-sized type duplicates," is a hidden internal step of one INP-import workflow, not a tool a modeler can point at an arbitrary family and a spreadsheet. Revit's own answer, type catalogs, only works for loadable families that ship a `.txt` catalog and cannot touch system families (Structural Column, Structural Framing, Wall, Floor, Duct, Pipe) at all — which is exactly where a structural team's type churn actually happens. Paid market tools (e.g. Case/Ideate-style "batch type creator" add-ins) do the create-from-spreadsheet half for a fee; none of them add the other half this idea includes for free: scanning the project for types that are already duplicates of each other in every parameter but name, which is the silent bloat that makes a family's type list unusable a year into a project.

**Description:**
`Type Matrix Generator.pushbutton` (new `TypeTools.stack` in `Advance.panel`, alongside `Parameter Combine.pushbutton`) runs in three modes from one window:

1. **Generate from matrix** — pick a category + family (any loaded family, system or component), pick a base type to duplicate from, then load a CSV/XLSX where each row is a new type: a name column (or name-template tokens: `{b}x{d}`, `{Prefix}-{Index:03}`) plus one column per parameter to set (dimensions, material, structural usage, cost code, comments — anything writable on that family). Live preview grid resolves every row to "will create" / "already exists, will update" / "blocked" (missing required parameter, invalid value, name collision) before anything runs.
2. **Harvest to matrix** — the reverse: pick a category/family, tick which parameters matter, and export every existing type as a spreadsheet row — the fast way to hand an engineer "here is every column type in the model today" or to seed the input for mode 1 when duplicating a size series.
3. **Duplicate-type finder** — scan a category (or the whole model) for types whose full parameter set matches another type in the same family bar name, surface them as merge candidates with a usage count (how many instances use each), and — on confirm — repoint every instance of the duplicate to the kept type via `ChangeTypeId` before deleting the redundant type, same "what's lost, confirm per row" gate the conversion tools already use.

**Implementation:**
- Excel read/write via the `openpyxl` path already vendored for `Export Schedule.pushbutton` / `BBS Generator.pushbutton` — no new dependency.
- Row → type resolution: `Element.Duplicate` (or `ElementType.Duplicate`) for the create path, then set each mapped parameter by `BuiltInParameter` → shared GUID → name, exactly the matching order `ConvertFloor.pushbutton` already uses for its own parameter carry-over, so a name typo in the sheet falls back the same predictable way instead of silently no-op'ing.
- Name-template tokens reuse the same small `re`-based token formatter proposed for the Universal Renamer idea below (`{b}`, `{d}`, `{Index:03}`, a regex capture from an existing name) — if that tool lands first, this one imports its formatter instead of re-implementing it.
- Duplicate-type finder: compare each type's full writable-parameter dict (value + unit) pairwise within a family; `Element.GetDependentElements` / `FilteredElementCollector` filtered by `ElementId` gives the instance count and the instance list to repoint with `doc.Regenerate()` + `ChangeTypeId` inside the same per-element transaction-rollback pattern `ConvertFloor.pushbutton` uses.
- One preview grid + Generate/Report-only toggle shared across all three modes, same modeless-window shell (`anongee_toolkit.ui.hostwnd` / `progressbar` / `theme`) `AutoLevel.pushbutton` already runs on, so the window behaves consistently with the rest of the toolkit.
- Sits in a new `TypeTools.stack` in `Advance.panel` next to `Parameter Combine.pushbutton` (its natural sibling — one sets instance parameter values, this sets type parameter values and creates the types themselves); own `bundle.yaml` tooltip and `CHANGELOG.md` since, like `AutoLevel`, it is a tool that will keep growing new source categories.

**Future update:**
- Two-way live sync mode: re-import the same sheet after an engineer edits it and diff against current model types, so a revised design table updates existing types in place instead of only creating new ones.
- Cross-project type library: save a matrix + base-family pairing as a firm-standard "type set" (e.g. "IS 456 column sizes 230–900mm"), reusable project to project without re-authoring the sheet.
- Extend the duplicate-type finder into a background/report-only health-check mode runnable on a schedule, surfaced next to the model's other cleanup tools (`Bulk Delete.pulldown`) as a periodic "type bloat" report.
- Feed the harvested matrix straight into `BBS Generator.pushbutton`'s member/diameter filters, so a rebar schedule and a type-dimension schedule stay traceable to the same source spreadsheet.

## 2026-09-11 15:39 UTC

**Idea name:** Convert Column ⇄ Wall — cross-category recreate for structural columns and shear/RC walls

**Potential:**
Mid-project structural revisions routinely flip vertical elements between the two systems a BIM modeler has zero built-in tooling to bridge: a lift core or stair shaft redesigned from a column grid into shear walls, a party/boundary wall that a revised scheme turns into a column line, or an architect's RFI that swaps a stub wall for a column so a door can open past it. Today that's manual: note the location, height, offsets, phase, structural flag and hosted rebar of every element, delete it, hand-place the replacement, retype every parameter, and re-tag rebar and joins — 15–30 minutes per element, with hosted reinforcement and join order silently lost if the modeler forgets to redo them. `Convert Slab` already proved the team will invest in solving exactly this class of problem for Floor ⇄ Foundation; this is the same problem one category pair over, on elements that carry far more downstream dependents (rebar cages, joins to beams/slabs, tags) per element than a slab does. It is squarely in the productivity bracket the team already funds, it is a direct sibling of a tool already shipped, and there is no free (or, as far as public listings show, even paid) Revit add-in that does a *bidirectional*, dependent-preserving Column ⇄ Wall recreate — the paid category-swap tools on the market handle system-family-to-system-family swaps (wall type to wall type) or rely on Revit's own Type Selector, which cannot cross `OST_StructuralColumns` ⇄ `OST_Walls` at all.

**Description:**
`Convert Column-Wall.pushbutton` (sibling to `Convert Slab.pushbutton` in `StrucBIM.stack`) converts selected Structural Columns to Structural (or Basic) Walls, and selected Walls back to Structural Columns, on a pre-selection or an interactive pick, in plan, 3D, section or elevation:

1. Reads the pre-selection, or prompts a pick filtered to the relevant category (columns when going →Wall, structural/basic walls when going →Column).
2. Builds a plan per element:
   - **Column → Wall:** the column's plan footprint (rectangular or the sketch of a non-rectangular profile) resolves to a wall location **line** (the long axis, or a user-chosen axis for a square column) and a **thickness** (the other side); base level + base/top offsets carry across as the wall's own; a column whose footprint isn't reducible to one straight line + thickness (an L/T-shaped or round column with no dominant axis) is flagged and blocked, same as `Convert Slab` blocks a non-horizontal sketch.
   - **Wall → Column:** a straight wall's length and thickness resolve to a rectangular column footprint at each end (or split into repeated column instances at a spacing the user sets, for a long wall being replaced by a column line) — curved or multi-segment walls are blocked with the reason named, same posture as isolated footings being out of scope for `Convert Slab`.
3. Shows the same pre-flight checklist UX as `Convert Slab`: resolved target type per element, what survives, what's lost, blocked rows pre-unticked, Convert vs. Report-only.
4. One `TransactionGroup`, one `Transaction` per element — a single failure rolls back only that element.
5. Result table with a geometry delta (footprint area / volume / centroid) per element, flagged the same way `Convert Slab` flags a delta over tolerance.

What survives: level + base/top offset, structural flag, phase, workset, pin state, all writable instance + shared parameters matched by BuiltInParameter → shared GUID → name (unmatched ones listed by name), joins and join order (recorded, re-applied via `SwitchJoinOrder`), and — the hard part that makes this worth building rather than hand-converting — **hosted rebar re-pointed to the new host** via `Rebar.SetHostId` before the original is deleted, exactly like `Convert Slab` already does for slab-hosted rebar. What's lost, reported per element for confirmation: tags, dimensions, face-hosted family instances (door/window openings on a converted wall have nowhere to go on a column and vice versa), parts, the ElementId itself.

**Implementation:**
- Fork `ConvertFloor.pushbutton/script.py` structurally rather than the code: same recreate → replay → delete → rejoin → rehost-rebar → verify pipeline, same `type_name_mode` (`"same"` 1:1 by name, `"convention"` prefix/suffix + dimension-tolerance matching), same model-group ungroup/convert/regroup handling for single-instance groups, same blocked-by-default posture for multi-instance groups and elements in assemblies/inactive design options.
- New geometry step neither existing tool needs: footprint → line+thickness resolution for Column→Wall (via the column's `BoundingBoxXYZ` / cross-section sketch, falling back to the family's cross-section parameters when available) and line+thickness → footprint for Wall→Column — this is the one genuinely new piece of logic; everything else (parameter carry-over, join preservation, group handling, checklist UX, transaction-per-element rollback) is lifted from `ConvertFloor.pushbutton` and `anongee_toolkit.ui`.
- Rebar rehost reuses the exact `Rebar.SetHostId` call already proven in `ConvertFloor.pushbutton`; the "what's lost" report reuses its `forms.confirm` gate naming every element whose dependents will be destroyed.
- New `column_wall_convert_config.json` alongside `script.py`, mirroring `slab_convert_config.json`'s shape (`type_name_mode`, `thickness_tolerance_mm`, `groups.multi_instance`, plus a new `wall_axis_mode: "long_side" | "prompt"` and `column_spacing_mm` for the wall→column split case).
- Sits in `Essential.panel/StrucBIM.stack/`, next to `Convert Slab.pushbutton`, using the same `bundle.yaml` / `README.md` / `CHANGELOG.md` layout the root README documents for versioned tools.

**Future update:**
- Extend the footprint resolver to L/T-shaped and round columns by decomposing into multiple straight wall segments instead of blocking outright.
- Wall→Column mode learns a "replace every column-spaced stretch of this wall" batch mode, for converting an entire shear-wall run back into a column-and-infill scheme in one pick.
- Curved wall support via a chord-segmented column line, once the straight case has proven the rebar-rehost and join-preservation path in production.
- Shared `anongee_toolkit.convert` package factoring the recreate/replay/delete/rejoin/rehost pipeline out of both `Convert Slab` and this tool, so a third cross-category converter (e.g. Beam ⇄ Wall) starts from a real base instead of a second fork.

## 2026-09-11 14:30 UTC

**Idea name:** Universal Renamer — token templates + Excel mapping, across every renameable category

**Potential:**
Renaming to match a client's naming standard is one of the most repeated, most tedious tasks on a BIM team, and today it's done by hand, category by category, inside dialog boxes that only support simple find/replace. A model handover or a mid-project standard change (new discipline codes, new level naming, a merged project) can mean renaming 100+ sheets, views, levels, grids and schedules by hand — normally a half-day to a full day of pure clicking, with the real risk of dropped titleblock/view sync errors along the way. A token-driven, previewed, one-batch rename collapses that to minutes and removes the manual-error risk entirely. This is squarely in the productivity bracket the team already values enough to have built **Bulk Rename** for — it just stops short of the categories modelers actually rename most.

**Description:**
`Universal Rename.pushbutton` (or a new `Universal Rename.pulldown` sibling to the existing `Bulk Rename.pulldown`) extends renaming beyond Fill Patterns / Line Patterns / Line Styles to the categories a BIM modeler renames every week:

- Views (all types) and Sheets
- Levels and Grids
- Schedules and View Filters
- Family Types (within a family, e.g. duplicating a type-catalog row)

Two rename modes, selectable per run:
1. **Token template** — build a pattern from live tokens (`{Discipline}`, `{Level}`, `{ViewType}`, `{SheetNumber}`, `{OldName}`, `{Index:03}`, a regex capture group from the current name) and apply it across every selected/filtered element in one pass, e.g. turn `S-101 - Foundation Plan` into `STR-L00-FOUNDATION-PLAN`.
2. **Excel mapping** — drop in a two-column workbook (`Old Name → New Name`), matched by exact name or regex; this is the fast path for a client-supplied rename schedule that already exists as a spreadsheet, and it's fully auditable (the workbook itself is the change record).

Both modes share one pipeline: filter (by category, by naming pattern, by pre-selection) → live preview table showing old name / new name / collision flags → user unticks anything wrong → single `Transaction`, one undo step → result report (renamed / skipped / collided), exportable back to Excel for a handover record.

**Implementation:**
- Reuse the existing `Bulk Rename.pulldown` engine (`AnonGee.extension/AnonGee.tab/Essential.panel/Bulk Tool.stack/Bulk Rename.pulldown/`) as the base: it already has the search/tick/apply/report loop for style-type elements — generalize its element-fetch + rename-call layer behind a small per-category adapter (`views.py`, `sheets.py`, `levels.py`, `grids.py`, `schedules.py`, `filters.py`, `family_types.py`), each just implementing "list renameable items" + "apply new name" + "collision check", same shape `ConvertFloor.pushbutton` uses for its own per-category resolution.
- Token engine: small formatter over Revit's built-in parameters + regex capture groups pulled from the current name (`re.match` against a user-supplied pattern) — no new dependency, CPython3 stdlib `re` is enough.
- Excel mapping: reuse the `openpyxl` path already proven in `Export Schedule.pushbutton` / `BBS Generator.pushbutton` for read + the write-back report.
- Collision detection before the transaction opens (duplicate target names within the batch, or a target name already used elsewhere in the model) — block those rows, same "blocked rows pre-unticked" UX `Convert Slab` uses.
- Sits as a new pulldown/stack entry in `Essential.panel/Bulk Tool.stack/`, next to `Bulk Rename.pulldown`, following the same `bundle.yaml` / `ui.xaml` / `script.py` bundle layout documented in the root README.
- Ships with its own `CHANGELOG.md` since it's a tool likely to grow new categories over time (same pattern as `AutoLevel.pushbutton`).

**Future update:**
- Saved rename profiles (per-client naming standard), reusable across projects — analogous to Filter View Manager's project/workstation rule store.
- Extend token source to shared parameters, not just built-ins, so a firm-specific "Discipline Code" parameter can drive the template directly.
- Batch rename across a set of linked/worksharing-central models in one run (multi-document mode), for firms that split a project into linked structural/architectural/MEP files with a shared naming standard.
- Dry-run diff export (old vs. new) as a standalone Excel report without touching the model, for client sign-off before the actual rename.

## 2026-09-15 10:41 UTC

**Idea name:** Bulk Purge — unused View Templates, Filters & Materials, with a live usage-count column (grows `Bulk Delete.pulldown` past styles-only)

**Potential:**
Revit's own **Manage → Purge Unused** stops at zero-instance family types and a short fixed list of style categories — it has never touched View Templates, Filters or Materials, because none of those are ever "in use" in the instance-count sense Purge Unused checks; a View Template with zero views assigned, a Filter added to no view's V/G list, a Material referenced by no compound-structure layer or paint override just sit there forever. On a project handed between modelers, copy-pasted from an old template file, or accumulated over a multi-phase job, this is routinely 30-80 dead View Templates and Filters and a couple dozen dead Materials by the time of handover — and today the only way to find them is opening **Manage → View Templates** / the Filters tab in Visibility Graphics / the Material Browser and eyeballing each one, with no usage count shown anywhere in Revit's own UI, so a modeler either leaves them (file bloat, a Type Selector and filter-picker cluttered with dead entries) or deletes on a guess and risks silently un-assigning a template or filter that actually was in use on a view nobody checked. This repo already solved exactly this shape of problem for three narrower categories — `Bulk Delete.pulldown`'s `Delete FillPatterns.pushbutton` / `Delete LinePatterns.pushbutton` / `Delete LineStyles.pushbutton` — but all three list *every* fill pattern / line pattern / line style with no usage information at all and rely on `doc.Delete()` silently failing on an in-use one (`Delete FillPatterns.pushbutton/script.py:242-249`, `"except Exception: skipped += 1   # system-locked / in-use"`), which works only because a fill/line pattern still in use is safe to leave untouched by a failed delete — a View Template still assigned to views or a Filter still in a view's filter list is not the same case: deleting either one *succeeds* and silently strips the assignment from every view carrying it, so the exact "list everything, try delete, skip failures" shape this repo already ships would quietly break views if pointed at these three categories unchanged. That gap — no usage-count anywhere in Revit's UI, and this repo's own working pattern being unsafe to reuse as-is for these categories — is what makes this worth building rather than just adding three rows to an existing dropdown. On the market, whole-model cleanup of unused View Templates/Filters/Materials in one pass is sold only as a "purge/clean-up" line item inside paid whole-project add-ins (Ideate Sidekick's Purge, BIM One's eTools-class utilities), licensed per seat as part of a much larger bundle; nothing free computes a per-item usage count for these three categories at all, Revit's own Purge Unused included.

**Description:**
Three new pushbuttons — `Delete View Templates.pushbutton`, `Delete Filters.pushbutton`, `Delete Materials.pushbutton` — added to the existing `Bulk Delete.pulldown` (`AnonGee.tab/Essential.panel/Bulk Tool.stack/Bulk Delete.pulldown/`), same search-box / checklist / Select-All-Select-None / live-count / delete-with-confirm shell as the three style-deletion buttons already there, with one addition every existing one is missing: a **usage-count badge per row** ("used by 0 views", "used by 12 views", "used by 3 types"), computed up front and never guessed at.

1. **Collect + count** — per category:
   - *View Templates:* every `View` where `IsTemplate` is true; usage count = how many non-template views in the model have `ViewTemplateId` equal to it.
   - *Filters:* every `ParameterFilterElement`; usage count = how many views return it from `GetFilters()`.
   - *Materials:* every `Material`; usage count = how many loaded types reference it in a `CompoundStructure` layer (paint-override references are a known gap, named explicitly in the dialog rather than silently missed — see Implementation).
2. **Sort and pre-tick by usage** — zero-usage rows sort to the top and are pre-ticked (this is the "safe, opt-out" purge case); any row with usage_count > 0 starts unticked, and ticking one changes the confirm-dialog wording to name exactly what breaks ("2 of the items you picked are still in use — deleting them will silently un-assign the View Template from 5 views / drop the Filter from 3 views' filter lists").
3. **Search + Select-All/None** — identical to the existing three buttons, operating only on the currently-visible (search-filtered) rows, same as `Delete FillPatterns.pushbutton/script.py`'s `_on_all`/`_on_none`.
4. **Delete** — one `Transaction`, same per-item try/skip loop the existing three buttons already use, since a genuinely-in-use element (a Material still painted onto a face, for instance) can still fail to delete and should be skipped rather than blocking the batch.
5. **Result** — "Deleted N of M (K skipped — still referenced)" exactly matching the existing message shape, so the three new buttons read as siblings of the three that already exist rather than a different tool bolted on.

**Implementation:**
- Add three entries to the `_PROVIDERS` dict in the shared engine, `anongee_toolkit/operations/bulk_delete.py:64-83`, alongside the existing `fillpattern` / `linepattern` / `linestyle` keys — `viewtemplate`, `filter`, `material` — each supplying a `collect(doc)` that returns `[(name, ElementId, usage_count)]`, a 3-tuple where today's collectors (`_collect_fillpatterns`/`_collect_linepatterns`/`_collect_linestyles`, `bulk_delete.py:34-57`) return a 2-tuple; `GenericBulkDeleteDialog._rebuild_list()` (`bulk_delete.py:161-174`) needs the extra field threaded through to a new usage-badge `TextBlock` next to each row's name, and `_on_delete`'s confirm prompt (`bulk_delete.py:213-215`) needs the in-use-row count spliced into its wording.
- `_collect_view_templates(doc)`: `FilteredElementCollector(doc).OfClass(View)` filtered to `.IsTemplate`; usage count via a single pass over every non-template view's `ViewTemplateId` (the exact property already read one-view-at-a-time in `ToggleLinked.pushbutton/script.py:16,43` and `AutoLevel.pushbutton/anongee_autolevel/revit_ops.py:492` — this is its first aggregate, whole-model use).
- `_collect_filters(doc)`: `FilteredElementCollector(doc).OfClass(ParameterFilterElement)`; usage count via `view.GetFilters()` per eligible view — a call that returns **zero hits anywhere in this repo today** (confirmed by grepping every `script.py` and every `anongee_toolkit` module for `GetFilters`), the closest existing code being `cad2bim/builders/view_filters.py:170-171`'s `AddFilter`, a write-only, create-time call that has never been paired with a read.
- `_collect_materials(doc)`: `FilteredElementCollector(doc).OfClass(Material)` — the same collector `cad2bim/builders/materials.py:75` already runs, there purely to create materials during a CAD import, never to count usage; usage count from `FilteredElementCollector(doc).OfClass(FloorType/WallType/...)`'s `GetCompoundStructure().GetLayers()` matched by `MaterialId`. Paint-override usage (`doc.Settings`/`Document.GetPaintedFaces`-style lookups) is left out of v1 and the dialog says so in its info badge, rather than reporting a false zero-usage count for a material that's actually painted onto a face somewhere.
- New `ui.xaml` row template: reuses the existing `ItemsList`/`ListBoxItem`/`CheckBox` shape verbatim (`Delete FillPatterns.pushbutton/ui.xaml`) with one added `TextBlock` bound to the usage-count string, styled with the same inlined theme resources already duplicated across all three existing `ui.xaml` files.
- The three new pushbuttons import `GenericBulkDeleteDialog` from `anongee_toolkit.operations.bulk_delete` directly — the first production caller of that shared module: today all three existing Bulk Delete pushbuttons (`Delete FillPatterns.pushbutton/script.py`, and its LinePatterns/LineStyles siblings) each carry their own fully inlined copy of the same dialog class and `PROVIDERS` dict rather than importing the shared one (confirmed: no `AnonGee.tab/**/script.py` imports `anongee_toolkit.operations.bulk_delete`), so this closes that three-way duplication as a side effect of adding the new categories on top of the shared engine instead of a fourth inline copy.
- Sits as three more pushbuttons inside the existing `Bulk Delete.pulldown/bundle.yaml` grouping, no new panel or stack needed.

**Future update:**
- Extend `_PROVIDERS` further to unused Line Weights, unused Detail Groups and unused Model Groups once the usage-count pattern is proven on these three.
- Paint-override usage detection for Materials, closing the v1 gap named above.
- A Filter's schedule-only usage (added to a schedule's own filter tab rather than a view's V/G filter list) isn't caught by `GetFilters()` alone — add a schedule-definition scan as a second usage source before calling a Filter's count final.
- One combined "Full Model Purge" dashboard running every provider (the existing three style categories, these three, and the already-logged Type Consolidator's duplicate-type sweep) in a single pass with one summary report and one Excel export of the before/after candidate list — reusing the `openpyxl` write-back path already proven in `Export Schedule.pushbutton` / `BBS Generator.pushbutton` — for a documented clean-up audit trail at handover.

## 2026-09-22 00:38 UTC

**Idea name:** Room Finish Auto-Fill & Mismatch Auditor — computes every Room's Base/Wall/Floor/Ceiling Finish parameters straight from the actual bounding walls/floors/ceilings' Type Mark or Material, instead of a modeler typing them in by hand, and flags every room whose boundary disagrees with itself

**Potential:**
Revit ships four built-in text parameters on every Room — Base Finish, Wall Finish, Floor Finish, Ceiling Finish — and every architectural/BIM handover schedule depends on them being filled in, but Revit never populates them: they are free-text fields a modeler fills by opening each room, looking at what wall type and floor/ceiling type actually bound it, and typing a finish name in by hand, room by room, for the entire building. On a 150-room hospital or hotel floor plate this is a full day of repetitive, error-prone clerical work with zero model-derived truth behind it — two modelers filling in the same floor independently will not agree, and nothing catches a room where three of its four bounding walls are `Painted Plaster` and the fourth (added late, or by someone reusing a template file) is still `Exposed Block`, silently shipping a wrong finish schedule to the client. This repo has never once touched Rooms or `SpatialElement` boundaries — confirmed by grepping every `script.py` and every `anongee_toolkit` module for `OST_Rooms`, `SpatialElement` and `GetBoundarySegments`, all zero hits — so this isn't an extension of an existing tool so much as it is the toolkit's first entry into an entire category (Room/space data) that every other discipline on the team touches daily and the toolkit currently has nothing for. It also directly generalizes a pattern this repo already trusts: `Rebar Cover Auditor`'s "auto-compute from geometry, flag whatever disagrees with itself for a human to confirm" shape, applied here to finishes instead of cover distance. On the market this exists only as a named feature inside paid whole-project suites (Ideate Sidekick and similar bundles ship a "room finish" helper as one line item among dozens, licensed per seat as part of the whole package); nothing free computes it from live boundary geometry with a majority/mismatch check the way this would.

**Description:**
`Room Finish Auto-Fill.pushbutton` (new `Room.stack`, sibling to `StrucBIM.stack` under `Essential.panel`, since Rooms are the first non-structural spatial category this toolkit touches):

1. Collects every placed, enclosed Room in the active view's level (or the whole model, user's choice) via `FilteredElementCollector(doc).OfCategory(OST_Rooms)`, immediately splitting off and reporting unplaced rooms (`Room.Area == 0`) and unenclosed rooms (`Location is None` / redundant/overlapping boundary) as their own "cannot compute" list — the same "flagged and blocked, not silently skipped" posture `Convert Slab` and `Convert Column ⇄ Wall` already use for their own unconvertible elements.
2. For every valid room, walks `SpatialElementGeometryCalculator` / `Room.GetBoundarySegments()` to resolve the actual bounding element behind each segment (wall, or the room's own floor/ceiling by level offset), and reads each one's `Type Mark`, `Type Name`, or a configurable "Finish" type parameter (a firm's own wall-type family parameter, if one exists) as that segment's finish value.
3. Computes, per room, per finish slot (Base/Wall/Floor/Ceiling): the majority value across all bounding segments, and a mismatch flag when any segment disagrees — mirroring `Rebar Cover Auditor`'s own majority/outlier logic, just run over boundary segments instead of bar-to-face distances.
4. Same preview-checklist UX as every other write tool in this repo, built on the shared `anongee_toolkit/ui/checklist.py` `CheckList` (the CPython-safe replacement for `forms.SelectFromList` already used across the toolkit rather than a bespoke dialog): one row per room, showing current parameter value → computed value, mismatch rooms sorted to the top and pre-unticked (same "resolved but risky rows start unticked" convention `Bulk Purge` uses for still-in-use elements), clean-majority rooms pre-ticked.
5. One `Transaction`, batch-writes the four Finish parameters for every ticked room; result report — filled / skipped (already had a manually-entered value, left untouched unless overwrite is explicitly ticked) / flagged-mismatch (written with the majority value but still listed for a human walk-through) — exportable to Excel via the same `openpyxl` write-back path `Export Schedule.pushbutton` / `BBS Generator.pushbutton` already use, so it doubles as the handover audit trail.

**Implementation:**
- New `anongee_toolkit/architectural/` package (first module outside `structural/` and `cad2bim/`) — `rooms.py` for boundary/segment resolution and majority-vote logic, kept separate from `structural/` since Rooms are conceptually a different domain from the column/beam/footing/wall code every existing module already assumes.
- Boundary-to-element resolution via `Room.GetBoundarySegments(SpatialElementBoundaryOptions())` → `BoundarySegment.ElementId`, cross-referenced against `doc.GetElement(...)`'s category to route to Wall vs. Floor vs. Ceiling handling; the room's own level (for matching the correct floor/ceiling by elevation) is read via the same `levels.py` `levels(doc)` helper `AutoLevel.pushbutton` already relies on for its own level table.
- Reuses `anongee_toolkit/ui/checklist.py`'s `CheckList` for the preview grid (already proven CPython3-safe, unlike `forms.SelectFromList`) and `anongee_toolkit/ui/dialogs.py`'s `WpfDialogBase` shell for the surrounding window chrome, matching every other tool's dialog look.
- Excel export reuses the exact `openpyxl` read/write path already proven in `Export Schedule.pushbutton` and `BBS Generator.pushbutton`.
- New `room_finish_config.json` alongside `script.py`: which Type parameter to read as "finish" per host category (defaults to `Type Mark`, overridable to a firm-specific shared parameter), and whether Base Finish inherits the Floor Finish value by default (a common firm convention) or is computed independently.
- Sits in a new `Room.stack` under `Essential.panel`, using the same `bundle.yaml`/`ui.xaml`/`script.py` bundle layout the root README documents for every other tool.

**Future update:**
- Extend the same boundary-resolution engine to auto-populate Room `Occupancy`/`Department` from a linked architectural program spreadsheet, closing the matching manual-entry gap on those two parameters.
- A "Room Data Sheet" one-page-per-room export (area, perimeter, volume, computed finishes, door/window count and adjacent-room list) built on the same `openpyxl`/report pipeline, for client-facing handover packages.
- Detect and flag Rooms whose boundary walls carry two structurally-different wall types with the same Type Mark (a false-agreement case the majority vote alone can't catch) by cross-checking `WallType.GetCompoundStructure()` as a second signal.
