## 2026-09-22 05:43 UTC

**Idea name:** Stair Landing Completer — sweeps the model for dog-leg stair pairs `stairs.sync` placed without the mid-flight landing between them, and finishes each one with the exact `StairsLanding` API call the toolkit already proved elsewhere but never wired into the pipeline whose own docs call this gap out by name

**Potential:**
`stairs.sync` (`AnonGee.extension/lib/py3/anongee_toolkit/commands/stairs.py`, registered as `("stairs.sync", "stairs", "run")` in `commands/__init__.py:47`) is the RC Automation command that places real Revit `Stairs` from the `STAIR_PLACEMENT` sheet — Option A, chosen over a beam-and-slab "waist stair" the project owner rejected outright (`todo-list.md:1016`, `FEAT-42`, `testing`: *"building stairs with beam and slab is management nightmare"*). Its own module docstring admits, in the same breath as explaining the dog-leg grouping idiom, that the fix is incomplete: *"A dog-leg is two rows sharing a mark with a blank row after them, the same idiom that makes a continuous beam a group — and, for now, the same idiom that gets two separate straight flights, because **the landing between them is not placed yet**"* (`commands/stairs.py:10-13`). This is not a stray comment — there is a whole superseded-decision document, `docs/specs/2026-08-27-stairs-option-b.md`, that names it in a feature table (line 39: `| **Landing** | *Not built yet* | See below. This is the honest gap |`) and gives it its own section (`### 2. No mid-landing`, line 86), and `todo-list.md:1016` repeats it in the live `FEAT-42` row: *"No mid-landing yet"*, status still `testing`. Three independent documents agreeing the same feature is missing is as close to a confirmed, current gap as this repo gets. What none of them mention is that the fix already exists, shipped, in a different pipeline: `grep -rn "StairsLanding" AnonGee.extension --include=*.py` (excluding the vendored `tzdata` tree) returns exactly five lines, and every one is in `cad2bim/builders/stairs.py` or its own changelog comment — `:22` imports it, `:303` calls `StairsLanding.CreateAutomaticLanding(doc, first, second)`, `:388` calls `StairsLanding.CreateSketchedLanding(doc, stairs_id, loop, relative_elevation)`, and `cad2bim/__init__.py:831,837` mention it only in changelog prose. Zero hits in `structural/stairs.py` or `commands/stairs.py` — the sheet-driven pipeline that admits it needs exactly this call has never imported the class that makes it. The reason it isn't a one-line port: `structural/stairs.py:198-216`'s `create()` opens its own `StairsEditScope` per flight (`scope.Start(base_level.Id, top_level.Id)`, line 216), and `commands/stairs.py:49-50`'s `_parse()` docstring says plainly *"Groups flattened — a flight is a flight downstream"* — the dog-leg pairing `rc_automation/placement.py:399-408`'s `parse_stairs()` establishes upstream (*"a dog-leg is two rows sharing a mark... a dog-leg stair is two flights that make one stair"*) is deliberately thrown away before `_apply()` (`commands/stairs.py:65`) calls `stairs.create()` once per row at line 129, independently. Two rows sharing a mark become **two separate `Stairs` elements**, not two `StairsRun`s on one `Stairs` — and `StairsLanding.CreateAutomaticLanding` requires both runs on the *same* `Stairs`, exactly what cad2bim's single-scope, multi-`CreateStraightRun` approach (`cad2bim/builders/stairs.py:165-260`) sets up before it ever calls the landing API at line 303. So today, every dog-leg `stairs.sync` places ships with a gap where the landing should be, and the only fix is a modeler manually re-entering the stair's sketch and drawing the landing by hand — the same "automation stops one hand-finishing step short of done" gap this log already treats as real lost time elsewhere (Rebar Cage Copier, Section Batch Generator). No market add-in targets this specifically — it is this repo's own pipeline's own gap — but the manual workaround it currently forces is exactly the repetitive clicking this toolkit otherwise exists to remove.

**Description:**
`Stair Landing Completer.pushbutton`, a standalone repair tool runnable any time after `stairs.sync` — it reads stairs already placed in the model, not the sheet, so it also catches dog-legs built in earlier sessions before this tool existed:

1. **Discover pairs** — collect every placed `Stairs` via `structural/stairs.placed(doc)` (`structural/stairs.py:291-306`), but re-keyed by **mark** instead of the first-run-midpoint key that function documents as being for the sheet planner's own matching purposes; every mark shared by exactly two `Stairs` elements is a dog-leg candidate.
2. **Check the gap** — for each pair, confirm the two flights are end-to-end adjacent (base/top level and run endpoint check, mirroring the adjacency `parse_stairs()` already establishes upstream on sheet rows, applied here to built geometry instead) and that no landing already sits between them; pairs that already have one are reported clean and skipped, not silently dropped.
3. **Preview list** — one row per candidate pair: mark, both element ids, base/top levels, a jump-to-element action zooming both flights into view; every row pre-ticked, since "adjacent same-mark pair, no landing" is unambiguous once confirmed.
4. **Place the landing** — re-enter each pair through a fresh `StairsEditScope`, using the same `_swallow_warnings()` commit pattern `structural/stairs.py:257,261` already runs, and call `StairsLanding.CreateAutomaticLanding(doc, first_run, second_run)` — the exact call proven at `cad2bim/builders/stairs.py:303` — once both runs are addressable from one scope; where that isn't achievable for two independently-created `Stairs` elements, fall back to `StairsLanding.CreateSketchedLanding(doc, stairs_id, loop, relative_elevation)` (proven at `cad2bim/builders/stairs.py:388`) sketched across the facing edges, and report which path each pair used.
5. Runs through `commands/_placement.outside_a_transaction` (`commands/_placement.py:174`) exactly as `stairs.sync` itself must, since `StairsEditScope` refuses to start inside an open transaction.
6. Result report: placed / already-had-a-landing / blocked-with-reason (non-adjacent flights, mismatched levels, or a merge-not-supported fallback), same finding shape `commands/stairs.py` already emits elsewhere.

**Implementation:**
- New `anongee_toolkit.structural.stair_landings` module: `pairs_by_mark(doc)` (re-keys `structural/stairs.placed()`'s output), `landing_gap(pair)` (adjacency + existing-landing check), `place_landing(doc, pair)` (wraps the `CreateAutomaticLanding` call with the `CreateSketchedLanding` fallback).
- Reuses `structural/stairs.placed()` (`structural/stairs.py:291`) for discovery, `_swallow_warnings()` (`structural/stairs.py:261`) for the commit, and `commands/_placement.outside_a_transaction` (`commands/_placement.py:174`) for the transaction-free run shape — all three already proven by `stairs.sync` itself.
- `StairsLanding.CreateAutomaticLanding`/`CreateSketchedLanding` call signatures copied verbatim from their one working call site (`cad2bim/builders/stairs.py:303,388`) rather than re-derived from API docs, since that is the only place in the repo they have ever run against a real model.
- New pushbutton bundle (`bundle.yaml`/`script.py`/`README.md`) alongside `Convert Slab.pushbutton`/`Draw Floor.pushbutton` in `Essential.panel/StrucBIM.stack/`, using the same modeless checklist preview shell every other batch repair tool in the repo already runs on.

**Future update:**
- Wire the same pair/gap check into `stairs.sync`'s own `_apply()` (`commands/stairs.py:65`) as a post-placement flag, so a dog-leg's missing landing surfaces the moment it's placed instead of waiting for a separate audit pass — the same "audit becomes a built-in preflight" progression the already-logged Naming Convention Auditor proposes for RC Automation's own duplicate-type risk.
- If merging two independently-created `Stairs` elements into one scope proves reliable, retrofit `commands/stairs.py`'s `_apply()` to build both flights of a dog-leg inside one shared `StairsEditScope` from the start, so future `stairs.sync` runs place the landing inline and this tool becomes a one-time repair pass for stairs built before the fix, not a permanent step in the workflow.
- Extend pairing past two-row marks to three-or-more-flight scissor/return stairs once the dog-leg case is proven, reading whatever grouping shape `rc_automation/placement.py`'s `parse_stairs()` gives a longer run.
- Push the same boundary-resolution engine the other direction: a "Finish Type Consistency" check that verifies a wall's actual built type matches what every room touching it already reports, catching a wall swapped to the wrong type after room finishes were already signed off.

## 2026-09-22 10:39 UTC

**Idea name:** Duplicate Instance Finder & Merger — flags coincident-location elements of the same category and type sitting exactly on top of each other (a copy-paste slip, a "Paste Aligned → Same Place" repeated by habit, a linked model accidentally bound into the host), the one everyday model-hygiene pass Revit's own warning log does not run and this repo's own dead bounding-box helper has never been pointed at

**Potential:**
Copying a column, wall, beam or generic-model instance and pasting it back "Aligned to Same Place" — the exact workflow a modeler reaches for after moving a family into position by trial and error, or reflexively hits twice out of habit — produces two fully valid, fully overlapping elements, and Revit raises **no warning for it**: unlike a genuinely illegal condition (two walls sketched on the same line in the same sketch, which Revit does flag), two independently-placed family instances occupying identical space are each individually legitimate, so the warning dialog stays silent while the schedule, the quantity take-off and the rebar/BBS count silently double for that one element. This is a well-known, recurring failure mode on any project that goes through more than one round of copy-paste coordination or a link-to-host bind gone wrong, and it compounds exactly where this toolkit already does its heaviest lifting: a duplicated column or footing does not just inflate a schedule row, it gives `BBS Generator.pushbutton` a second full rebar cage to count and `structural/inventory.py`'s own category counts (`_categories()`, `inventory.py:73`) a silently wrong total the moment anyone reads the model's own self-description. The repo already has exactly the primitive this needs, built once and used for something else entirely: `anongee_toolkit.revit.geometry.bounding_boxes_overlap()` (`revit/geometry.py:40`) is public API — re-exported in `anongee_toolkit/__init__.py:108,223` and `anongee_toolkit/revit/__init__.py:13,37` — and `grep -rn "bounding_boxes_overlap" AnonGee.extension` finds exactly one real caller outside its own definition: `BIMGeneration.pushbutton/core.py:369-373` doesn't even import the shared one, it hand-rolls its own near-identical copy, `_bounding_boxes_overlap()`, and uses it only to decide whether to `JoinGeometryUtils.JoinGeometry()` a column against a beam (`core.py:489-490`) — a completely different question (do these two touch?) from the one this idea asks (are these two the *same* element, twice?), and answered with a *loose* tolerance (`tolerance=-0.05`, i.e. boxes must overlap by more than half an inch before joining) rather than the tight, near-zero tolerance a true duplicate check needs. So today the one exported helper capable of this check has a hand-rolled duplicate of itself sitting right next to it, and neither copy has ever been asked "is this a duplicate instance," only "should I join these." On the market, this exact check — coincident-instance detection across categories — exists only as one line item inside paid whole-model QA/health-check suites (Ideate Sidekick's "Model Health" duplicate scan, CTC BIM Tools' Health Check, BIM One's eTools Pro "Duplicate Finder"), each licensed per seat as part of a much larger bundle; Revit's own Purge Unused and warning log, by design, catch neither case, because a duplicate instance is neither "unused" (both copies are placed and visible) nor "invalid" (each one individually is a perfectly legal element).

**Description:**
`Duplicate Instance Finder.pushbutton`, a new sibling inside the existing `Bulk Tool.stack` (alongside `Bulk Delete.pulldown` / `Bulk Rename.pulldown` / `Rebar.pulldown`), since this is a whole-model hygiene sweep in the same family as the bulk-delete tools already living there:

1. **Scope + category picker** — whole model or active view; category list seeded from the same category walk `structural/inventory.py:73`'s `_categories()` already runs, defaulting to the categories this toolkit places (columns, framing, footings, floors/foundation slabs, walls, generic models) but not restricted to them — any placed category can be checked.
2. **Group by Category + TypeId** — only two instances of the *same* Type can be a true duplicate; two overlapping instances of different types is a real design decision (a haunch beam sharing space with a drop panel, say), not a mistake, so different-type overlaps are never flagged. Grouping mirrors the same category/type walk `structural/inventory.py:117`'s `_families()` already does over every `ElementType`, adapted here to instances.
3. **Signature match within each group**, picked by how the family is hosted:
   - Point-hosted (columns, generic models): compare `instance.Location.Point` pairwise at a tight default tolerance (2 mm) plus matching rotation and host level — the same property read already proven at `structural/columns.py:297`.
   - Curve-hosted (beams, framing, walls): compare `instance.Location.Curve` endpoint pairs (either order) at the same tolerance — the same read already proven at `structural/framing.py:258`.
   - Sketch-hosted (floors, foundation slabs): compare the boundary loop's vertex set after sort/round, extracted via the floor's `Sketch` element the exact way `ConvertFloor.pushbutton/script.py:390,397` already pulls one out (`el.GetDependentElements(DB.ElementClassFilter(DB.Sketch))`).
   - Anything else: fall back to `bounding_boxes_overlap()` itself (`revit/geometry.py:40`), called at a tight duplicate-detection tolerance rather than the loose join-check tolerance `BIMGeneration.pushbutton` uses it at today.
4. **Preview checklist**, one cluster per coincident group (2+ instances): the lowest-ElementId instance in each cluster is marked "keep" and shown unticked, every other instance in the cluster is marked "duplicate" and pre-ticked for deletion — same pre-ticked-safe-default convention `Bulk Purge`'s zero-usage rows and `Type Consolidator`'s drifted clusters both already use. Each duplicate row also shows a dependency count (hosted tags, dimensions, rebar) so a duplicate that actually carries something real hosted on it doesn't vanish as a surprise.
5. **Delete** — one `Transaction`, ticked duplicates deleted; result report ("Removed N duplicate instances across M clusters, K left for review") exportable to Excel via the same `openpyxl` write-back path `Export Schedule.pushbutton` / `BBS Generator.pushbutton` already use, giving the run a before/after audit trail.

**Implementation:**
- New `anongee_toolkit/operations/duplicate_finder.py`, parallel to the existing `operations/bulk_delete.py`: `group_by_category_type(doc, categories)` (adapts the walk `structural/inventory.py:117`'s `_families()` already runs), `find_clusters(group, tol_mm=2.0)` dispatching to point/curve/sketch/bbox comparators, and `merge(doc, clusters, keep="lowest_id")`.
- `bounding_boxes_overlap()` (`revit/geometry.py:40`) is imported directly from `anongee_toolkit.revit`, becoming this function's first real cross-category caller — `BIMGeneration.pushbutton/core.py:369`'s hand-rolled `_bounding_boxes_overlap()` is left untouched (it solves a different, still-valid problem: join tolerance, not duplicate tolerance), but this closes the gap of the shared helper never being used for the thing its own docstring ("bounding-box overlap tests") already promises.
- Point/curve location reads copy `structural/columns.py:297` (`instance.Location.Point`) and `structural/framing.py:258` (`instance.Location.Curve`) verbatim rather than re-deriving the `Location` cast pattern.
- Floor/slab sketch-boundary extraction reuses `ConvertFloor.pushbutton/script.py:390,397`'s `GetDependentElements(DB.ElementClassFilter(DB.Sketch))` call, the only place in the repo that has ever pulled a `Sketch` element back out of a placed floor.
- Dependency count per duplicate uses plain `GetDependentElements(None)`, the same unfiltered call already proven at `ConvertFloor.pushbutton/script.py:581`.
- Preview grid reuses `anongee_toolkit/ui/checklist.py`'s `CheckList`, the same CPython3-safe shell every other batch tool in this repo already builds its dialog on.
- New pushbutton bundle (`bundle.yaml`/`script.py`/`README.md`) added directly inside `AnonGee.tab/Essential.panel/Bulk Tool.stack/`, no new panel or stack needed.

**Future update:**
- A lower-confidence "possible duplicate" tier for near-but-not-exact matches (offset by a few centimetres rather than coincident) — flagged for review but unticked by default, catching a duplicate that was nudged slightly after being pasted.
- A cross-link mode: compare host-model instances against a selected linked model's transformed geometry, to catch the "linked model accidentally bound into the host" case named above, which today's same-document-only scope can't see.
- Feed confirmed clusters into the already-logged Warning Auditor as one more finding category once both tools exist, so a duplicate-instance sweep becomes part of the same batch-resolve pass as native Revit warnings instead of a separate button to remember.

## 2026-09-22 15:42 UTC

**Idea name:** Base Point & Survey Datum Auditor — an interactive, in-session pushbutton front end for the toolkit's own Project-Base-Point/Survey-Point offset engine, so a modeler can see and safely correct a model's dual-elevation datum without ever opening the Excel bridge

**Potential:**
This repo already has a small, carefully-written module that answers a question every structural team eventually gets wrong — "what does this level read on the survey/site drawing versus what Revit itself calls its elevation, and are those two numbers even talking about the same building?" `anongee_toolkit/structural/coordinates.py` (133 lines total) exists purely for this: its own module docstring states the problem outright — "So a level at 3.3 on a structural drawing and the same level at 103.3 on a site drawing are the same level, and the difference between them is a property of the model rather than of the sheet" (`coordinates.py:9-11`). `survey_offset_mm(doc)` (`coordinates.py:22-57`) reads `BasePoint.GetProjectBasePoint(doc)`/`BasePoint.GetSurveyPoint(doc)` and returns the live offset in millimetres, or `None` with a stated reason when it can't be read; `set_survey_offset_mm(doc, wanted_mm)` (`coordinates.py:60-124`) is the one function in the module that writes, and it does so carefully — unpinning the (pinned-by-default) survey point, writing `BASEPOINT_ELEVATION_PARAM`, then re-pinning it exactly as found (`coordinates.py:92-121`) — with the docstring stating why it is off by default: "moving a survey point re-coordinates a whole project… That is a decision somebody makes on purpose" (`coordinates.py:14-16`, repeated at `62-68`). But grepping the whole toolkit for `BasePoint`/`SurveyPoint` turns up exactly one real source file besides `coordinates.py` itself: its one and only caller, `anongee_toolkit/commands/levels.py` (`levels.py:162,170,175,187`) — the headless, bridge-only implementation of `levels.sync`. That command reads `SURVEY_OFFSET`/`APPLY_SURVEY_OFFSET` off an `INFO` sheet cell, and the actual dual-elevation reconciliation it feeds lives in `rc_automation/datum.py`'s `resolve_elevations()`, which converts a survey-measured row via `row.elevation_mm = row.elevation_survey_mm - survey_offset_mm` and flags a row that states both elevations and disagrees with the model's own offset via `expected = row.elevation_mm + survey_offset_mm` checked against `ELEVATION_TOLERANCE_MM`. This is not a theoretical gap: `todo-list.md`'s **BUG-25** (`testing`, line 779) is this exact mismatch happening in a real run — *"Reading the `FINDINGS` sheet from a real run: seven warnings, one per level, all saying the survey elevation disagreed. The model puts its survey point at the origin; the workbook says 100 m. One wrong cell, reported seven times."* — a discrepancy that only ever surfaced as a warning buried seven times over in an Excel `FINDINGS` sheet, and per `BUG-32` (line 335) that same sheet gets wiped by the next command run anyway. A modeler working directly in Revit — this toolkit's daily audience, per every one of the 45 already-logged pushbutton ideas — has no way to ask "is our base point/survey point relationship even sane" without that entire Excel/HTTP round trip; `AutoLevel.pushbutton` (the repo's one interactive level tool) reads and writes level elevations through its own planner, entirely in Project-Base terms, and never touches `BasePoint` at all. The already-logged **Link Position Guard** idea explicitly scoped this module out of its own remit, in its own words: *"`anongee_toolkit.structural.coordinates` looks adjacent but solves a different axis entirely… it says nothing about a link's XY position or rotation, which is a separate number tracking a separate problem"* — confirming this gap was seen and deliberately left for a later idea, not missed. On the market, native Revit offers no batch view of this at all: the Project Base Point and Survey Point are two separate, one-at-a-time Properties dialogs, and a level's Elevation Base (Project/Survey) toggle is set per level with no table anywhere comparing what each level reads under both. Coordinate-reconciliation tooling otherwise appears only as a minor feature bundled inside paid site/civil-coordination suites; nothing free gives a structural modeler a single table of every level's dual elevation, backed by the model's own live offset, with a guarded one-click way to correct it.

**Description:**
`Base Point Auditor.pushbutton`, new sibling to `RoundElementDist.pushbutton` in `Essential.panel/StrucBIM.stack` — the stack that already owns "read live datum/structural state, report first, approve each fix" tools, the posture `RoundElementDist.pushbutton`'s own tooltip states:

1. **Read** — on open, calls `coordinates.survey_offset_mm(doc)` for the model's live offset and note, and `structural.levels.levels(doc)` for every level (name, `ElementId`, Project elevation in mm) — the first interactive caller either function has ever had.
2. **Dual-elevation table** — one row per level: Project elevation (native), derived Survey elevation (`project_elevation_mm + offset_mm`, the same formula `datum.py` already uses to check agreement, run here in the open direction instead of buried in a headless diff), and the level's own Elevation Base toggle read off its type parameter, so a mismatch between what a level claims to be measured from and what the table shows is visible at a glance.
3. **State panel** — the Project Base Point's and Survey Point's raw elevation, pinned state, and whether either read as unreachable (`coordinates.py:42-43`'s "this model has no project base point or survey point" case reported plainly rather than silently skipped).
4. **Optional correction** — a modeler types one authoritative number (from a survey/site drawing) against the offset; the tool previews what every level's derived Survey elevation would become, and only on explicit confirm calls `coordinates.set_survey_offset_mm(doc, wanted_mm)` inside a named `Transaction`, reusing its existing unpin/set/re-pin sequence verbatim rather than reimplementing the pinned-element trap it already solved.
5. **Report only by default** — matching `set_survey_offset_mm`'s own documented restraint, the write step is opt-in and requires the same explicit confirmation `levels.sync`'s own `APPLY_SURVEY_OFFSET` switch already demands — here as a checklist tick rather than an Excel cell.
6. **Export** — the same `openpyxl` path every other batch tool in the repo already writes its report through, so a coordination-meeting record of "our survey offset was 0mm, corrected to 100000mm on 2026-09-xx" leaves a documented trail rather than living only in an Excel `FINDINGS` row that `BUG-32` will overwrite on the next bridge run.

**Implementation:**
- No new structural module needed for the core read/write path — this pushbutton's whole job is to be the first interactive caller of `anongee_toolkit.structural.coordinates.survey_offset_mm`/`set_survey_offset_mm` and `anongee_toolkit.structural.levels.levels`, all already written and currently reachable only through the bridge.
- New thin module `anongee_toolkit.structural.datum_report` for exactly one new piece of logic: `dual_elevation_rows(doc)` — zips `levels.levels(doc)` with `coordinates.survey_offset_mm(doc)` into display rows, plus a per-level read of the Elevation Base `BuiltInParameter` — since nothing today assembles Project+Survey elevation into one row for display rather than for a plan/diff pipeline.
- Correction step wraps `coordinates.set_survey_offset_mm` in a single `Transaction`, mirroring the transaction shape `commands/levels.py`'s survey-move handling already uses for the identical call, just interactive instead of envelope-driven.
- Same modeless window shell (`anongee_toolkit.ui.hostwnd`/`checklist`/`theme`) and `openpyxl` export path every other batch/audit tool in the repo already runs on; own `bundle.yaml`/`README.md`, tooltip modeled on `RoundElementDist.pushbutton`'s own "report first, approve each fix" phrasing since both tools share that exact posture.
- Sits in `Essential.panel/StrucBIM.stack`, next to `RoundElementDist.pushbutton`, `DrawFloor.pushbutton`, `ConvertFloor.pushbutton` — the stack that already owns single-document, live-datum, report-then-commit tools.

**Future update:**
- Extend the same table to every `RevitLinkInstance`'s Position mode (Auto-Origin/Auto-Center/By Shared Coordinates), so a modeler can see in one screen whether the model's own datum and every consultant link's positioning mode actually agree — a natural pairing with the already-logged Link Position Guard's transform-drift baseline, but answering "is coordination even configured correctly" rather than "did something move since we last checked."
- Wire the read-only half as its own bridge command (`datum.audit`) so a pre-flight check can run headless before `levels.sync`, catching a BUG-25-shaped disagreement before a workbook is even posted.
- A saved "expected survey elevation per level" list (from a civil/site drawing), so the table becomes pass/fail per level instead of a single flat model-wide offset, catching a level whose own type mistakenly overrides Elevation Base independently of the rest.
- Feed a confirmed offset correction into the already-logged Sheet Issue Kit's since-last-run diff, so a datum change shows up as "what changed since the last issue" alongside sheet and standards changes.

## 2026-09-23 00:42 UTC

**Idea name:** Schedule Drift Reconciler — wires up `anongee_toolkit.rc_automation.reconcile`, a fully-built comparison engine that has sat with zero real callers since it shipped, so a second `RC Automation` run on an already-placed footing or column actually checks cover, size and rotation against the schedule instead of stopping at "position and storey matched"

**Potential:**
`reconcile.py` (`AnonGee.extension/lib/py3/anongee_toolkit/rc_automation/reconcile.py`) is complete, not a stub: `Difference`/`Reconciliation` classes, `compare_footing()`/`compare_column()`/`compare_rebar()` (`:353-398`), `actionable_conflicts`/`geometric_conflicts` (`:234-242`), `resolved_values()` (`:295-297`), `issues_for()` (`:418-430`), `summarise()` (`:401-415`) — all shipped at `__version__ = "0.1.0"`. It is imported exactly once, at `rc_automation/__init__.py:24`, purely `from anongee_toolkit.rc_automation import reconcile  # noqa: F401` to keep the module importable — the `# noqa` is the tell. A repo-wide grep for its own API (`compare_footing`, `compare_column`, `compare_rebar`, `actionable_conflicts`, `resolved_values`, `issues_for`) turns up **zero real callers anywhere in `AnonGee.extension`**. The module's own PHASE 3 comment block (`:27-61`) states plainly "**None of it is implemented yet** -- today every geometric difference resolves to REPORT_ONLY" for `GEOMETRIC_FIELDS` (`length_mm, width_mm, thickness_mm, depth_mm, rotation_deg, outline`, `:90-91`), gated by `GEOMETRY_CHANGES_ARE_DEFERRED = True` (`:85`) — and RC Automation's own `todo-list.md` names exactly that half as **SCOPE-1** ("Phase 3, resolving a geometric difference · `pending`", line 304). But `actionable_conflicts` (`:239-242`, "Differences that can be resolved by setting a parameter" — cover fields, `diameter_mm`, `spacing_mm`, `count`) and `resolved_values()` need none of SCOPE-1's deferred sketch-edit/rehost machinery to apply safely — and that half isn't listed as pending anywhere, it is simply never called. `commands/columns.py`'s own module docstring states the actual gap outright: "**A second run must report `matched` and nothing else.** That is the acceptance test... The match is on **position and storey**, never on the mark" (`columns.py:8-11`). `commands/footings.py`'s `_existing(doc)` (`:43-65`) confirms it in code: it returns `[(ElementId, (x, y), level_name, type_name)]` — position, level, type name, nothing else; no cover, dimension or rotation is ever read back off a matched pad before the command calls it "matched." `reconcile.py`'s own worked example is exactly this: a footing "modelled 3200 wide against a schedule that says 3000" (`:5-6`) — under the current commands that footing sails through a second run reported no differently from one that agrees perfectly. RC Automation is explicitly meant to be re-run repeatedly as a schedule is revised — that is the entire reason a "second run" has an acceptance test — so a normal site reality (cover raised on an engineer's instruction, a bar count hand-edited after a site query) currently leaves zero trace anywhere the next run would see. No tool on the market reconciles a live Revit model back against a rerun schedule at the field level in this direction; paid rebar/detailing suites (AGACAD, Naviate) treat the schedule as generated *from* the model, not as the controlled document the model is checked against — this repo's own architecture already treats it the other way round (`reconcile.py:10`, "the schedule is the controlled document"), so this closes a gap in a design nothing else on the market makes in the first place.

**Description:**
No new pushbutton is the entry point — this rides inside `RC Automation.pushbutton`'s existing flow (`Dev.panel/col2.stack/RC Automation.pushbutton`), since it only has meaning as a second-run behaviour of `columns.sync`/`footings.sync`, not a standalone tool:

1. **Extend the match, not just position** — `commands/footings.py`'s `_existing()` and `commands/columns.py`'s equivalent read back the same fields their own `structural/footings.py`/`structural/columns.py` already write on creation (cover, length/width/thickness or width/depth, rotation) once a position/storey match is found, instead of stopping there.
2. **Compare** — feed the schedule row's values and the read-back model values through `reconcile.compare_footing()`/`compare_column()` (already built, already unit-aware in mm, already tolerant of noise via `DEFAULT_TOLERANCE_MM`) to get a `Reconciliation` per matched element.
3. **Apply the safe half now** — `Reconciliation.actionable_conflicts` go through `resolved_values()` and get written inside the command's existing `_apply()` transaction, Excel-wins-by-default per the module's own design (`SOURCE_EXCEL`, `:108-111`), with the per-row Excel/Model choice exposed as a checklist column rather than a silent overwrite.
4. **Report the deferred half honestly** — `Reconciliation.geometric_conflicts` (the SCOPE-1 territory) surface through the already-built `issues_for()` as `Info`-severity findings via the same report path every other RC Automation run already prints to, worded exactly as `describe()` already writes: "Reported only — length would have to change in the model, which this release does not do."
5. **Nothing is invented** — this is data flowing through machinery that already exists end to end (`Reconciliation`, `summarise()`, the report writer); the only new work is the handful of call sites in `commands/footings.py`/`commands/columns.py` that read back live values and hand them to a module that has been sitting complete and unused since it shipped.

**Implementation:**
- `commands/footings.py`: extend `_existing(doc)` (or add a sibling `_matched_values(doc, floor)`) to also read `cover_top_mm`/`cover_bottom_mm`/`cover_side_mm` and the sketch-derived length/width/thickness off the matched `Floor`, reusing the `ft_to_mm` conversion the function already imports (`footings.py:52`).
- `commands/columns.py`: same shape — read back `width_mm`/`depth_mm`/`cover_mm`/`rotation_deg` off the matched `FamilyInstance` before the acceptance-test "matched" path returns.
- Call `reconcile.compare_footing(placement, footing_type, model_values)` / `compare_column(...)` per matched row; both already exist and need no changes.
- Route `.actionable_conflicts` → `.resolved_values()` into the existing per-element write inside `_apply()`; route `.geometric_conflicts` into `models.Issue`s via `reconcile.issues_for()`, appended to the `findings` list `commands/_placement.py`'s `run()` already threads through to the report.
- `rc_automation/reconcile.py` itself needs zero changes — this is purely wiring an already-finished, already-unit-tested module into the two command modules that currently stop short of calling it.
- Footing rebar reconciliation (`compare_rebar`) can start immediately since footing rebar placement already ships; column rebar reconciliation waits on the already-logged Column Reinforcement Placer idea, since there is no placed column rebar to read back yet.

**Future update:**
- Once `GEOMETRY_CHANGES_ARE_DEFERRED` is eventually flipped off and SCOPE-1's `STRATEGY_SKETCH_EDIT`/`STRATEGY_RECREATE_AND_REHOST` are built, this same wiring is what feeds them — `Reconciliation.strategy(has_dependents)` already exists and is already called nowhere, so today's report-only wiring is the on-ramp that later work needs rather than a dead end.
- Extend `compare_rebar` into the same read-back-and-apply path once column reinforcement placement exists, so a hand-edited bar count or spacing on a placed column is caught the same way a hand-edited cover is.
- Surface `summarise()`'s counts (`using_excel`/`using_model`/`user_decided`/`report_only`) as a one-line summary in RC Automation's own run report, so "how much did this re-run actually change versus just re-confirm" is answered without reading every row.
- A per-row Excel/Model override, once chosen, could be written back into the workbook itself (a new column on the placement sheet) so a deliberate "model wins here" decision survives the next re-run instead of being asked again — closing the loop the already-logged Schedule Round-Trip idea opens from the read side.

## 2026-09-23 05:47 UTC

**Idea name:** Wall Level Splitter — cuts a single wall drawn with an Unconnected Height (or a Top Constraint set far above the next storey) into one properly base/top-constrained wall per level it actually passes through, the one wall-geometry rebuild this toolkit's own wall pipeline has never been asked to run in reverse

**Potential:**
Walls are the thinnest-covered structural category in this whole repo, and it is documented as a live gap rather than an oversight: `commands/` (`AnonGee.extension/lib/py3/anongee_toolkit/commands/`) has `columns.py`, `beams.py`, `footings.py`, `slabs.py`, `grids.py`, `levels.py`, `stairs.py`, `naming.py` — no `walls.py` at all — and `todo-list.md:1042`'s own status table records why: `| FEAT-12 | walls.sync | Medium | pending |`, the only structural placement row still `pending` while columns (FEAT-10), beams (FEAT-11), footings (FEAT-23) and slabs (FEAT-13) are all already `testing`. The one place in the repo that *does* touch walls, `cad2bim/builders/walls.py`, gets the exact problem this idea targets right by construction and never wrong: its own module docstring states plainly that "STRUCTURAL walls rise base-to-top like columns; ARCH walls run base level to the level above — **in a per-storey run the same pair**" (`cad2bim/builders/walls.py:21-24`), and `place_walls()` (`walls.py:231-299`) only ever calls `Wall.Create(doc, line, wall_type.Id, base_level.Id, height_ft, 0.0, False, structural)` (`walls.py:292-293`) with a height computed from one level to the very next, then constrains the top via `_attach_top()` (`walls.py:171-203`, writing `WALL_HEIGHT_TYPE`/`WALL_TOP_OFFSET`/`WALL_BASE_OFFSET`) — so no wall this toolkit itself has ever created spans more than one storey. A wall that *does* span several levels — an early schematic massing wall drawn at "Unconnected Height" before storeys were finalized, a shear/core wall pasted in from a consultant's model and bound, or one hand-modelled by a rushed junior who never split it — is therefore, by definition, a wall this toolkit built no tooling for at all: nobody has ever read one back. Confirmed by grep: `WALL_BASE_CONSTRAINT` and `WALL_USER_HEIGHT_PARAM` — the two `BuiltInParameter`s that tell you what level a wall actually starts from and whether its top is "Unconnected" — appear **zero times** anywhere in `AnonGee.extension` (`grep -rn "WALL_BASE_CONSTRAINT\|WALL_USER_HEIGHT_PARAM" AnonGee.extension --include=*.py` returns nothing); `_attach_top()` only ever *writes* `WALL_HEIGHT_TYPE` on a wall it just made, never reads one back off a wall that already exists. This is a real, recurring, and consequential modeling mistake, not a cosmetic one: a wall left unconnected across three storeys reports one Mark, one Volume, one row in a quantity schedule instead of three, cannot be phased or worksplit per floor, breaks any per-storey rebar/BBS take-off the moment reinforcement is eventually detailed against it, and is exactly the kind of thing `structural/inventory.py`'s own category counts (`_categories()`, `inventory.py:73`) would silently under-report a storey's worth of wall for. Native Revit gives no help here at all: "Split Element" only cuts a wall's *plan* length into two colinear pieces in a view — it has no notion of "levels" and does nothing to a wall's vertical Base/Top constraint, so it cannot solve this even conceptually. The Revit-community answer for years has been ad-hoc Dynamo graphs (a handful of "split wall by level" custom nodes circulate on forums and in third-party packages), which need Dynamo installed, a graph assembled or found and trusted per project, and give no per-wall preview before committing — not a one-click, no-Dynamo pyRevit button with a report-first safety net; paid whole-suite add-ins bundle "level normalization" as one line item inside a much larger licensed package (AGACAD- and Naviate-class tools), never as a free standalone. `wall_types(doc)` (`walls.py:63-86`) already draws exactly the category line this idea needs for free — it labels Curtain and Stacked wall types "(curtain/stacked, width not settable)" because they don't carry a resizeable compound structure — the same split that should exclude Curtain/Stacked walls from a level-splitter's v1, since each has its own, much harder, per-panel/per-type-segment story.

**Description:**
`Wall Level Splitter.pushbutton`, new sibling to `RoundElementDist.pushbutton` and `ConvertFloor.pushbutton` in `Essential.panel/StrucBIM.stack` — the stack that already owns "read live geometry, report first, approve each fix" batch-repair tools for exactly this audience:

1. **Scope + collect** — whole model, active view, or pre-selection; `OST_Walls`, restricted to Basic walls only (Curtain/Stacked excluded up front, the same split `wall_types()` already draws at `walls.py:63-86`).
2. **Detect spanning walls** — for each wall, read `WALL_BASE_CONSTRAINT` (+ `WALL_BASE_OFFSET`) for its real bottom elevation and, per `WALL_HEIGHT_TYPE`, either the Top Constraint level (+ `WALL_TOP_OFFSET`) or `WALL_USER_HEIGHT_PARAM` for its top — the exact pair of reads that, per the grep above, nothing in this repo has ever made on an existing wall. Cross the [bottom, top] interval against `anongee_toolkit.structural.levels.levels(doc)` (`structural/levels.py:19-28`, already sorted bottom-to-top): any wall whose interval strictly contains one or more level elevations is a spanning candidate.
3. **Flag-and-block unsupported cases up front**, the same "named reason, never silently mishandled" posture `ConvertFloor.pushbutton`/`Convert Column ⇄ Wall`/`Room Finish Auto-Fill` already established for their own unconvertible elements: a profile-edited wall (detected the same way `ConvertFloor.pushbutton/script.py:383-400`'s `get_sketch()` already pulls a dependent `Sketch` element off a floor, generalized here to a wall), and any wall carrying a hosted door/window/opening instance (`GetDependentElements` filtered to hosted `FamilyInstance`s) — both listed with why, left untouched, not attempted.
4. **Preview checklist** (`anongee_toolkit.ui.checklist.CheckList`, the same shell every other batch repair tool in the repo runs on) — one row per spanning wall, expandable into its proposed per-level split: level name, elevation range, and which segment inherits the original's base/top offset (only the bottom-most and top-most segments do; the rest sit flush on their own level).
5. **Split** — one `TransactionGroup`: before deleting anything, capture the wall's currently-joined neighbours via `JoinGeometryUtils.GetJoinedElements(doc, wall)` — the exact call `RoundElementDist.pushbutton`'s own union-find over joined clusters already uses (`RoundElementDist.pushbutton/script.py:24,52,376`) — then create one new wall per level with `Wall.Create()` against the identical location curve and wall type (mirroring `walls.py:292-293`'s call shape exactly), constrain each one's top the same way `_attach_top()` already does (`walls.py:171-203`), copy every non-read-only instance parameter from the original wall onto every new segment (new logic — `anongee_toolkit.revit.parameters` only ever reads/writes one named parameter at a time, `revit/parameters.py:12,36,85`; there is no existing "clone every instance parameter" helper to reuse), re-run `JoinGeometryUtils.JoinGeometry` against each captured neighbour per segment and report any that refuse (the same "a refusal is a sentence, not an exception" posture `_disallow_joins()` already models at `walls.py:220-227`), and only then delete the original wall.
6. **Report** — walls split / levels created / joins restored / joins that refused / walls blocked-with-reason, exportable via the same `openpyxl` path `Export Schedule.pushbutton`/`BBS Generator.pushbutton` already use.

**Implementation:**
- New `anongee_toolkit.structural.wall_levels` module: `spanning_walls(doc, level_rows)` (the two-parameter read plus the interval-vs-levels test above), `blockers(wall)` (profile-edit + hosted-opening checks), `split_wall(doc, wall, level_rows)` (join-capture → per-level `Wall.Create` → parameter copy → re-join → delete original).
- As a small first-commit side-fix: promote `cad2bim/builders/walls.py`'s private `_attach_top()` (`walls.py:171-203`) and `_disallow_joins()`/`WallUtils.DisallowWallJoinAtEnd` pattern (`walls.py:208-227`) into a shared `anongee_toolkit.revit.walls` module both `cad2bim` and this new tool import, rather than this tool re-deriving the same top-constraint-write logic a second time — the same "one function, not two silently-diverging copies" fix this log has already made for `bounding_boxes_overlap()` elsewhere.
- Reuses `anongee_toolkit.structural.levels.levels(doc)` (`structural/levels.py:19-28`) for level ordering, `ConvertFloor.pushbutton/script.py:383-400`'s `get_sketch()` pattern for profile-edit detection, and `RoundElementDist.pushbutton`'s proven `JoinGeometryUtils.GetJoinedElements` reads (`RoundElementDist.pushbutton/script.py:24,52,376`) for join capture/restore.
- Same modeless `CheckList`/`hostwnd`/`theme` window shell and `openpyxl` export path every other batch tool in the repo already runs on; own `bundle.yaml`/`README.md`.

**Future update:**
- Auto-handle hosted openings (delete-and-recreate the door/window on whichever new segment now contains its insertion elevation) instead of blocking them, once the recreate-on-correct-host pattern is proven safe elsewhere in the repo.
- Feed every newly-split wall straight into the already-logged Column Stack Auditor's own named future item — "Structural Walls acting as vertical stacks" — so continuity auditing finally has real per-level wall segments to walk instead of one unconnected multi-storey element it cannot cluster at all.
- Once FEAT-12's `walls.sync` ships, register this as a bridge-callable `walls.split` post-placement normalizer, the same "pushbutton capability exposed to the bridge" progression already logged for Naming Auditor.
- Extend the same spanning-detection idea to Structural Framing/Columns modelled with an oversized Unconnected Height instead of proper per-level constraints — the mirror-image of this mistake, one category over.

And `REVIT_API_RESEARCH.md`, the repo's own scratchpad for API feasibility notes, contains the word "beam" zero times — this surface has not even been scouted, let alone built, unlike the column path Column Reinforcement Placer could point to for `RebarHostData`/`SetPreferredConstraintForHandle` notes. Value-wise, beams are the highest-volume reinforced element in almost every RC frame building — every one needs top steel at supports, bottom steel at midspan, and stirrups closer near each end exactly the way a column needs closer ties near its ends — so a modeler currently hand-places every beam cage in every project this toolkit otherwise automates footings and (once built) columns for. On the market, schedule-driven beam cage generation with support/midspan curtailment exists only inside paid, seat-licensed rebar-detailing suites bundled with much larger BIM packages (AGACAD Rebar for Revit, Naviate Structure, BIM One's structural modules) — the same competitive set Column Reinforcement Placer already checked against — nothing free reads an Excel beam schedule and places a compliant cage.

1. **Match the beam instance** — for each row keyed by Type Mark on a new `BEAM_REBAR` sheet, index placed beams via `rebar_hosts.index_by_key(doc, rebar_hosts.BEAM_CATEGORY, key_parameter)` (`rebar_hosts.py:146-159`, already category-agnostic — every function in that module operates on a generic `element`, so pointing it at a new `BEAM_CATEGORY` constant needs no change beyond adding the constant itself). No new placement sheet is needed: the beam's own geometry (span, width, depth) is read directly off the already-modelled `FamilyInstance` — its `LocationCurve` for the span, and its type's `b`/`h` parameters via the exact `WIDTH_NAMES`/`DEPTH_NAMES` lookup `structural/framing.py:22-23` already defines for beam type-duplication — rather than trusting a second, possibly-stale workbook copy of the section size, the same "measure the model, don't trust the sheet" posture `structure_run._sized_as_scheduled()` already takes for footings.

## 2026-09-24 05:39 UTC

**Idea name:** Level Rehost — moves structural elements onto a different host level without moving them in space (it recalculates every offset so the element's absolute elevation stays exactly where it was), plus a "Vacate Level" mode that empties a stray or duplicate level so it can finally be deleted safely. The toolkit's own level engine refuses to delete such levels, and this repo has never given the modeler a way to empty one

**Potential:**
The toolkit already knows that an element on the wrong level is a real, costly mistake. It says so in its own docstrings. `structural/framing.py`'s `create()` says it outright: "The line is drawn at the level's own elevation and the offset applied through the parameter, not by drawing the line higher: **a beam drawn high is a beam whose reference level is wrong, and every schedule that groups by level says so**" (`framing.py:130-133`). `todo-list.md`'s BUG-29 (`todo-list.md:694-706`) is the field incident behind that sentence: "a beam on `06 TERRACE LVL.` with `Start Level Offset` 0, `End Level Offset` 0, and **`z Offset Value` −1500.000**", where "−1500 is the foundation level — a value from somewhere else in the model that the API happily carried into ninety beams". The fix it records is only preventive. `_state_the_justification()` (`framing.py:190-216`) now writes `Z_OFFSET_VALUE` = 0 and puts the sheet's offset into `STRUCTURAL_BEAM_END0_ELEVATION`/`END1_ELEVATION` (`framing.py:209-216`) on beams the toolkit *creates*. Nothing in the repo ever goes back to an *existing* element to check or correct its level/offset relationship.

The level engine goes further and tells the modeler to leave the mess in place. `rc_automation/datum.py:10-12` gives the first of its four rules: "*Never delete.* A level in the model that the sheet does not mention is left alone and reported. **Deleting a level takes every view, every sheet reference and every element hosted on it**, and no spreadsheet is worth that". `plan_levels()` (`datum.py:538-544`) then emits exactly that Info issue ("the model has {0!r} at {1:g} mm and the sheet does not mention it — left alone") for every unclaimed level. `commands/levels.py:16-19` repeats the promise ("it never deletes a level the sheet omits, never renames one"). The "never create a second level at an occupied elevation" rule (`datum.py:21-24`) likewise ends in a report and hands the fix to "somebody... on purpose". So every `levels.sync` run correctly reports stray levels: a template's `Level 0`, a pasted-in architect's `FFL +3000` sitting 50 mm off the structural `SFL +2950`, or a `Level 2(1)` left by a copy-paste between models. The toolkit then gives the modeler no way to empty such a level so it *can* be deleted without taking its columns, beams and slabs with it.

The gap is total in code, not partial. The repo has at least six independent *readers* of an element's host level, each with a different fallback order:
- `framing._level_name()`: `INSTANCE_REFERENCE_LEVEL_PARAM` → `FAMILY_LEVEL_PARAM` → `SCHEDULE_LEVEL_PARAM` (`framing.py:328-341`)
- `columns._level_name()`: `FAMILY_BASE_LEVEL_PARAM` → `SCHEDULE_BASE_LEVEL_PARAM` (`columns.py:362-375`)
- `rebar_hosts.level_name()`: `LEVEL_PARAM` → `FAMILY_BASE_LEVEL_PARAM` → `SCHEDULE_LEVEL_PARAM` (`rebar_hosts.py:122-135`)
- `BBS Generator`'s `_floor_level_name()` (`core/rebar_reader.py:85-112`)
- `FramewinToBIM`'s five-parameter tuple (`FramewinToBIM.pushbutton/script.py:1002-1006`)
- `stairs.py`'s `stairs.LevelId` read (`stairs.py:395`)

None of them ever *writes* a level back onto an existing element. The only level/offset writes in the extension happen at creation time: `columns.create()` (`columns.py:270-275`), `framing._state_the_justification()`, `footings.sit_on_level()` (`footings.py:293-313`), `cad2bim/builders/walls.py:185-200`, and `ConvertFloor`'s copy of `FLOOR_HEIGHTABOVELEVEL_PARAM` from source to new slab (`ConvertFloor.pushbutton/script.py:1301,1336`). `ConvertFloor` keeps the same `level_id` it read (`:1299-1300`) and so never changes it. A repo-wide grep for `WALL_BASE_CONSTRAINT` returns nothing. `WALL_BASE_OFFSET` appears exactly once, at `cad2bim/builders/walls.py:192`, and there it is only ever *zeroed* on a freshly made wall. Because six modules read level membership and it drives grouping in every downstream output (BBS Generator groups bars by `SCHEDULE_LEVEL_PARAM`, per its own defaults at `BBS Generator.pushbutton/ui/main_window.py:300`; `Export Schedule` and every per-storey quantity schedule group by level), a wrongly-hosted element silently lands in the wrong floor's quantities, the wrong floor's BBS sheet, and the wrong floor's plan-view visibility.

Native Revit makes this a manual chore. Changing an element's level in the Properties palette keeps the offset as a number relative to the *new* level. For floors, columns and walls, the element therefore physically jumps by the level-to-level distance, and the modeler has to work out and type the compensating offset by hand, one element (or one same-offset selection) at a time. For columns there are two level/offset pairs to fix, and for beams there are two end offsets. Deleting a level gives only a count warning before it takes everything hosted on it. A "change level, keep position" command exists as a line item inside paid Revit productivity bundles and as ad-hoc community Dynamo scripts that need Dynamo, a found-and-trusted graph, and no preview. Nothing free does it as a one-click, report-first pyRevit button. Nothing anywhere pairs it with a "vacate this level, then delete it" flow that knows about structural offset conventions such as this repo's own rule that sunk beams keep their drop in `Z_OFFSET_VALUE`, not in the level offsets (`cad2bim/builders/beams.py:40-50`).

This is an everyday tool, not an occasional one. It applies whenever an architect's level set is merged or replaced, a template level is retired, a storey is renamed or split into SFL/FFL pairs, or a junior models a whole floor's beams on the level below with +3000 offsets.

**Description:**
`Level Rehost.pushbutton` is a new sibling to `RoundElementDist.pushbutton`, `ConvertFloor.pushbutton` and `DrawFloor.pushbutton` in `Essential.panel/StrucBIM.stack`, the stack that already owns "read live geometry, preview, then fix" batch repair tools. It has three modes in one window:

1. **Rehost selection.** Pre-select or pick elements: Structural Columns, Structural Framing, Floors (slabs and floor-based footings, per `footings.sit_on_level()`'s convention), Structural Foundations, Walls and Generic Models. Then pick a target level from `anongee_toolkit.structural.levels.levels(doc)` (`structural/levels.py:19-28`, already sorted lowest first with elevations in mm). For each element the tool computes, per level/offset pair, `new_offset = old_offset + (old_level_elevation − new_level_elevation)`:
   - Columns: base and top pairs independently (`FAMILY_BASE_LEVEL_PARAM`/`FAMILY_BASE_LEVEL_OFFSET_PARAM`, `FAMILY_TOP_LEVEL_PARAM`/`FAMILY_TOP_LEVEL_OFFSET_PARAM`, the exact four parameters `columns.create()` writes at `columns.py:270-275`), with an option to rehost only the base, only the top, or both.
   - Framing: `INSTANCE_REFERENCE_LEVEL_PARAM` plus both `STRUCTURAL_BEAM_END0/END1_ELEVATION`.
   - Floors: `LEVEL_PARAM` plus `FLOOR_HEIGHTABOVELEVEL_PARAM`.
   - Walls: `WALL_BASE_CONSTRAINT` plus `WALL_BASE_OFFSET`, and the top constraint if it is attached.
2. **Auto-rehost ("nearest governing level").** No target is picked. Each element is re-assigned to the level its *actual* reference elevation sits on or just above: the top of a beam, the top of a slab, or the base of a column. That elevation is measured from the element itself, not from its current level. This fixes the BUG-29 class of problem in bulk: a whole floor of beams modelled on the level below with a +3000 offset comes back on the correct level with a 0 offset, and every per-level schedule, BBS sheet and plan view starts agreeing with the building.
3. **Vacate Level.** Pick a level, typically one `levels.sync` just reported as "left alone" (`datum.py:538-544`). The tool lists everything hosted on it, grouped by category, and every plan/ceiling view whose `GenLevel` is that level. It rehosts every element onto a chosen replacement level using the Mode 1 compensation, then reports "0 elements remain on `Level 0`; 3 views will be deleted with it". Deleting the level itself is a separate, explicitly confirmed checkbox. It is never automatic, which honours the spirit of `datum.py`'s "never delete" rule: the tool makes deletion *safe*, and the decision stays with a person.
4. **Preview first.** A checklist (`anongee_toolkit.ui.checklist.CheckList`, `ui/checklist.py:64`) shows one row per element: category, Mark, current level + offset, proposed level + offset, and the resulting absolute elevation, which should equal the current one. Rows the tool will not touch are listed with a named reason, not skipped silently. The reasons reuse `RoundElementDist.pushbutton`'s `skip_reason()` vocabulary (`RoundElementDist.pushbutton/script.py:708-739`: linked, pinned, inside a group, stacked/curtain wall) and add two more: "parameter read-only on this family" and "sloped/slope-arrow floor — offset is not a single number".
5. **Apply and verify by measurement.** Everything runs in one `RevitTransactionGroup` (`revit/transactions.py:117`), so a single Ctrl+Z undoes it. Before writing, the tool records each element's bounding-box Min.Z/Max.Z (and, for curve-based elements, its `LocationCurve` endpoints). After `doc.Regenerate()` it reads them again, and any element whose absolute position moved by more than `datum.ELEVATION_TOLERANCE_MM` (0.5 mm, `datum.py:38`) is rolled back to its captured level/offset and reported. The tool never trusts Revit's per-category behaviour when changing a level; it measures, so an unexpected family or category behaviour becomes a sentence in the report, not a silently moved slab.
6. **Explicit offset conventions.** `Z_OFFSET_VALUE` is never touched. `cad2bim/builders/beams.py:40-50` records the owner's instruction that a sunk beam's drop lives there "only... not the Start/End Level Offset". Compensation always goes into the level offsets, so a sunk beam is still sunk after rehosting.
7. **Report.** The report lists rehosted, rolled back and blocked-with-reason counts per category, plus the vacated-level summary. It can be exported through the same `openpyxl` path `Export Schedule.pushbutton`/`BBS Generator.pushbutton` already use.

**Implementation:**
- **New `anongee_toolkit.structural.rehost` module.**
  - A `HOST_PAIRS` table per category holds the `(level BuiltInParameter, offset BuiltInParameter)` pairs listed above, with two pairs for columns, two end offsets sharing one level for framing, and one pair for floors and walls.
  - `host_pairs(element)` reads current level ids and offsets.
  - `plan_rehost(element, target_level_id, level_rows)` returns a pure-arithmetic plan of new offsets in mm and needs no transaction.
  - `governing_level(elevation_mm, level_rows)` handles Mode 2. It is a bisect over the already-sorted `levels.levels(doc)` output, and it is the one Revit-free function, unit-testable the same way `rc_automation/datum.py` is tested off Revit.
  - `apply_rehost(doc, element, plan)` does the writes. It sets the level first and the offset second, both in feet via `anongee_toolkit.revit.units.mm_to_ft` (`units.py:28`).
- **New `vacate_candidates(doc, level_id)`.** A `FilteredElementCollector` pass over the categories in `HOST_PAIRS`, filtered by the same level parameter reads, plus `ViewPlan`s whose `GenLevel.Id` matches. It feeds Mode 3's preview and the "N views go with it" warning.
- **Side fix in the first commit.** Promote a single `anongee_toolkit.revit.levels.host_level_id(element)` reader, built from the fallback order in `rebar_hosts.level_name()` (`rebar_hosts.py:122-135`) extended with `INSTANCE_REFERENCE_LEVEL_PARAM` and `WALL_BASE_CONSTRAINT`. Point `framing._level_name()` (`framing.py:328-341`) and `columns._level_name()` (`columns.py:362-375`) at it, so "what level is this on" has one answer instead of the six diverging fallback orders listed above. This is the same "one function, not silently-diverging copies" clean-up this log has already proposed for `_attach_top()` and `bounding_boxes_overlap()`.
- **Reused as they are.**
  - `levels.levels(doc)` (`structural/levels.py:19-28`) for level ordering and elevations.
  - `RevitTransactionGroup`/`RevitTransaction` (`revit/transactions.py:71,117`).
  - `CheckList` (`ui/checklist.py:64`) for the preview.
  - `RoundElementDist.pushbutton`'s `skip_reason()` pattern (`script.py:708-739`) for block reasons.
  - `datum.ELEVATION_TOLERANCE_MM` (`datum.py:38`) as the verify tolerance, so "didn't move" means the same thing here as "same elevation" means to `levels.sync`.
  - The shared `ui.hostwnd`/`theme` shell and `openpyxl` export path.
- **Packaging.** Own `bundle.yaml`/`README.md` and an icon matching its `StrucBIM.stack` siblings.

**Future update:**
- Register `levels.vacate` as a bridge command next to `levels.sync`. Its Info issue for an unclaimed level (`datum.py:538-544`) could then carry a "vacate onto →" suggestion using the nearest claimed level, turning a report line into a one-click follow-up without weakening `datum.py`'s "never delete" rule.
- Extend Mode 2's governing-level detection into a read-only "Level Hygiene" audit pass across the whole model. It would flag every element whose level offset is larger than one storey height, the exact signature of BUG-29 and the "beam drawn high" warning in `framing.py:130-133`, without changing anything.
- Rebar follows its host. After a host moves level, re-read hosted `Rebar`'s `SCHEDULE_LEVEL_PARAM` (the parameter BBS Generator groups by) and report any bar that did not follow, so a rehosted footing's cage lands on the same BBS sheet as the footing.
- Support sloped floors and slope-arrow slabs by compensating both the level offset and the arrow's tail/head offsets, once the flat case is proven in production.
- Feed the whole-floor case into the logged Column Stack Auditor. Once columns are consistently base-hosted on their true storey, its "level-chain gap/overlap" check stops producing false positives caused by columns that are the right height but on the wrong level.

## 2026-09-25 05:52 UTC

**Idea name:** Assembly Batch-Builder — groups repeated structural elements (a precast column run, a repeated footing type, a stair flight) into native Revit `AssemblyInstance`s in one pass, with auto-named assembly types and auto-generated assembly views/sheets, closing the one Revit-native fabrication mechanism this toolkit has only ever treated as an obstacle to route around, never a feature to build

**Potential:**
Revit's own Assembly feature (`AssemblyInstance`, assembly types, assembly views, assembly-level schedules — the mechanism behind precast/prefab fabrication drawings, "shop ticket" sheets and piece-mark tracking) has **zero creation, zero read and zero management code anywhere** in this repository. A repo-wide grep for `AssemblyInstance`, `CreateAssemblyInstance` and `AssemblyViewUtils` returns nothing outside vendored `RevitAPI`/`RevitAPIUI` stub references pulled in by the pyRevit engine itself; every other hit for the word "assembly" in the codebase is the unrelated .NET/CLR sense — "Duplicate type name within an assembly", the recurring pythonnet load-order gotcha `REVIT_API_RESEARCH.md` documents and roughly ten different pushbuttons' own exception handlers already work around — or the one mention in `todo-list.md:1059`, FEAT-19 "Shop drawing assembly", which is about `Dimension.Create` needing stable `Reference`s for shop-drawing dimensioning, not Revit's `AssemblyInstance` object at all.

The single place the codebase does touch assembly membership treats it as purely defensive, never generative: `ConvertFloor.pushbutton/script.py:1176-1179`
```python
if el.AssemblyInstanceId is not None and eid_val(el.AssemblyInstanceId) > 0:
    p.blockers.append("inside an assembly")
```
and `ConvertFloor.pushbutton/README.md:81` lists exactly that outcome under its own "Blocked (never attempted)" section: **"Element inside an assembly."** So the toolkit's only assembly-awareness, anywhere, is "refuse to touch it" — nothing in the extension has ever *created* one, and a modeler at this firm builds every fabrication assembly the fully manual, one-at-a-time native way: select elements, right-click → Create Assembly, type a name, open Manage → Assembly Views to make each view, then place each on its own sheet by hand. For a precast column run of forty identical pieces, or a repeated footing type poured across a whole grid, that is forty (or more) repetitions of a five-step manual sequence Revit has no batch command for at all.

None of the 55 ideas already logged in this file touch Assemblies, assembly views, or precast/prefab grouping — the closest neighbor, Pour Break Manager, deliberately works one level below this (splitting a single host's geometry across a pour joint via `PartUtils.CreateParts`, a different API surface it explicitly claims as unclaimed for itself), and none of Column Stack Auditor, Type Consolidator, Rebar Cage Copier or Duplicate Instance Finder & Merger ever group elements into a fabrication unit — they classify, audit or copy onto existing elements, never assemble new ones. This is exactly the workflow profile this firm already has in volume: RC Automation and BBS Generator template and repeat footings, columns and stairs across a whole structure, and cad2bim recreates that same repetition from CAD imports — every one of those pipelines produces the kind of repeated, same-type element run that a precast or fast-track fabrication schedule wants grouped into assemblies, and nothing downstream of placement ever does that grouping. On the market, no paid Revit add-in (Ideate, CTC BIM Project Suite, Naviate) ships bulk/batch assembly creation with auto-generated views and sheets — batch assembly-building is otherwise done one Dynamo graph at a time, which needs Dynamo installed, a graph someone trusts, and gives no preview before it commits.

**Description:**
`Assembly Batch-Builder.pushbutton` lands as a new sibling in `Essential.panel/StrucBIM.stack`, reusing the same "read live geometry, preview, then commit" shape every batch tool in that stack already runs on.

1. **Grouping rule.** The modeler picks one of three ways to cluster elements into assemblies: by a shared parameter value already on the elements (`Mark`/`Type Mark`, or a house `Assembly Group` shared parameter if one exists), by hand-picked selection sets applied one cluster at a time, or by mutual proximity — reusing the same `BoundingBoxIntersectsFilter`-driven "find what else is touching/overlapping this" pattern `JoinPriority.pushbutton`'s own overlap detector already runs (`JoinPriority.pushbutton/script.py:256-295`), generalized from "find a joint partner" to "find everything in this cluster" the same way the already-logged Footing Combiner idea proposes generalizing it for pad-merging.
2. **Preview.** Each proposed assembly is one row on the shared `CheckList` shell (`anongee_toolkit/ui/checklist.py:64`): element count and categories, the proposed assembly type name, and a naming-collision warning when that name is already taken by an unrelated assembly type elsewhere in the model. Nothing is created until rows are ticked and confirmed.
3. **Apply.** For each ticked cluster, `AssemblyInstance.Create(doc, elementIds, categoryId)` runs inside one `RevitTransaction` (`anongee_toolkit/revit/transactions.py:71`) per assembly, wrapped in one `RevitTransactionGroup` (`transactions.py:117`) for the whole batch so a single Ctrl+Z undoes everything — the same per-item-transaction, whole-run-group posture every converter tool in this repo already uses. Naming a new assembly type reuses the "try the wanted name, fall back to a numbered variant, report which happened and why" discipline `structural/typemaker.py`'s `duplicate()` already establishes for type creation (`typemaker.py:170-226`, returning `(symbol, made, note)` so a caller can always tell a fresh type from a reused one) — applied here to assembly-type naming instead of family-type naming, so two clusters that would otherwise collide on the same generated name get an honest, reported numbered suffix instead of a silent Revit-side rejection.
4. **Views and sheets (optional, per run).** For each newly created assembly, optionally call `AssemblyViewUtils.CreateDetailSectionView`/`Create3DOrthographic` to generate its standard section/elevation/3D views, then drop them onto an auto-created assembly sheet — reusing the sheet-and-viewport placement plumbing the already-logged Sheet Set Builder and Sheet Issue Kit ideas both build for their own batch sheet creation, rather than standing up a second sheet-placement code path.
5. **Report.** Lists elements skipped because they were already inside another assembly (the exact `ConvertFloor` blocker condition, now surfaced as an actionable "already grouped, skipped" line instead of a dead end), elements skipped because they crossed a design-option or worksharing boundary, and every assembly actually created, with its element count and generated view/sheet names.

**Implementation:**
- New `anongee_toolkit/structural/assemblies.py`: `cluster_by_parameter()`, `cluster_by_proximity()` (the promoted, category-agnostic version of `JoinPriority.pushbutton`'s inline `BoundingBoxIntersectsFilter` loop — the same promotion-to-shared-helper this repo's own Footing Combiner write-up already calls for), and `build_assembly(doc, element_ids, category_id, preferred_name)` wrapping `AssemblyInstance.Create` plus the `typemaker`-style naming fallback.
- `AssemblyViewUtils` calls (`CreateDetailSectionView`, `Create3DOrthographic`, `GetAllValidTypesForAssemblyLocation` for verifying the assembly type is a valid target before naming it) live in the same new module — this is the first call site for that API surface anywhere in the repo, so it gets its own small wrapper the way every other never-before-called API surface in this toolkit (`PartUtils` for Pour Break Manager, `DesignOption` for Design Option Scheme Comparator) gets one.
- `Assembly Batch-Builder.pushbutton/script.py` wires the three clustering modes to the shared `CheckList` shell and the sheet-placement helpers already built for Sheet Set Builder/Sheet Issue Kit, follows the same version-bump-and-`CHANGELOG.md` convention every other pushbutton in the repo follows, and ships its own `bundle.yaml`/`README.md`/icon matching its `StrucBIM.stack` siblings.
- One small, additive change outside the new module: `ConvertFloor.pushbutton/script.py:1176-1179`'s blocker message gains a one-line mention that `Assembly Batch-Builder` can be used to un-assemble first (`AssemblyInstance.Disassemble` or `Document.Delete` on the `AssemblyInstance`, still leaving the member elements intact) — a pointer, not a behavior change, so the existing "inside an assembly" refusal keeps its meaning.

**Future update:**
- `AssemblyInstanceId`-aware skip logic, once this exists, becomes the natural home for un-assembling: a "Disassemble" mode listing every assembly in the model with its member count, so a modeler can dissolve a wrongly-grouped assembly without hunting for it in the project browser first.
- Assembly-level schedules: once assemblies exist in the model, wire a thin schedule generator (reusing `Schedule Builder`'s already-logged `ViewSchedule`-from-spec engine) scoped to `Category == OST_Assemblies`, giving a native piece-count/piece-mark schedule for free.
- Feed newly-built assemblies into `Sheet Issue Kit`'s since-last-run diff, so a re-issue of fabrication sheets only touches the assemblies that actually changed since the last shop-drawing set went out.
- Tie `Assembly Group` shared-parameter values back into `BBS Generator`'s own grouping so a precast piece's rebar cage schedule and its fabrication assembly report under the same piece mark, instead of two independently-numbered systems for the same physical unit.
