## 2026-09-25 15:40 UTC

**Idea name:** Family/Type Swap Wizard — batch-replaces every instance of one Family/Type (or a whole saved list of family pairs run in one pass) with a different, unrelated Family/Type anywhere in the model, in any category, carrying over location, host, level, rotation and every parameter it can match — the one migration step Family Health Auditor's own "Duplicate family clusters" finding stops short of, because that finding is explicitly logged as "report-only, no safe auto-fix"

**Potential:**
A repo-wide grep for `NewFamilyInstance` returns exactly five call sites, all of them category-locked structural creators — `structural/columns.py:267`, `structural/framing.py:158`, and three inside `cad2bim/builders/{columns,beams}.py` — and none of them is a generic "recreate this instance as a different family/type at the same place" helper; each one only ever *creates new* elements from scratch during an INP/DXF/RC-Automation import, never *replaces* an element that already exists in the model. There is no generic instance-swap helper anywhere in `anongee_toolkit`, only these import-time creators.

The gap this leaves is one this file has already named twice without closing. **Family Health Auditor** (line 490) builds a "Duplicate family clusters" report — two differently-named families that are actually the same geometry, e.g. a consultant's door re-imported under a different name than the firm's own — and its own write-up says plainly (line 500): *"Duplicate family clusters (... which one the report proposes as canonical by instance count) plus a fourth, safely-actionable section: Fully unused families ... report-only, no safe auto-fix."* Its own **Implementation** section (line 508) only ever gets as far as computing the signature that *finds* the duplicate; nothing in that idea, or anywhere else in the repo, ever executes the merge. **Type Consolidator** (line 1222) looks like the same problem solved already, but it isn't: it "merge[s] every instance onto one canonical type" only when both types already belong to the **same Family** and share an identical compound-structure/parameter signature (`ConvertFloor.pushbutton/script.py:704-759`'s `cs_signature()`, lifted verbatim) — it operates one level below Family, and has nothing to say about two instances of genuinely *different* Families that happen to model the same thing. `Convert Slab` and the logged **Convert Column ⇄ Wall** (line 1484) solve a structurally related problem — recreate across a category boundary — but both are hard-coded, single-category-pair, sketch-rebuild engines built for exactly two structural conversions; neither is a general "any Family, any category" swap, and neither was meant to be one.

Revit's own Type Selector cannot help either: it swaps Types *within* the same Family only. Crossing a Family boundary — the firm's own door family for a consultant's, a placeholder generic-model block for the final loadable family before issue, or migrating a whole legacy project's furniture/window library onto a new firm template after a merger — is, today, a fully manual per-instance cycle: note the location/host/level, delete, place the new family, retype every parameter by hand, repeat for every instance in the model. On the market this exact capability — cross-family instance replace with a reusable, saved parameter-mapping table — is sold only as one paid line item inside enterprise whole-model suites (Ideate Sidekick's family tools, CTC BIM Tools); nothing free does a general, any-category, saved-mapping family swap.

**Description:**
`Family Swap Wizard.pushbutton` is a new sibling to `Parameter Combine.pushbutton` in `Advance.panel` — it is model-wide parameter-and-placement work across many elements, not structural-category-specific, so it belongs next to the other cross-category batch tools rather than in `StrucBIM.stack`.

1. **Build or load a mapping row.** Pick a source Family + Type already present in the model, and a target Family + Type (loading the target family into the project first if it isn't already there). Add as many rows as needed in one run — a whole library migration is one mapping table, not one run per family pair.
2. **Scan.** `FilteredElementCollector` finds every instance of each source Type — pre-selection if there is one, otherwise the whole model — the same "pre-select, else prompt, else whole model" convention `ConvertFloor.pushbutton` already uses.
3. **Preview.** One `CheckList` row per instance: category, location/level/host, and a per-parameter transfer forecast computed by running `find_target_param` (`ConvertFloor.pushbutton/script.py:866-886`, the exact `BuiltInParameter → shared GUID → name` chain six other tools in this repo already rely on) against the *target* symbol's parameter set — so the modeler sees, before anything happens, exactly which parameters will carry over and which have no counterpart on the new family, per mapping row.
4. **Apply.** For each ticked instance: activate the target `FamilySymbol` if needed, call `doc.Create.NewFamilyInstance` at the source instance's own location/host/level/rotation/facing-orientation, run `copy_instance_params`'s matching logic (`script.py:889-929`, generalized to take an arbitrary target element instead of one hard-coded to floors/foundations) to carry parameters across, then delete the original — one `RevitTransaction` per instance inside one `RevitTransactionGroup` (`revit/transactions.py:71,117`), so a single Ctrl+Z undoes the whole run and one bad instance never blocks the rest of the batch.
5. **Save/load the mapping.** The mapping table (source family+type → target family+type) persists to JSON next to the project, or via the same `openpyxl` round-trip `Export Schedule.pushbutton`/`BBS Generator.pushbutton` already use — so the exact same migration replays identically across every project still carrying the old family.
6. **Report.** Per mapping row: instances swapped, instances skipped (nested/hosted-on-a-face instance whose host isn't in the run, inside a group or assembly — reusing `ConvertFloor`'s own blocker vocabulary), and every parameter name that had no counterpart on the target family, so nothing silently disappears.

**Implementation:**
- New `anongee_toolkit/operations/family_swap.py`, sitting next to the already-existing `operations/bulk_rename.py` and `operations/bulk_delete.py` (`operations/` is an established home for cross-category, non-structural-specific batch engines in this repo).
- `find_target_param`/`copy_instance_params` (`ConvertFloor.pushbutton/script.py:866-929`) get promoted into a shared helper both buttons call, rather than forking a second copy — the same "one function, not a diverging copy" clean-up this file has already called for elsewhere (Level Rehost's `host_level_id()` promotion, line 1820).
- `swap_instance(doc, old_instance, target_symbol)` wraps the generic recreate: reads `Location`/`Host`/`LevelId`/rotation off the old instance (branching on `LocationPoint` vs `LocationCurve` the same way `structural/columns.py:267` and `structural/framing.py:158` already do for their own single-category cases, but without the structural-only assumptions), places the new instance, runs the promoted parameter-copy helper, deletes the old instance.
- Mapping persistence reuses the same JSON convention `ConvertFloor.pushbutton/slab_convert_config.json` already establishes for this toolkit's config files.
- Reused as-is: `CheckList` (`ui/checklist.py:64`), `RevitTransactionGroup`/`RevitTransaction` (`revit/transactions.py:71,117`), the `openpyxl` export path, and the shared `ui.hostwnd`/`theme` shell every other dialog-driven button already uses.
- Own `bundle.yaml`/`README.md`/icon in `Advance.panel`, next to `Parameter Combine.pushbutton`.

**Future update:**
- Wire directly into Family Health Auditor's "Duplicate family clusters" report: a "Swap onto canonical →" button on that report becomes a one-row mapping fed straight into this tool, finally closing the gap that idea's own write-up flags as unfixed.
- Extend `swap_instance` to system-family-hosted instances (a wall-hosted window swapped for a different wall-hosted window family) once the loadable-family case is proven, since the host-preservation logic is the same either way.
- Batch-import a whole mapping table from one Excel sheet, so a firm-wide library migration (every legacy project touched by a merger) runs as one spreadsheet instead of one run per project.
- Feed swap counts into a per-project "library migration progress" tracker across a portfolio of files, reusing the same report format `Sheet Issue Kit`'s since-last-run diff already produces.

## 2026-09-27 05:44 UTC

**Idea name:** Group Drift Auditor — walks every `GroupType` with more than one placed instance, flags the instances whose real member composition has quietly diverged from the others (an excluded beam, a swapped fixture, a locally-modified copy nobody flagged as different), and separately finds the near-identical `GroupType` clusters that Revit's own ungroup→modify→regroup cycle forks into siblings, offering a preview-gated consolidation back onto one canonical type

**Potential:**
`ConvertFloor.pushbutton/script.py:957-969` carries a code comment explaining a real Revit API limitation the author had to design around rather than fix: *"There is no API to edit a group's definition in place... A GroupType with SEVERAL instances does not [round-trip cleanly]. Ungrouping one instance leaves the others untouched... and regrouping produces a NEW type rather than editing the shared one. The group type splits. That is not a bug this script can engineer around — it is the shape of the API — so multi-instance groups are blocked by default."* That single sentence describes a failure mode every BIM modeler who works with repeated Model Groups (a typical toilet room, a repeated stair core, a structural bay template) eventually causes by hand: pick one placed instance, edit it slightly (add a note, swap a fixture, exclude one member for a site condition), and Revit silently either mutates every other instance sharing that type along with it (surprising, if the edit was meant to be local) or, the moment anyone ungroups-and-regroups to make a local change stick, forks a new `GroupType` with an auto-numbered name (`Toilet Core 2`, `Toilet Core 3`...) that nothing distinguishes from the original in the Project Browser except a number nobody remembers the reason for. `ConvertFloor.pushbutton` built `GroupRecord` (`:972-987`), `group_instance_count()` (`:989-1004`, walking `GroupType.Groups`) and `attached_detail_count()` (`:1007-1011`, calling `GetAvailableAttachedDetailGroupTypeIds`) purely to detect this shape and *avoid* touching it during a slab conversion — a repo-wide grep for `GroupType`/`Group.GetMemberIds`/member-exclusion turns up zero other hits anywhere in `anongee_toolkit` or any other pushbutton, so this API surface is called in exactly one place today, defensively, and has never once been pointed at the problem as a feature in its own right. Two of the 63 ideas already logged here brush past Groups without covering this: `ConvertFloor`'s own model-group handling is a conversion-time workaround, not an audit; and `Bulk Purge`'s **Future update** section names "unused Detail Groups and unused Model Groups" as a follow-on to its zero-usage-count purge pattern — but that is the opposite failure mode (a GroupType nobody placed at all), not a *placed, in-use* GroupType whose instances have silently drifted apart or whose sibling types are actually the same group repeated under different numbers. On a real project this is pure, invisible file bloat and design risk: two "identical" toilet-core groups that are actually one edited copy of the other means a plumbing fixture change made in one location never reaches the other, and nobody finds out until a clash or a site RFI. No comment anywhere in this repo's own idea log frames Group hygiene as something a paid add-in already owns — unlike the rebar and schedule domains, where this log repeatedly names Ideate/CTC/Naviate by product, Group drift is never mentioned in either direction, and Revit's own UI gives no usage or divergence signal for groups at all (the Project Browser lists GroupTypes by name only, with no member-diff view and no "instances of this type differ" flag).

**Description:**
`Group Drift Auditor.pushbutton` lands as a new sibling in `Essential.panel/Bulk Tool.stack`, next to the existing `Bulk Delete.pulldown` and `Bulk Rename.pulldown`, running the same collect → preview-checklist → confirm → single-transaction-group shape every batch tool in this stack already uses.

1. **Collect.** `FilteredElementCollector(doc).OfClass(DB.GroupType)` — for Model Groups and, separately, Detail Groups. For every `GroupType` with `group_instance_count(gtype) > 1` (the exact helper `ConvertFloor.pushbutton` already wrote and proved, promoted out of that script rather than forked a second time), walk each placed `Group.GetMemberIds()` and build a per-instance signature: member count, and a multiset of (Category, TypeId) pairs for every member.
2. **Diverged-instance detection.** Within one `GroupType`, compute the majority signature (the composition most instances actually share) and flag every instance whose signature differs — reporting exactly what differs: "Toilet Core (Instance at Grid C-4): missing 1× Sink Faucet, has 1 extra Wall (Basic Wall: Partition 100mm) not present on the other 5 instances." This is a read, not a write: Revit gives no safe API to force a diverged instance back into conformance with its type (that would mean editing the group definition in place, the exact operation the ConvertFloor comment says does not exist), so the tool's job stops at telling the modeler precisely which instance to open and fix, and how — not attempting an unsupported auto-repair.
3. **Split-type detection.** Across *different* `GroupType`s, compare member signatures the same way `ConvertFloor`'s own `cs_signature()` (`script.py:704-759`) compares compound structures for Type Consolidator's floor/foundation case — two GroupTypes whose member composition is identical (or differs only in ElementId, i.e. a pure geometric clone) are flagged as a likely ungroup/regroup fork, with each contributing instance count and the group name(s) involved shown side by side.
4. **Preview + consolidate.** Both report classes land as rows on the shared `CheckList` shell (`anongee_toolkit/ui/checklist.py:64`). Ticking a split-type cluster runs the safe single-instance path the ConvertFloor comment already describes as clean — ungroup every instance of the non-canonical type, regroup each onto the canonical `GroupType` by matching members positionally, delete the now-empty duplicate type — inside one `RevitTransaction` per cluster wrapped in one `RevitTransactionGroup` (`anongee_toolkit/revit/transactions.py:71,117`), so a single Ctrl+Z undoes the whole run. Diverged-instance rows are report-only and are never ticked for a write action.
5. **Report.** Instance-drift table (group name, instance location, exact member delta) for manual fix-up, plus a consolidation summary (types merged, instances re-pointed, types deleted) for anything actually applied.

**Implementation:**
- New `anongee_toolkit/structural/group_audit.py` (or a non-structural-specific `operations/group_audit.py`, since Model Groups are not a structural-only concept): `member_signature(group)` building the (Category, TypeId) multiset, `group_instance_count()` and `attached_detail_count()` promoted verbatim from `ConvertFloor.pushbutton/script.py:989-1011` rather than re-implemented, `find_diverged_instances(gtype)`, and `find_split_type_clusters(all_group_types)`.
- The actual ungroup → regroup → delete-duplicate sequence reuses `ConvertFloor.pushbutton`'s own already-proven single-instance round-trip (`script.py` model-group phase) rather than writing a second implementation of the same Revit API dance — this is the first real second caller of that logic, which today exists only inlined in the one script.
- `GroupType.Groups`, `Group.GetMemberIds()` and `GetAvailableAttachedDetailGroupTypeIds()` get their own thin wrapper module the way every other never-before-generalized API surface in this toolkit already does (`PartUtils` for Pour Break Manager, `DesignOption` for Design Option Scheme Comparator).
- Reused as-is: `CheckList` (`ui/checklist.py:64`), `RevitTransactionGroup`/`RevitTransaction` (`revit/transactions.py:71,117`), and the shared `ui.hostwnd`/`theme` dialog shell every other button in this stack already uses.
- Own `bundle.yaml`/`README.md`/icon in `Bulk Tool.stack`, following the same version-bump-and-`CHANGELOG.md` convention every other pushbutton here follows.

**Future update:**
- Once a diverged instance is flagged, add a "copy canonical composition onto this instance" repair mode for the one sub-case that *is* safe to automate: an instance missing a member the others share, where adding a matching new element (not editing the shared definition) closes the gap without touching Revit's unsupported "edit group in place" boundary.
- Feed split-type clusters into the already-logged `Type Consolidator`'s reporting shape, so a single end-of-project "duplication sweep" covers both Type-level and GroupType-level drift in one pass.
- Extend `member_signature()` to also compare each member's *instance* parameters (not just Category+Type), catching the subtler drift case where every instance has the same members but one was individually retyped or resized after placement.
- Wire the split-type consolidation path into `Bulk Purge`'s already-logged "unused Model/Detail Groups" future extension, so one end-to-end pass both merges the accidental duplicates this tool finds and then purges whatever zero-instance leftovers that merge produces.

## 2026-09-27 10:39 UTC

**Idea name:** BOQ & Cost Estimator — walks every structural element the toolkit already knows how to quantify (concrete volume, rebar weight, formwork/shutter area), multiplies each quantity against a firm-maintained rate card keyed off the element's own Structural Material and bar diameter, and rolls the result into one per-level, per-category Bill of Quantities workbook that recomputes on a re-run instead of a one-off export nobody keeps in sync with the model

**Potential:**
This toolkit already computes every raw quantity a BOQ needs, and none of them is ever costed. `anongee_toolkit/revit/geometry.py:10-37`'s `get_solid_volume_m3(element)` walks an element's `Solid`/`GeometryInstance` geometry and returns net volume in m³, failing safe to `0.0` rather than raising, and is re-exported as public API from both `anongee_toolkit/__init__.py:17,107,222` and `anongee_toolkit/revit/__init__.py:13,37` — yet the already-logged Design Option Scheme Comparator idea (2026-09-16) confirms by grep that it has **zero call sites in any `AnonGee.tab/**/script.py`**, so the one function in the repo that already answers "how much concrete is in this element" has never been wired to a report. `BBS Generator.pushbutton/core/bar_record.py:9-24`'s `BarRecord` carries `unit_weight_kg_per_m` and `total_weight_kg` per bar, grouped by `diameter_mm`, computed and exported to Excel today (`output/excel_writer.py`) but purely as a cutting/bending schedule — no rate is ever applied to that weight anywhere in the repo (`grep -rniE "cost|rate_table|unit_rate" AnonGee.extension --include=*.py`, excluding vendored `lib/py3`, returns zero hits outside this idea log and the already-logged Design Option Scheme Comparator's own **Future update** line, which only ever *names* per-m³ costing as a follow-on it never builds). The already-logged Formwork & Shuttering Takeoff idea (2026-09-13) would be the third input, giving per-face contact area classified into shutter-needed vs not — but its own write-up stops at an `openpyxl` quantity report, never a cost. Structural material grade is already sitting on every element ready to key a rate lookup: `BuiltInParameter.STRUCTURAL_MATERIAL_PARAM` is read today by `FramewinToBIM.pushbutton/script.py:103,1021,1031` purely to copy a material assignment across symbols during import, never to classify a quantity by grade for costing. Today, turning a model into a client-facing BOQ is a fully manual, off-toolkit spreadsheet exercise: export a concrete schedule, export a rebar schedule, export (or hand-calculate) a shuttering quantity, then paste all three into a rate sheet maintained separately from the model — redone from scratch on every revision because nothing ties the rate card back to whatever the model now measures. On the market this exact capability — model-quantity-to-cost in one pass — exists only inside separately licensed 5D/QTO platforms sold as their own seat on top of Revit (CostX, Assemble, Naviate's QTO module, Ideate's cost-adjacent Explorer add-ons), each requiring a model export or a parallel database; nothing free reads a live Revit structural model's own already-computed volume/weight/area numbers and prices them against a firm's own rate card in the same session.

**Description:**
`BOQ Estimator.pushbutton` lands as a new sibling in `Advance.panel`, next to `Parameter Combine.pushbutton` — it is a model-wide reporting pass over parameter and geometry data, not a structural-category-specific converter, so it belongs with the other cross-category batch tools rather than in `StrucBIM.stack`.

1. **Scope.** Pre-selection, active view, or whole model — the same three-way picker `ConvertFloor.pushbutton` already offers — filtered to `OST_StructuralColumns`, `OST_StructuralFraming`, `OST_StructuralFoundation` and structural floors/walls, the categories the rest of the toolkit already treats as structural.
2. **Quantity pass.** For every scoped element: concrete volume via `get_solid_volume_m3()` (first real caller, promoted from unused public API to production code); rebar weight via the same `BarRecord.total_weight_kg` aggregation `BBS Generator` already produces, grouped by `diameter_mm` per member; formwork area via the already-logged Formwork Takeoff engine once it exists, or a minimal same-shape stand-in (perimeter × height for columns/walls, width×2+soffit for beams) if it hasn't landed yet — the same "reuse if built, else a small standalone fallback" posture the already-logged Section Batch Generator idea already commits to for its own naming/sheet dependencies.
3. **Rate card.** A small, editable JSON/XLSX rate table — cost per m³ concrete keyed by `STRUCTURAL_MATERIAL_PARAM`'s grade name (M20/M25/M30…), cost per kg steel keyed by `diameter_mm` bracket, cost per m² formwork keyed by category — loaded the same way `slab_convert_config.json` already establishes for this toolkit's per-project override files, with a firm-default fallback shipped alongside the button.
4. **Preview.** One `CheckList` row (`anongee_toolkit/ui/checklist.py:64`) per level+category group: quantity, matched rate, extended cost, and a flagged "no rate found for this grade/diameter — priced at ₹0, review before export" warning rather than silently zeroing a line item. Nothing is exported until rows are reviewed.
5. **Export.** One Excel workbook via the same `openpyxl` styling module `BBS Generator.pushbutton/output/excel_writer.py` and `Export Schedule.pushbutton` already share — one sheet per discipline (Concrete, Reinforcement, Formwork), one rollup sheet totalling by level and grand total, formatted with the toolkit's own header/border styling so it reads as a firm deliverable, not a raw dump.
6. **Re-run / diff.** Reusing `BarRecord._compute_hash()` (`bar_record.py:47-58`)'s already-proven per-record revision-hash pattern, a second run after a design change recomputes only what actually changed and marks prior BOQ rows New/Changed/Deleted with the exact `C_NEW`/`C_CHANGED`/`C_DELETED` cell-fill convention `excel_writer.py` already defines and uses for its own schedule revisions — so a BOQ stays a living document across a project instead of a one-off export nobody re-touches.

**Implementation:**
- New `anongee_toolkit/costing/boq.py`: `quantity_pass(elements)` returning per-element volume/weight/area, `load_rate_card(path)`, `price(quantities, rate_card)` producing priced line items with an explicit `unpriced` flag rather than a silent zero.
- `get_solid_volume_m3` imported from `anongee_toolkit.revit.geometry` as-is; rebar weight aggregation reuses `BarRecord`'s own grouping rather than re-deriving bar mass from scratch.
- Rate-card file format and load/fallback behaviour mirror `slab_convert_config.json`'s existing override-by-config shape used throughout the repo.
- Excel output reuses `BBS Generator.pushbutton/output/excel_writer.py`'s header/border/fill constants and column auto-fit helper directly rather than a second styling implementation, and the New/Changed/Deleted revision-hash diffing reuses `BarRecord._compute_hash()`'s pattern applied to a priced-line-item record instead of a bar record.
- Same modeless `CheckList`/`hostwnd` shell every other batch tool in the repo already runs on; own `bundle.yaml`/`README.md`/icon in `Advance.panel`, following the same version-bump-and-`CHANGELOG.md` convention every other pushbutton here follows.

**Future update:**
- Once the already-logged Formwork & Shuttering Takeoff and Bar Cutting Optimizer ideas land for real, swap their stand-in quantity/waste numbers into this tool's inputs directly, so cutting-stock wastage is priced in rather than assumed at zero offcut.
- Feed the already-logged Design Option Scheme Comparator's own per-option volume/count delta through this same rate card, finally closing that idea's own **Future update** line ("which scheme is smaller" into "which scheme is cheaper") instead of leaving it as an unbuilt bullet.
- Per-level cost trend across a project's revision history (one line per re-run date), so a client-facing cost report shows the trajectory of a scheme's cost through design development, not just its current total.
- Extend the rate card to key off `S_ScheduleMark`/procurement stage once `todo-list.md:1019`'s FEAT-45 "Phase or Procurement" schedule column ships, so a staged BOQ can report what each construction stage costs independently, the same stage-cost split that gap was opened to give a client in the first place.
